**Evidence tags.** `[V]` means read or run during this design, with the citation or the command given. `[I]` means inferred; the task named alongside it supplies the proof. File references are relative to the repository root, at commit dfa596c (this branch changes only documents). Pinned CLI evidence names the package and the command. Claude's help output is the fixture `tests/fixtures/provider_cli/claude-2.1.325-help.txt`; Gemini bundle references are to files in the `bundle/` directory of `@google/gemini-cli@0.68.0`. Every CLI ran with an empty scratch HOME and no credentials, and no model was called. The Codex request captures of revision 4 point a `model_providers` entry at a local HTTP stub on `127.0.0.1` with a dummy key; the stub records the request and answers 400, so nothing leaves the host. Revision 5 adds three such stubs, each run inside `unshare -rn` (a user and network namespace whose only interface is loopback), with dummy keys: a Gemini gateway stub (`GOOGLE_GEMINI_BASE_URL`) that records the first request and answers 400; a Responses-API stub for Codex that answers with scripted, forced tool calls and records the follow-up request; and an Anthropic Messages stub (`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`) that answers one text reply. Claude runs from the pinned platform binary `@anthropic-ai/claude-code-linux-x64@2.1.325`. Revision 6 reruns the Gemini captures in new directories, adds a Gemini stub that answers the first request with a forced `read_file` call and records the follow-up request (JIT discovery), and runs two spawns of one agent concurrently, each in its own home, for Codex and for Gemini, against one stub. Its code claims come from reading the files and from a static AST probe of `koda/control_plane/manager.py` that follows `self.<method>` calls to `decrypt_secret`, `encrypt_secret` and `get_signing_key`. The Landlock probes used a helper that applies `no_new_privs` and a Landlock ruleset (ABI 7) whose only rule allows every filesystem right on `/`, then execs the probe. Revision 7 runs Codex and Claude at `workspace_exec` against the Responses and Anthropic stubs inside `unshare -rn`, with a forced command that leaves a `setsid` background process and runs a long foreground one, each started through a prototype of the spawn lifetime of §2.3.5 (a Python stand-in for `koda-sandbox-exec`'s process model, with that Landlock ruleset applied in the CLI's process) in each of its three modes, and kills the worker or the helper mid-run; it measures Claude's project directory naming and resume; and it replaces revision 6's self-call probe with the whole-package call graph that §5.1.4 (c) specifies. Revision 8 runs a probe inside an identity-mapped user and PID namespace, under a Landlock ruleset that grants `/proc` read-only, once with the host's `/proc` and once with a fresh procfs that init mounts in a new mount namespace; runs the pinned Gemini at `workspace_exec` against revision 6's forced-call stub inside `unshare -rpfn`, with and without `--mount-proc`, with a forced shell command that leaves a `setsid` process and stays silent past a 3 s inactivity timeout; and kills a spawn cgroup nested in a populated workspace cgroup on this host's cgroup2 hierarchy. Its code claims about the kernel's task and worker lifecycle come from reading `rust/koda-runtime-kernel/src/server.rs`, `agent_workers.rs`, `processes.rs` and `commands.rs`. Revision 9 runs four contained probes, the first three inside `unshare -pf --mount-proc` with Python stand-ins for the kernel, the worker and the helper: a spawn cgroup on this host's cgroup2 hierarchy under a kernel stand-in that kills every orphan it adopts (AG-15b), once with a helper that is not a subreaper and once with one that is; a command's `kill(-1, SIGKILL)` with the kernel stand-in as an ordinary process (native) and as PID 1 (compose); Koda's SIGKILL of a stopped subreaper helper, directly and after killing the helper's children; and the host's git 2.43 running the kernel's own git commands in a repository with a planted `core.fsmonitor` and `post-checkout` hook, with and without the §2.9.1 hardening. Its code claims about operator commands and the kernel's spawn sites and environments come from reading `koda/handlers/commands.py`, `koda/handlers/packages.py`, `koda/services/scheduled_job_runtime.py`, `koda/services/runtime/api.py` and `controller.py`, and `rust/koda-runtime-kernel/src/workspace.rs`, `checkpoints.rs`, `state.rs` and `server.rs`. Revision 10 runs two contained git 2.43.0 probes: auto-gc's detach (with `gc.autoDetach` at its default, `git gc --auto` returns at once while a detached gc runs on; with `-c gc.autoDetach=false` it finishes inside the command), and the worktree admin layout for a base that is a repository subdirectory (`git -C <base> worktree add` succeeds, `<base>/.git` does not exist, and `git -C <base> rev-parse --git-common-dir` names the base repository's `.git`) and for a base that is a linked worktree (`<base>/.git` is a file into the common directory). Its other claims come from reading `koda/services/tool_dispatcher.py`, `koda/services/scheduled_jobs.py`, `koda/services/cron_store.py`, `koda/utils/workdir.py`, `koda/config.py`, `koda/agent_contract.py`, `rust/koda-runtime-kernel/src/commands.rs`, `isolation.rs`, `proto/runtime/v1/runtime.proto`, `koda/knowledge/v2/postgres_backend.py` and `koda/services/sandbox_doctor.py`. Revision 11 runs three contained probes with the host's git 2.43.0 in a scratch HOME, with `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null` (a `git add -A` stopped by SIGTERM and by SIGKILL; repeated commits in a repository with young unreachable loose objects; `maintenance.autoDetach` on 2.43), reruns the classifier probe with `uv run python` and a scratch HOME, and runs `sort --compress-program=./p` in a scratch directory; its other claims come from reading `koda/services/tool_dispatcher.py`, `scheduled_jobs.py`, `scheduled_job_runtime.py`, `cron_store.py` and `runtime/api.py`, `koda/internal_rpc/runtime_kernel.py`, `koda/knowledge/v2/postgres_backend.py`, `koda/config.py`, `docker-compose.yml`, `proto/runtime/v1/runtime.proto`, and `rust/koda-runtime-kernel/src/server.rs`, `workspace.rs`, `checkpoints.rs` and `isolation.rs`. Revision 12 runs two contained probes in a scratch HOME: `validate_work_dir` from the worktree's environment with `python -I` and `AGENT_ID=AGENT_A`, on directories created there; and a subreaper stand-in, in a scratch directory with the host's git 2.43.0, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, that stops `sh -c 'git add -A; true'` on a 300 MB tree once with SIGTERM to its own child and once with SIGTERM to every descendant. Its other claims come from reading `koda/config.py`, `koda/utils/workdir.py`, `koda/services/tool_dispatcher.py`, `koda/services/scheduled_jobs.py`, `koda/services/runtime/api.py` and `controller.py`, `koda/services/queue_manager.py`, `koda/handlers/commands.py`, `koda/control_plane/settings.py`, `supervisor.py` and `manager.py`, `docker-compose.yml`, `docs/install/local.md` and `rust/koda-runtime-kernel/src/commands.rs`.

## Revision 12 log

Revision 12 is scoped to the round-11 findings, as revision 11 was to round 10's: each change is local, no section is restructured, and no mechanism is added beyond what a finding needs. Round-11 findings are numbered in review order. `S11-n` for SEC: S11-1 the major (the worker-side workspace grant has no cross-agent rule) and S11-2 the minor (OQ-11's user impact). `R11-n` for REV: R11-1 the major (the predicate passes directories the threat model protects), R11-2 to R11-4 the three minors (the `CreateEnvironment` row against §7.9's keying, the §6 table's OQ-9 combinations, the spawn-end SIGTERM's targets) and R11-5 the nit (invariant 3's R-11 entry). `S11-0` and `R11-0` are done-when item 5. What revision 11 settled stays settled: model-written shell jobs are OQ-11, there is no install id (R-13), and S9-1, S9-3, R9-1 and R9-6 stay closed (revision 11 rows 2 to 5); rows 3 and 4 below correct only the text the findings name. Implementation decisions from these findings are listed in §7.10 with their owning item, acceptance criterion and test.

| # | Change | Answers |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The work-dir predicate knows the agent, and where Koda owns the parent it allows instead of denies** (§2.4.2 L2, L3, L4, AG-12, AG-14, AG-15, §5.4, §2.8.2, the notice). Revision 11's predicate passed another agent's `<AGENT_WORKSPACE_ROOT>/<agent>` in both layouts, every Koda directory under `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT` outside `provider-homes` in compose (`snapshots/`, the control plane's `CONTROL_PLANE_RUNTIME_DIR`, `control_plane/settings.py:56-59`, with its login CLIs' cwd, `manager.py:2841-2843`; the image and artifact scratch, `config.py:1019-1036`; every agent's runtime task directory, `controller.py:111-113, 265-270`), all on the `koda-runtime` volume that every agent's worker shares with one `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT` (`docker-compose.yml:57, 250`; `supervisor.py:91, 512-515`), and on native installs another agent's `~/.koda-runtime/<agent>`; the cross-agent rule sat only in L4's Otherwise branch, so the worker side, which checks the predicate alone (AG-14) and is the default path (`RUNTIME_ENVIRONMENTS_ENABLED` defaults to false, `config.py:1392`), granted them to a work dir the model chose (`tool_dispatcher.py:1428-1437`). The predicate now takes the token's agent and the task's recorded environment: under `RUNTIME_KERNEL_ROOT` only that environment passes, at or under `AGENT_WORKSPACE_ROOT` only the agent's own workspace, and anything else under `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT` or, on native installs, `~/.koda-runtime` is refused; the Koda directories that default under `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT` are "under" roots wherever they are set. L4's Otherwise branch now cites the predicate, so the worker side refuses what it refuses. AG-12's matrix and the Rust parity test gain the must-refuse rows, AG-14 an integration case, and §5.4 a model-chosen work dir. This removes nothing users have today: those parents hold only Koda's own files, `~/.koda-runtime` is new, and in compose they lie under `/var/lib`, which `validate_work_dir` refuses today (`config.py:775`; `[V]`, §1.2). | S11-1, R11-1 |
| 2 | **Work dirs the model chooses, and the install directory, are operator question OQ-12; residual R-14** (§9.1, §2.5.4, §6, AG-12, invariant 3, the notice). Outside Koda's roots the predicate passes any directory under HOME, and the model chooses the path itself: `validate_work_dir` accepts `~/.ssh`, `~/.aws`, `~/.config/systemd/user`, `~/.local/bin` and the Koda checkout (`[V]`, §1.2; `utils/workdir.py`, which refuses only `SENSITIVE_DIRS`, `config.py:766-780`), and `config.py:15` loads the checkout's `.env` into the worker. (a) Limiting a model-chosen work dir to the agent's workspace, `PROJECT_DIRS`, the control-plane workspace root and the operator's `/setdir`, and (b) refusing the install directory (`SCRIPT_DIR`, `config.py:14`) as any work dir, both remove what agents or operators can do today, so they are OQ-12 with a recommendation and its user impact, not design decisions; R-14 names what a decline leaves. Revision 11 row 1's "no operator question is needed" held only for the parts it changed. | R11-1 |
| 3 | **`CreateEnvironment` is keyed by agent and task, as §7.9 says** (§2.4.2 L4 table, AG-15). The table row and AG-15's Accept refused a task id another agent held, while §7.9 keys records by the token's agent and the task id; task ids are one global `BIGSERIAL` (`postgres_backend.py:933-940`), so first-come refusal was a denial of service. Both now read: the record is keyed by the token's agent and the task id, a body `agent_id` is ignored, and lookups resolve within the token's agent. | R11-2 |
| 4 | **§6 states what the two unsettled OQ-9 combinations do** (§6, OQ-9, R-11a, L4, A-O-01 (c), MASTER_PLAN M1-E2-T2). OQ-9 (c) acts through the `--lifeline` helper that (b) adds, so with (b) declined (c) has no effect, and A-O-01 (c) leaves with (b). With (a) declined the kernel's own git runs as today, without the flags or the config check (inside the helper from AG-15), and R-11a and L4 now say "hardened with OQ-9 (a)" and "config-checked by AG-03 with OQ-9 (a)". | R11-3 |
| 5 | **Each lifetime mode says whom the spawn-end SIGTERM reaches** (§2.3.5, AG-08, §7.9). `pid_namespace`: init signals every process in the namespace; `cgroup`: every pid in the spawn's `cgroup.procs`; `subreaper`: every descendant found by walking `/proc/<pid>/task/*/children`. A SIGTERM to the helper's own child alone reaches `sh`, not the git it runs: `sh` exits, git is reparented and keeps writing, and the kill 2 s later leaves `.git/index.lock`; signalling every descendant lets git remove it (`[V]`, §1.2). The test adds `sh -c 'git add -A; true'` in each mode, which makes revision 11 row 6's "covers governed git at a worker exit too" hold. | R11-4 |
| 6 | **OQ-11's user impact is exact** (OQ-11, §6, §7.9). An unchanged dashboard save writes nothing (`update_job` returns "No changes detected", `scheduled_jobs.py:1559-1566`), so it reclassifies nothing; only an edit of the command or work dir, or re-creating the job with `/cron`, makes a job operator-authored, and §7.9's criterion and test say so. A pre-upgrade model-created job keeps R-11's exposure until AG-15 with OQ-9 (b) approved, and for good if (b) is declined. | S11-2 |
| 7 | **Nit.** Invariant 3's R-11 entry names the model-written jobs of a declined OQ-11 (a) and the permanent case of a declined OQ-9 (b), and it names R-14. | R11-5 |
| 8 | Done-when item 5 can be met only by the round-12 SEC and REV reviews, recorded in VALIDATION_REPORT.md. | S11-0, R11-0 |

## Revision 11 log

Revision 11 is scoped to the round-10 findings: each change is local, no section is restructured, and no mechanism is added beyond what a finding needs. Round-10 findings are numbered in review order. `S10-n` for SEC: S10-1 to S10-6 the six minors (the model's `job_update`, the work-dir predicate, the RPC list, the install id, AG-15b's behaviours, the kernel's git at a worker restart) and S10-7 the nit list. `R10-n` for REV: R10-1 the major (the work-dir predicate, the same defect as S10-2), R10-2 to R10-7 the six minors (the model-versus-operator split, AG-15b and the gate, the install id, the RPC list, the copy fallback, auto-gc's cost) and R10-8 to R10-10 the three nits (invariant 3 and R-11a, the §3 row and the spawn function's owner, the evidence slips). The round-9 items that both reviews still listed as open are S9-1, S9-3, R9-1 and R9-6; the row that names each closes it. `S10-0` and `R10-0` are done-when item 5. Decisions that belong to implementation are listed in §7.9 with their owning item, acceptance criterion and test.

| # | Change | Answers |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The work-dir predicate refuses "under" only the Koda-owned roots** (§2.4.2 L2, L3, L4, AG-12, AG-15, §2.8.2 to §2.8.4). Revision 10 applied "or under" to every root, so it refused the compose default workspace and kernel root, which lie under `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT` (`/var/lib/koda/runtime`, `docker-compose.yml:57`), every worktree and copy environment (`<RUNTIME_KERNEL_ROOT>/worktrees/…`, `workspace.rs:109-111, 143-153`) and every native work dir under HOME (`DEFAULT_WORK_DIR`, `config.py:201`; `/setdir`, `commands.py:369-391`), and AG-12's Accept contradicted itself. Now "is, contains or lies under" applies to the state roots, the provider and login homes, the artifact and `tmp_images` roots and `<RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT>/provider-homes` (every spawn home, spawn record and Claude session store, §2.3.5), and "is or contains" to HOME, the shared temp directories, `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT` and `RUNTIME_KERNEL_ROOT`; under `RUNTIME_KERNEL_ROOT`, L4 accepts only the task's kernel-recorded environment. L4's Otherwise branch stops listing `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT`, which since revision 9 refused governed shell in the compose workspace. The native `AGENT_WORKSPACE_ROOT` default is `~/koda-workspaces`; the notice states the default work-dir move and the refused work dirs; the predicate matrix and the Rust floor test gain must-pass rows. No work dir under HOME is refused, so no operator question is needed. **Extended by revision 12 rows 1 and 2:** the predicate takes the agent and allows only the agent's own directories where Koda owns the parent; limiting the work dirs the model chooses under HOME, and refusing the install directory, are OQ-12. | R10-1, S10-2 |
| 2 | **AG-15b has one behaviour, and the release gate is met under every answer** (AG-15b, §6, OQ-8, OQ-9, R-10, §2.3.5, §5.4, O-11). AG-15b is enabled only once every kernel spawn path runs under a `--lifeline` helper, which needs OQ-9 (b) and (c) approved: while either is unanswered its scan is off, and if either is declined it is dropped and R-10 case (1) stays open in compose, as under OQ-8 (b). OQ-8's and OQ-9's "still ends … within a second" sentences are gone, AG-15b's test reads "not enabled while OQ-9 (b) or (c) is unanswered or declined", and §6 states what each answer does to the gate. Found while answering: §6 required AG-32, which by its own Deps ships one release later, and AG-31 depended on "every other AG item", AG-32 included; both now leave it out. | S10-5, R10-3; R9-1 (closed) |
| 3 | **Scheduled shell jobs the model wrote are operator question OQ-11** (§9.1, §2.9.1, S9, §2.4.1, R-11, §3, §4, the notice, AG-14, AG-15, A-O-01 (d), §9.3 item 1). Revision 10 decided that a model-created job runs governed, which changes what users' agents can do today, so it is now OQ-11, with a recommendation and its user impact. (a) A job whose command or work dir the model last wrote, by `job_create` or by a `job_update` of any of the user's jobs (`tool_dispatcher.py:1073-1101`; `scheduled_jobs.py:1473, 1506-1507`, which reactivates an active job, `:1583-1586`), is model-authored and runs as a governed class M call, at fire time on `policy_allow` only. (b) Existing jobs, for which no creator is recorded (`scheduled_jobs.py:917-921`; `postgres_backend.py:1626-1652`), are classified at upgrade from the scheduler's `agent_tool` edit events. The caller records the author in a new column (§7.9). R-11 now says that cron and the model's `job_run_now` fire an operator-authored job with no operator step, and AG-14 owns the refusal in the AG-14 to AG-15 window. | S10-1, R10-2; S9-1 (closed) |
| 4 | **Every kernel RPC has a stated binding** (§2.4.2 L4, §5.4, AG-15, §9.3 item 14). L4's list becomes a table of all 27 RPCs of `RuntimeKernelService` (`runtime.proto:551-583`): bound to the task's agent, to the session's or checkpoint's own task, or to the browser session's task; supervisor-only; or none (`Health`). `GetCheckpoint` and `RestoreCheckpoint` require the checkpoint's own task to be the body's, because both look the id up in one global map (`server.rs:3012-3069`), and `StartBrowserSession` derives `runtime_dir` from the task (it takes the body's today, `server.rs:2402-2411`). The auth matrix iterates the proto, and §5.4 cites it. §9.3 item 14 carries the token-holder change to ADR-035 and M1-E6-T1. Environments keyed by a global task id are an obligation (§7.9). | S10-3, R10-5; S9-3 (closed) |
| 5 | **No per-install claim; two installs sharing roots on one host is residual R-13** (§2.3.5, §2.5.4, AG-08, AG-15, A-L-02, the notice). `<bid>` is the agent id (`config.py:18-19`), and the kernel has no install id (`isolation.rs:136-146`; `docker-compose.yml:244-247`), so revision 10's `<KODA_CGROUP_ROOT>/<bid>` subtree is withdrawn and cgroups stay at `<KODA_CGROUP_ROOT>/ws_*`. A real install id is not introduced: two native installs under one OS user already share the state root and the kernel root by default (`config.py:205-206`; `server.rs:85-87`), so the only per-install anchor is the roots the operator sets. R-13 names what two installs sharing them do to each other, and A-L-02's case uses two installs with one agent id. | S10-4, R10-4; R9-6 (closed) |
| 6 | **The kernel's own git, tar and copy belong to the kernel, not to the incarnation** (§2.3.5, AG-15, A-L-02). A worker exit no longer closes their lifeline: they end when they finish, at their own timeout or when the kernel exits, so a worker restart during `SaveCheckpoint`, `RestoreCheckpoint` or `CreateEnvironment` never SIGKILLs git mid-write (`[V]`, §1.2: a SIGKILLed `git add -A` leaves `.git/index.lock`, and the next `git reset --hard` fails; a SIGTERMed one removes it). At every spawn end the helper sends SIGTERM and waits up to 2 s before the mode's kill, which covers governed git at a worker exit too (once revision 12 row 5 says how each mode finds the spawn's processes). A test proves that the kernel's checkpoint git and tar run inside the helper's domain. The three are obligations (§7.9). | S10-6 |
| 7 | **The `copy` environment's copy runs through the helper too** (§2.4.2 L4, R-11a, AG-15). `copy_workspace_recursive` takes the type from readdir and then copies by path (`workspace.rs:68-97`), so a link swapped in between is followed, and a FIFO blocks a kernel thread; the copy now runs under the task profile, follows no link and skips FIFOs and devices (§7.9). | R10-6 |
| 8 | **Auto-gc's cost is stated and bounded; the class M git keys are listed** (§2.9.1, AG-03, D11-13). With `gc.autoDetach=false` git writes no `gc.log`, so in a repository whose unreachable loose objects are too young to prune every commit reruns the foreground gc (`[V]`, §1.2); the commit is recorded first, the gc is bounded by the command's timeout, and D11-13 adds the case. `maintenance.autoDetach=false` is passed on every git version (2.43 accepts it, `[V]`). The model's own git inside a class M spawn gets only the two autoDetach keys through `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`, not `core.hooksPath` or `core.fsmonitor`: the model already runs its own code there, and the repository's hooks keep running for its commits as today, so the notice needs no hook sentence. | R10-7, S10-7 (git keys) |
| 9 | **Nits.** Invariant 3 names its residuals; R-11a names the AG-03 to AG-15 window and uses the strongest profile the host enforces, in-process only without Landlock; §2.9.1 and the class M profile grant the common directory and, for a restore, only that checkpoint's patch and bundle, read-only (the kernel writes the checkpoint files itself, `server.rs:2931-2950`); the §3 row on registering after the fork describes the one spawn function, which AG-15 owns and AG-15b extends; revision 10 row 5's Answers column reads S9-5; §1.2 gains the revision 10 and 11 probe rows, and the evidence paragraph's gc clause is rewritten; the shared uid is C11; health shows R-12 only below ABI 6 outside `pid_namespace`. | S10-7, R10-8, R10-9, R10-10 |
| 10 | Done-when item 5 can be met only by the round-11 SEC and REV reviews, recorded in VALIDATION_REPORT.md. | S10-0, R10-0 |

## Revision 10 log

Each row names what changed and the round-9 finding it answers. Both round-9 reviews approved with changes and raised no blocking finding. Round-9 findings are numbered in review order: `S9-n` for SEC (S9-1 to S9-5 the five minors: the model-created shell job, the kernel's own git, the RPC agent binding, the operator terminal's lifetime, the work-dir predicate; S9-6 the `kill -9 -1` nit), `R9-n` for REV (R9-1 to R9-6 the six minors: AG-15b versus an open OQ-9, git auto-gc detach, the pending-entry double registration, the worktree git directory, `kill -9 -1` across agents, the per-install cgroup root; R9-7 and R9-8 the two nits: the release gate's OQ-10 branch, the A-O-01 split). `S9-0` and `R9-0` are done-when item 5, pointed at the round-10 reviews.

| # | Change | Answers |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **A shell job the model created is a governed agent call** (§2.9.1, §2.4.1, S9). A `shell_command` job made through the model's `job_create` tool with no `migration_source` chooses its own command and work dir (`tool_dispatcher.py:1116`) and activates with no operator step (`auto_activate_after_validation` defaults `True`, `:1124-1127`; `scheduled_jobs.py:1282`), and the read-only classifier does not confine it: a command that calls a workspace-relative helper is classified `read` and runs that helper (`[V]`, §1.2). So the dispatcher runs it as governed class M, not on the `scheduled_job` operator basis: S9 refuses it from AG-14 wherever the mode's layers are missing (a kernel spawn counts as missing them until AG-15), and from AG-15 it runs confined under the agent's class M profile. An **operator-authored** job (via `/cron`, or a migrated one, `migration_source` set) stays an operator command (R-11, OQ-9). New A-O-01 (d). **Superseded by revision 11 row 3:** the governed handling of a job the model wrote, `job_update` included, is operator question OQ-11. | S9-1 |
| 2 | **The kernel's own git and tar run through the helper, or are a named residual** (§2.4.1, §2.4.2 L4, invariant 3, R-11a, AG-15, AG-03). The kernel's worktree creation and removal and its checkpoint `diff`, `ls-files`, `reset`, `apply` and `tar` run in the kernel process (`workspace.rs:130-138, 182-189`; `checkpoints.rs:23-73, 97-104, 145-150, 238-242`), which revision 9 added to class M but only hardened. AG-15 now runs each of these through `koda-sandbox-exec` under the task's class M profile (the workspace, the repository's common directory for a worktree, row 10, and, as revision 11 narrows it, one checkpoint's files read-only); where that profile cannot be enforced, the hardened in-process path is the named residual **R-11a**, with what the config check does not cover. New AG-15 Accept case and AG-03 Rust test. | S9-2 |
| 3 | **Every task-, environment-, terminal- and checkpoint-scoped RPC binds to the token's agent** (§2.4.2 L4, §5.4). Revision 9 bound only `CreateEnvironment`, `StartTask`, `ExecuteCommand` and `OpenTerminal`; the other RPCs took ids or paths from the body (`runtime.proto:555-581`; `server.rs:3059-3061` `RestoreCheckpoint` uses `payload.workspace_path`; `:1568-1576` `WriteTerminal` by `session_id`; `:945, 1002-1007` `CreateEnvironment` inserts `env-<body task id>` over any record; `postgres_backend.py:933-940` one global `BIGSERIAL` task id). L4 now resolves the task's or session's agent from kernel state and compares it on every such RPC, never takes a `workspace_path` from the body, refuses `CreateEnvironment` for a task id another agent holds, and restricts `EnsureAgentWorkers` and `TerminateAgentWorker` to the supervisor's token. Workers hold only the per-worker kernel token from AG-15, not the M1-E6-T1 shared token. Mitigating: in-process worker code already shares the uid, so this is defense in depth (C11). New Rust auth-matrix test. **Extended by revision 11 row 4:** every RPC has a row in L4's binding table. **Corrected by revision 12 row 3:** `CreateEnvironment` keys its record by the token's agent and the task id instead of refusing a task id another agent holds. | S9-3 |
| 4 | **An open operator terminal closes when the agent's worker restarts** (OQ-9 (c), notice, A-O-01). Revision 9 binds `OpenTerminal` to the requesting worker's incarnation (§2.3.5, 738), so the end of that incarnation closes the terminal; today the terminal is a kernel process that survives a worker restart (`server.rs:1412-1470`). OQ-9 (c) and the notice now say this, so the operator can weigh it under §13. New A-O-01 (c) case. | S9-4 |
| 5 | **The kernel floor denies work dirs under the state roots and login homes** (§2.4.2 L4, L2, AG-12). AG-12's predicate refused only a work dir that is or contains a state root or a login home (798, 1410), so a work dir **inside** `WORKER_STATE_ROOT`, `STATE_ROOT_DIR`, a login home, the artifact root or the `tmp_images` root passed and became the read-write grant (the model picks the path, `tool_dispatcher.py:1116, 1413`). AG-12's predicate now denies anything **under** them too, and L4's "lies under none of" list names them; the Rust predicate is tested for parity with the Python one. New predicate-matrix and floor cases. **Narrowed by revision 11 row 1:** "under" applies only to the Koda-owned roots, so the default workspaces, the environments and native work dirs under HOME pass. | S9-5 |
| 6 | **`kill(-1, SIGKILL)` reaches every same-uid agent, as residual R-12** (R-10 cases (1) and (2), §5.4, notice). Below ABI 6 a confined command's `kill(-1, SIGKILL)` ends every same-uid process but PID 1 and the caller (`[V]`, §1.2, 288), so every other agent's worker, helpers and CLIs, and on native the control plane and the operator's session; each such restart counts one recovery for the tasks those workers ran (`queue_manager.py:8955-8975`, limit 3), so repeated kills push other agents' in-flight tasks to the DLQ. R-10 cases (1) and (2), the §5.4 check (two agents) and the notice now say so. | S9-6, R9-5 |
| 7 | **AG-15b's Deps name the lifeline form on every kernel spawn path** (AG-15b, OQ-9, notice, §2.9.1). AG-15b SIGKILLs every unrecorded orphan reparented to the kernel, on the premise that such an orphan is what a killed helper left; that premise fails for operator commands, scheduled jobs and the terminal while OQ-9 is pending, when (b) is declined (no helper) or when (c) is declined (exec form, no lifeline), where `ExecuteCommand` spawns `sh -lc` directly (`commands.rs:126-146`) and the terminal forks directly (`server.rs:700`). AG-15b's Deps now name AG-15's operator-command confinement with OQ-9 (b) and (c) approved; OQ-9's row and the notice say that where AG-15b runs, an open OQ-9 or a declined (b)/(c) still ends these commands' background processes within a second, so (c) cannot be declined on its own in compose, and OQ-8 and OQ-9 are decided together. New AG-15b test. **Superseded by revision 11 row 2:** AG-15b is off while OQ-9 (b) or (c) is unanswered and dropped if either is declined, so it never ends those commands' processes. | R9-1 |
| 8 | **Git auto-gc finishes inside the command** (§2.9.1 flags, AG-03). Git's auto-gc daemonizes by default and holds `.git/gc.log.lock`; the lifetime rule and AG-15b kill that detached gc, and a stale `gc.log.lock` then silently stops every later auto-gc (`[V]`, §1.2: with `-c gc.autoDetach=false` the commit packs inline and leaves no lock). §2.9.1's flags and the Rust builder add `-c gc.autoDetach=false` (and `-c maintenance.autoDetach=false` for pins of git 2.47+); class M spawns set the same keys through `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`/`GIT_CONFIG_KEY_n`/`GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_n` in AG-03's env builder. New D11-13 / A-L-01 case. **Corrected by revision 11 row 8:** `maintenance.autoDetach=false` on every version, and only the two autoDetach keys through `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`. | R9-2 |
| 9 | **One spawn function for AG-15 and AG-15b; the pending-entry scheme is gone** (§2.3.5, A-L-02, AG-15b). The incarnation lock covers check, fork and pid-plus-lifeline record, so every kernel spawn is either fully registered or refused and closure always knows of a spawn in flight; the "closed in between" branch and the second fault-injection case go away (the lock order is incarnation then scan, so AG-15b stays deadlock-free). | R9-3 |
| 10 | **A worktree floor grants the real git common directory** (§2.4.2 L4, AG-15). For a `worktree` environment, `<base>/.git` does not exist when the base is a repository subdirectory and is a file when the base is a linked worktree (`[V]`, §1.2); the worktree's admin files and the objects and refs live in another repository's common directory (`workspace.rs:25-39, 108-138`). The kernel now records `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` (resolved, config-checked by AG-03) and the new worktree's own admin directory, and grants them only when they lie under the base or the token agent's workspace root and pass AG-12's predicate; otherwise it creates the environment as `copy` or refuses it. New floor and L2 cases. | R9-4 |
| 11 | **Each kernel's cgroups sit under a per-install subtree** (§2.3.5 cgroup mode, AG-15 lifetime, L0). `KODA_CGROUP_ROOT` defaults to `/sys/fs/cgroup/koda` with no install id (`isolation.rs:136-146`; `sandbox_doctor.py:261`), so two kernels on one native host share the root and a starting kernel could kill the other's live spawns. The cgroups move under `<KODA_CGROUP_ROOT>/<bid>/ws_*`, namespaced per install like the runtime roots. New Rust test with two instance ids under one parent. **Withdrawn by revision 11 row 5:** `<bid>` is the agent id, not an install id, so there is no per-install subtree; R-13. | R9-6 |
| 12 | **§6 names OQ-10's declined branch**; §6 reads "… or, for OQ-10, adds the registration path to it". | R9-7 |
| 13 | **A-O-01 is split into (a) to (d)** with their owning items (OQ-9 (a) `/git` and the kernel's git, AG-03; (b) the class M profile, AG-15; (c) the terminal and `nohup` end, OQ-8 with OQ-9 (c); (d) a model-created shell job, revision 10 row 1); MASTER_PLAN M1-E2-T2 and the M1-E2-T4 done-when name the parts whose OQ-9 answer is approved. | R9-8 |
| 14 | Done-when item 5 can be met only by the round-10 SEC and REV reviews, recorded in VALIDATION_REPORT.md. | S9-0, R9-0 |

## Revision 8 log

Each row names what changed and the round-7 finding it answers. Round-7 findings are numbered in review order: `S7-n` for SEC (S7-1 the kernel-spawn major, S7-2 the cgroup-placement minor), `R7-n` for REV (R7-1 to R7-4 the four minors in review order: CLIs the kernel starts, the §5.1.4 (c) exemption, the PID namespace's `/proc`, OQ-8's consequences; R7-5 to R7-10 the six nits: the cgroup placement, R-3's per-agent secret, the bootstrap owner, M1-E2-T3's Deps, the AG-22 split, consume). `S7-0` and `R7-0` are done-when item 5, with each review's cross-reference to the other's. The carried-over blocker (every descendant ends with its spawn) is answered by revision 7 for worker-side spawns and by row 1 for the spawns the kernel starts.

| # | Change | Answers |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Processes the kernel starts end with the worker that asked for them.** Governed shell always runs as a kernel `ExecuteCommand` (`shell_runner.py:38-47`; revision 7's C17 placed it in the worker), and with runtime environments on (`RUNTIME_ENVIRONMENTS_ENABLED`, an operator system setting, off by default) every provider CLI is a kernel `StartTask` (`queue_manager.py:3867, 4094` → `kernel_subprocess.py:152-160`). `StartTask` returns while the process runs, and the kernel's worker termination and reaper touch no task (C17). From AG-15 the kernel draws each worker's incarnation and mints its kernel token when it spawns it, records every `StartTask` and `ExecuteCommand` under that incarnation, and holds the lifeline of each helper it starts. It closes those lifelines when the task ends, when the worker exits, and in `terminate_internal`, which waits for the helpers before the next worker is spawned (`agent_workers.rs:266-281, 455-519, 542-559`); a revoked token starts nothing. The worker writes a spawn record for a CLI it asks the kernel to start, so the sweep's backstop covers it too, and a home is removed only after its helper has ended. AG-15's Accept reads "ends with its task, with the requesting worker, or with the kernel"; new A-L-02; the §5.3 restart check runs once with runtime environments on; C17, §2.2.3, §2.3.5, §2.4.2, §4 and S9 (the kernel path counts as unconfined until AG-15) are corrected, and A-L-01 covers the worker-side kinds. SEC's fallback (refuse runtime environments at class M levels) is not needed (§3). | S7-1, R7-1 |
| 2 | **The spawn cgroup nests in the workspace cgroup.** The helper creates `spawn_<spawn id>/` as a child of the cgroup it starts in, the agent's `ws_<workspace>` (`isolation.rs:142-146`), with no controller enabled below it, so the workspace's `memory.max`, `cpu.max`, `pids.max` and `cpuset.cpus` (`isolation.rs:148-171`) bound every spawn; the kernel starts its own helpers in that cgroup. The worker and the sweep act only on a recorded path equal to `<KODA_CGROUP_ROOT>/ws_<workspace>/spawn_<spawn id>` for that record. `[V]` (§1.2): a spawn cgroup under a populated workspace cgroup is killed as a unit, the workspace's own process keeps running, and no canary is written. New §5.1.9 case: a fork past the workspace's `pids.max` fails. | S7-2, R7-5 |
| 3 | **The PID namespace gets its own `/proc`.** Init unshares a mount namespace, makes `/` recursively private and mounts a fresh procfs before the command applies Landlock, so the `/proc` grant is that procfs; the self-test selects the mode only if the mount works. `[V]` (§1.2): with the host's `/proc` (revision 7), `/proc/self` names an outer pid, `/proc/<getpid()>` is another process, `pgrep -P` finds nothing, `pkill` misses, and the pinned Gemini's own kill of a cancelled shell command leaves its `setsid` descendant running (Gemini collects descendants with `pgrep -P`, `chunk-RCB6BI5H.js:324855-324872`); with the fresh procfs all of them work, only the namespace's processes are listed, and the Gemini descendant is gone. The wording now says everywhere that the helper's first child is init, and AG-08's Accept requires a single-threaded helper. New A-L-01 and §5.1.9 cases. | R7-3 |
| 4 | **§5.1.4 (c) names its one exemption**: the call of `apply_runtime_env_from_control_plane` in `__main__.main`'s unsupervised branch (`koda/__main__.py:53-57`), behind the `_KODA_RUNTIME_BOOTSTRAPPED` check that (a) tests. The counts are 39 edges in 17 modules before it and 38 in 16 after, and "nothing is exempt" becomes "nothing else is exempt". C10, AG-19's Accept and MASTER_PLAN M1-E2-T3's done-when say so. REV's other option (move the branch into a module outside worker code) is not taken (§3). | R7-2 |
| 5 | **OQ-8 holds only the lifetime.** AG-14's and AG-15's Accepts are split into a confinement part, which OQ-1 needs and no question gates, and a lifetime part gated by OQ-8; until OQ-8 is approved the helper confines and execs, as revision 6 did (AG-08). An undecided OQ-8 therefore holds neither M3-E3-T1 nor AG-19b. **The compose residual gets an owner.** OQ-8 and the notice now say that the Docker deployment is expected at `subreaper` on kernels below 6.12 (Landlock ABI 6, signal scoping), where a model process can kill its helper and keep the processes it held. New AG-15b, required for the release if AG-08 records that (as expected): the kernel, PID 1 in compose and the parent of every worker, makes itself a subreaper and ends, within a second, every process it adopts that it did not start. OQ-8 asks for it with a recommendation; R-10, §3, §5.4, §6 and O-11 match. | R7-4 |
| 6 | R-3: only the agent's own secret, which travels in its snapshot, gives an agent its own runtime-access secret; `<AGENT>_RUNTIME_LOCAL_UI_TOKEN` does so only when no global `cp_secret_values` row exists, because that row is read first (`runtime_access.py:66-88`). | R7-6 |
| 7 | Bootstrap's change (it never publishes from a worker, and an unsupervised start of a never-published agent fails with `agent_not_published`) moves from AG-21b to AG-21, on which both AG-11 (the item that makes a worker-side `publish_agent` raise) and AG-21b depend, and whose Files already list `bootstrap.py:53-56`. `test_bootstrap_never_publishes_from_a_worker` moves with it; the caller table, R-9, §5.1.10 and §9.3 item 13 say "from AG-21". ## Revision 7 log

Each row names what changed and the round-6 finding it answers. Round-6 findings are numbered in review order: `S6-n` for SEC (S6-1 the spawn-lifetime major, S6-2 the runtime-access dispatch minor, S6-3 the native-install minor, S6-4 the M1-E2-T3 wording nit, S6-5 the slug nit), `R6-n` for REV (R6-1 to R6-5 the five minors in review order: the orphan sweep, the AG-07 helper, AG-19's wait on OQ-2, the M1-E2-T3 wording, the slug; R6-6 and R6-7 the two nits: reachability, `worker_reset.py`). `S6-0` and `R6-0` are done-when item 5, with each review's cross-reference to the other's.

| # | Change | Answers |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Every descendant ends with its spawn** (carried-over blocker). `koda-sandbox-exec` no longer execs the CLI: it stays as the spawn's root, outside the Landlock domain, and its child execs the CLI. The worker holds the only write end of a lifeline pipe, so the helper also sees the worker die, however it dies. When the CLI exits, on SIGTERM, or when the lifeline closes, the helper ends the spawn by the strongest mechanism the host offers, which the self-test chooses and the confinement status reports as `lifetime`: a PID namespace whose init is the helper's first child (`pid_namespace`), else a per-spawn cgroup v2 killed as a unit with `cgroup.kill` (`cgroup`), else a subreaper that kills every descendant (`subreaper`, new residual R-10, which names what remains). The same helper runs governed shell, `shell_bg`, git and `cli_runner` children, under `off` too (with no ruleset), so a worker restart ends `shell_bg` and its descendants. `[V]` with the pinned CLIs (§1.2): with no mechanism, a command's `setsid` process outlives Codex's and Claude's normal exit and a SIGKILL of the worker; in every mode it is gone at the normal end and after the worker's SIGKILL; after a SIGKILL of the helper itself it is gone in the first two modes and survives in the third. The spawn record names the cgroup before the CLI starts, and the lifeline covers the moment before the record is complete. §2.3.5, §2.4.2, §2.4.3, C11, C17; §2.2.3 and §4 are corrected; A-H-02 is rewritten, A-L-01 and a §5.1.9 case are new, and the §5.3 restart check now asserts the commands are gone. The behaviour change (a model-started server stops when its turn ends) is new operator question OQ-8. **Extended by revision 8 rows 1 to 3 and 5:** the spawns the kernel starts end with the worker that asked for them; the spawn cgroup nests in the workspace cgroup; the PID namespace mounts its own procfs (and this row now says that its init is the helper's first child, as §2.3.5 always did); OQ-8 gates only the lifetime parts, and AG-15b closes the compose case. | S6-1, R6-1 |
| 2 | **"Workers never decrypt" is checked over the whole package.** §5.1.4 (c) builds one call graph of `koda/`: calls and bound-method references through imports (lazy ones included), typed receivers, constructor chains and one-level parameter type flow, with the three crypto functions as targets. At dfa596c it finds 133 of the manager's 457 methods reaching crypto (revision 6's self-call probe: 128) and 38 worker call sites in 16 modules: C10's list plus squad runtime-HTTP dispatch (`koda/squads/dispatch.py:453-455` → `send_dashboard_squad_message` → `get_runtime_access` → `ControlPlaneRuntimeAccessBroker`, `koda/control_plane/runtime_access.py:66-79`). C10 lists that path; AG-17 owns it with `POST /internal/agents/{agent}/runtime-access` on the worker token, for the token's agent and its squad peers only; §5.1.4 (b) gets a row and a dispatch test with the key absent. R-3 records that the runtime-access signing secret is shared by every agent unless set per agent and sits in each worker's env (`manager.py:12901-12910`; `koda/services/runtime/api.py:87-101`). **Corrected by revision 8 rows 4 and 6:** the check reports 39 edges in 17 modules, 38 in 16 apart from its one named exemption; only the agent's own secret reliably sets it per agent. | S6-2, R6-6 |
| 3 | **Native installs never close R-3 to R-5.** There the worker runs as the operator's uid next to the key file in every confinement mode; L2 keeps only its children out. §2.5.2, R-3, R-4 and R-5 say "compose only; never on native installs", and the notice adds one sentence. SEC's other option (declare native installs a development mode) is not taken: it would change the documented install paths, and the residual can be stated without removing anything. | S6-3 |
| 4 | MASTER_PLAN M1-E2-T3's done-when says "the supervised env step of `koda/__main__.py` imports neither …", matching §5.1.4 (a) and AG-17's accept. | S6-4, R6-4 |
| 5 | **Claude's project directory.** The rule of 2.1.325 is recorded (`[V]`): every character outside `[A-Za-z0-9]` becomes `-`, and a result longer than 200 characters is cut to 200 and followed by `-` and a base-36 hash of the path. Koda does not compute it: it sets `CLAUDE_CODE_PROJECT_DIR_NAME=koda` on every Claude spawn, which 2.1.325 honours whenever `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is set and validates itself (`^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$`), so the transcript is always `projects/koda/<session>.jsonl` (`[V]`: resume from a copy there works). The reviewers' recommendation (compute the rule) is not taken, because the hash branch would have to be reproduced. A-C-03 covers an agent id with `_`, a workspace path with `.`, and one past 200 characters, and fails on a pin that stops honouring the variable. | S6-5, R6-5 |
| 6 | **AG-07's helper is interactive.** `run_in_transaction(fn)` runs an async callable that receives the connection, reads, computes and writes inside one transaction, as `koda/squads/threads.py:195` does. The gate's write, consume (lock, verify the MAC, increment, event) and resolution (the grant id from its sequence before the MAC) use it. §2.6, §2.9.2, AG-07, §9.3 item 2. | R6-2 |
| 7 | **AG-19 no longer waits on OQ-2.** The move of the chat setters and the runtime-settings read into `app` becomes AG-17b (M1-E2-T3), which changes no behaviour and needs no gate: `app` does what the worker does today. AG-21b keeps the OQ-2 semantics (chat changes publish their own patch, settings come from the published snapshot). The skills draft fallback moves from AG-11 to AG-10a, whose one accessor already gives secure defaults for a missing spec. AG-19 now depends on AG-10a, AG-17b and AG-22, so neither it nor, through OQ-1, M3-E3-T1 waits on OQ-2. OQ-2 states what it does gate: AG-21 and AG-21b, and through AG-21, AG-11, AG-29, AG-30 and the release. REV's first option (add OQ-2 to AG-19's gate) is not taken, because the key removal does not need the chat semantics. **Revised by revision 8 row 9:** AG-19 depends on AG-22a, not AG-22. | R6-3 |
| 8 | **No `worker_reset.py`.** A supervised worker is a new process (`supervisor.py:546-547`; the kernel's `TokioCommand::new`, `agent_workers.rs:646`), and before its env step neither `koda.knowledge.v2.common` nor `koda.state.primary` is loaded; a fresh import of them has an empty backend cache and no bridge loop (`[V]`). The reset clears nothing, so AG-17 drops it and keeps only the skip in `__main__`. R-9, AG-17, MASTER_PLAN M1-E2-T3. | R6-7 |
| 9 | Done-when item 5 can be met only by the round-7 SEC and REV reviews, recorded in VALIDATION_REPORT.md. | S6-0, R6-0 |

## Revision 6 log

Each row names what changed and the round-5 finding it answers. Round-5 findings are numbered in review order: `S5-n` for SEC (S5-1 the bootstrap-import major, S5-2 the symlink minor), `R5-n` for REV (R5-1 the AG-19 dependency major, R5-2 to R5-6 the five minors in review order: the publish-gate lock, orphan spawn homes, credential write-back, Claude's system prompt file, the AG-22 channel; R5-7 the four stale sentences). `S5-0` and `R5-0` are done-when item 5, with each review's cross-reference to the other's. SEC's unresolved R4-6 is answered by row 1.

| # | Change | Answers |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The supervised worker no longer imports `bootstrap.py`.** Today every worker does (`koda/__main__.py:53-57`; the supervisor sets `AGENT_ID`, `supervisor.py:536`), and `bootstrap.py:9` imports the manager, which imports `decrypt_secret` (`manager.py:157`); revision 5's R-9 said the opposite. Found while answering: the package `__init__` imports `bootstrap`, `manager` and `supervisor` (`koda/control_plane/__init__.py:3-5`), so importing any `koda.control_plane` submodule loads `crypto` (`[V]`). From AG-17, `__main__` imports `bootstrap` only when `_KODA_RUNTIME_BOOTSTRAPPED` is unset; the supervised branch's pool reset (`bootstrap.py:28-45`) moves to a new `koda/control_plane/worker_reset.py` that imports no manager (SEC's option (a)); and the package `__init__` drops those three imports, which nothing uses. AG-17's Files list all three files; R-9 and C10 are corrected; §5.1.4 (a) checks `sys.modules` after the supervised env step. **Corrected by revision 7 row 8:** the reset does nothing in a new worker process, so `worker_reset.py` is dropped. | S5-1, R4-6 |
| 2 | **"Workers never decrypt" is restated as a reachability property with an owner for every path.** C10 now lists every worker call that reaches crypto today, found by static reachability over the manager's self-calls (128 of its 457 methods reach crypto), with the item that removes it: AG-17 (MCP env and OAuth, the squad peer bot tokens, ElevenLabs, image generation, and the agent-directory reads `list_agents` and `get_agent`, which decrypt through derived fields, including the dashboard catalog module a child run imports), AG-21b (the runtime settings and chat setters, through `build_draft_snapshot` → `_provider_connection_env`), AG-22 (the squad spec reads), AG-11 (the skills fallback). AG-17 accepts only its own paths (§5.1.4 (b)). The whole-worker check (§5.1.4 (c)) moves to AG-19's accept and M1-E2-T3's done-when, and AG-19 and M1-E2-T3 depend on AG-11, AG-21b and AG-22, so removing the key breaks no feature and the plan has no cycle. SEC's import-closure assertion is not adopted, for the reason in §3: importability is not the boundary, the key's absence is (R-3 now says so). **Extended by revision 7 row 2:** the check covers the whole package, and C10 gains the squad runtime-HTTP path. | R5-1, S5-1 |
| 3 | **Publish gate: compare-and-swap instead of a row lock.** The store has no multi-statement transaction (each call autocommits on its own pooled connection, `koda/state/control_plane_store.py:60-97`, `koda/state/primary.py:330-388`), and the draft build writes the row the lock would hold (`manager.py:12509-12513`). The gate now builds and validates with no lock, then writes the version, `desired_version`, the apply operation and the event in one short transaction on one connection; `UNIQUE (agent_id, version)` rejects a concurrent winner, and the gate re-reads and retries once. AG-07 adds the one-connection transaction helper, not AG-21 as REV suggested, because AG-07's `required=True` audit path must write through it first and AG-21 already depends on AG-07; §2.6, §2.9.2 and §9.3 item 2 use it. The §3 rationale is corrected; the G-PG test stays, restated. **Revised by revision 7 row 6:** the helper runs an interactive callable. | R5-2 |
| 4 | **Orphaned spawns are swept.** The CLIs run in their own session (`start_new_session=True`), so the kernel's SIGKILL of the worker's group does not reach them (`[V]` for the mechanism), and nothing clears `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT`, a persistent volume in compose. Spawn homes now sit under the worker's incarnation, each spawn has a record (pid and start time) outside every grant, and a starting worker kills every recorded CLI of an earlier incarnation that is still running, then removes its home and writes nothing back from it. New A-H-02; AG-13a's accept; C17. **Superseded by revision 7 row 1:** killing the CLI's process group does not reach the model's commands; every descendant now ends with its spawn. | R5-3 |
| 5 | **Credential write-back only from spawns whose model could not write the copy:** `none` and `read_only`, and `workspace_write` once AG-23 or AG-25 proves its write tools write only the workspace; never from a shell level. AG-13a's worker-side write-back runs AG-13b's schema and same-account checks. A refresh made at a shell level is lost; whether that logs a file-OAuth provider out is O-4's evidence, and OQ-4 (c) asks the operator. A-H-01 and §5.3 add a shell that rewrites its credential copy with a later expiry. | R5-4 |
| 6 | **Every copy across the spawn-home boundary refuses links.** The worker opens the source component by component with `O_NOFOLLOW`, accepts only a regular file with one link and within a size limit, and writes a new file in the destination with `O_EXCL` before renaming it there; Claude's session id must be a bare UUID, and the project slug is computed by Koda (`[V]`: `shutil.copyfile` follows a planted link; the walk refuses links, a linked directory, a FIFO and a hard-linked file). A-C-03, A-H-01 and a §5.3 canary. **Corrected by revision 7 row 5:** the slug rule was wrong; Koda now names the project directory itself. | S5-2 |
| 7 | Claude's system prompt file moves from the task tmp into the spawn home, created with `O_EXCL`, `O_NOFOLLOW` and mode 0600 (`[V]`: the request carries its canary; a planted link at the path makes the create fail). A-C-05 asserts the path. | R5-5 |
| 8 | **Named channels.** AG-22's squad readers use `GET /internal/agents/{agent}/published-spec` on the AG-17 token (a closed field list from the target's published snapshot, for the token's own agent or a member of its squad), and AG-22 depends on AG-17. **Moved to AG-22a by revision 8 row 9.** AG-21b's runtime settings come from `GET /internal/agents/runtime-settings`, computed in `app` from the published snapshot and the global settings. §2.2.1 says so. | R5-6, R5-1 |
| 9 | Stale sentences: §1.2's prompt-input clause dropped; §4's approval expiry dated to M3-E3-T2; §5.1.4 rewritten (row 2); AG-31 documents R-1 to R-9. | R5-7 |
| 10 | **Gemini startup context rechecked** (carried-over blocker (a)). New captures at every level, and a forced `read_file` call for JIT discovery, give the per-level table of §2.3.2 (`[V]`): at `none` no tool, no workspace content and no GEMINI.md; above it the workspace-root GEMINI.md at start and, by JIT, the GEMINI.md files between the read file and the workspace root, never one above it, and never the spawn home's credential through an import. Without `memoryBoundaryMarkers: []` the ancestors and the credential import appear (positive control). A-K-05 (6) now fails closed on any ancestor or spawn-home canary, on a credential, and on any workspace content at `none`. A layout stop (empty `.git` directories) also works and is rejected as redundant (§3). R-7 and R-1 stay the named residuals. | Task brief (a) |
| 11 | **Per-spawn configuration rechecked under concurrency** (carried-over blocker (b)). Two Codex spawns of one agent run at once against one stub, at `none` and with the shell, each with its own home and developer instructions: each request carries only its own instructions and memory context, and its own tools. Two Gemini spawns at `none` and `read_only` likewise carry only their own settings, tools and cwd (`[V]`, §1.2). A-H-01 stays the race test. | Task brief (b) |
| 12 | Found while answering R5-1: turn-time reads already take the published `AGENT_SPEC_JSON`, and a dashboard draft edit reaches the worker only after a chat settings command, menu or setter forces a rebuild from the draft (`agent_settings.py:436-440`; C17). Revision 5's "within 5 s" is corrected in C9, C17, §2.2.3, the notice and OQ-2. | R5-1 (same area) |

Each row names what changed and the round-4 finding it answers. | # | Change | Answers |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Gemini startup context.** §1.2 corrects revision 3 row 7: at startup Gemini 0.68.0 loads the GEMINI.md of every workspace directory, climbs to an ancestor git root, and lists the workspace tree, at every level; `loadMemoryFromIncludeDirectories` only affects `/memory reload`. Now `none` passes no `--include-directories`, and every level writes `context.memoryBoundaryMarkers: []` and `context.includeDirectoryTree: false` (`[V]` at a gateway stub: `none` carries no canary and no tree, and no level carries an ancestor or spawn-home canary or an `@`-imported credential, with a `.git` above). §2.3.2 states each level's real capability. | 2 | Found while answering S4-1: a workspace GEMINI.md that is a symlink is followed at startup and by JIT discovery, so at `read_only` and above it can put the provider credential or `/proc/self/environ` into the request (`[V]`). R-1 records it as a Gemini path that AG-25's file-tool proof does not close; A-K-05 (6) records it per pin; the notice and §3 say so. | S4-1 (same class) |

O-4 covers concurrent spawns of one agent. ## Revision 4 log

Each row names what changed and the round-3 finding it answers. Round-3 findings are numbered in review order: `S3-n` for SEC, `R3-n` for REV. `S3-0` and `R3-0` are done-when item 5, with each review's cross-reference to the other's; only the round-4 reviews can meet it.

| # | Change | Answers |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Chat settings changes leave the worker** (new AG-21b). The worker calls `POST /internal/agents/settings-change` with its AG-17 token (the agent comes from the token), a typed patch from the closed list and the asserted Telegram user id, which the control plane checks against the agent's allowed users. The control plane writes the patch to the draft, applies it to the last published snapshot, runs the gate and signs. `publish_agent` and signing run only in `app`: AG-11 depends on AG-21b, and a worker-side `publish_agent` yields no version the supervisor accepts (A-P-07). A-P-04 moves to the route. §2.9.2 and R-4 record the route as a resolution the worker asserts. The header and §7.1 say that M1-E2-T5's scope is extended. | R3-1, R3-11 |
| 2 | The worker's model policy moves to the published snapshot in AG-21b, the same merge that makes `/provider`, `/model` and functional defaults publish; AG-10a no longer moves it. Until AG-21b the worker reads the draft model policy as today, and S10 refuses for the turn a provider that the published ceiling does not cover. §2.2.3, the notice, OQ-2 and A-P-06 state that every chat settings change restarts the agent's worker: the kernel gives a running turn 15 s, unfinished tasks are re-queued by the new worker, and pending approvals expire. **Qualified by revision 5 row 11** (approvals expire only from M3-E3-T2; the recovery limit; dashboard edits on publish). | R3-2 |
| 3 | The closed `telegram_user` patch list adds `providers.{elevenlabs,kokoro}_default_{voice,language}`. The caller table, AG-21b and OQ-2 list the natural-language settings path (`messages.py:29` → `chat_settings.py`) and `commands.py:2398`. §5.1.10 names one test per caller. | R3-3 (a), (b) |
| 4 | The runtime-snapshot fallback publish is deleted. It published whenever the requested version's snapshot was missing (`manager.py:12633-12636`). Now a missing version raises and nothing is published. A `system` publish of an agent that has any version is refused, so resetting `desired_version` to 0 cannot cause a republish. From AG-21b on, bootstrap never publishes from a worker. §5.1.10 tests both. **Revised by revision 8 row 7:** from AG-21 on. | S3-4, R3-3 (c) |
| 5 | **Gemini `.env` search.** The home rebuild writes an empty, Koda-written `<gemini home>/cwd/.gemini/.env`. Gemini's search walks up from the cwd to `/`, so it now stops at the cwd. Verified: no ancestor or home file is loaded, and no new warning appears. The pins stay as defence in depth, plus three more: `GEMINI_CLI_HOME` set to the Gemini home, and `GEMINI_CLI_SYSTEM_SETTINGS_PATH` and `GEMINI_CLI_SYSTEM_DEFAULTS_PATH` set to a path under `/dev/null`, which can never exist. The statement about which ancestors exist is corrected: on native installs they include the operator's HOME, and in compose `/var/lib/koda/runtime`. A-K-05, A-G-01, A-G-02, A-G-08 and the §5.3 canaries plant unpinned canaries such as `CODE_ASSIST_ENDPOINT`. | S3-1, R3-4 |
| 6 | A-K-05 adds a per-key wrong-type probe. Every key of the §2.3.2 user settings, written with the wrong type, must print `Error in: <key>` (for `telemetry.*`, 0.68.0 names the enclosing `telemetry` object). Otherwise Gemini is unavailable. **Revised by revision 5 row 5:** `telemetry.*` leaves the per-key probe. | S3-2 |
| 7 | Codex 0.169.0 feature counts corrected: 72 default-on, 39 at `none`, 40 at shell levels. `instant_interrupt` joins the keep list: it is a code-mode interrupt behaviour, and disabling it leaves the model-visible input unchanged apart from ids and timestamps. A-K-02's fixture records the exact lists. | S3-3, R3-5 |
| 8 | **Codex `none` is proven offline for every model Koda configures.** A request captured at a local stub at `none` carries `apply_patch` and `request_user_input` for `gpt-5.5`, and only `request_user_input` for `gpt-5.4`, `gpt-5.4-mini`, `gpt-5.4-nano`, `gpt-5.3-codex`, `gpt-5.3-codex-spark` and `gpt-5.2`. With the shell enabled, `exec_command` and `write_stdin` are added. A-K-04 uses this capture, and uses prompt-input only for code-mode models. §5.3 adds a live check per configured model, and a model without both proofs is unavailable below the shell levels. Revision 3 row 2 is qualified. **Revised by revision 5 row 4:** forced calls replace the live check in the proof, and the prompt-input branch is dropped. | R3-6 |
| 9 | AG-11 depends on AG-21b, which brings AG-21's actor-aware primitive. The `get_agent_spec` clause moves to AG-22, which changes the planner. | R3-7 |
| 10 | Gate lines are added: OQ-2 on AG-21 and AG-21b, OQ-3 on AG-13a and on AG-23 to AG-25, OQ-5 on AG-16, OQ-6 on AG-24. The §9.1 rows name their items, and MASTER_PLAN M1-E2-T2 lists them. | R3-8 |
| 11 | R-7 adds Claude's session transcripts. Bash at `workspace_exec` can rewrite them, and the next `--resume` loads the edit. §5.3 adds a canary. The residual is recorded rather than closed (§3). | R3-9 |

Each row names what changed and the round-2 finding it answers. Round-2 findings are numbered in review order: `S2-n` for SEC, `R2-n` for REV (`S2-0` and `R2-0` are each review's done-when item 5, which only the round-3 reviews can meet).

Under L2 (the defaults `strict` and `filesystem`) Codex therefore has `none` or `workspace_exec` (`danger-full-access`, confined by L2).
