The pins stay as defence in depth. `GEMINI_CLI_HOME` is pinned because a planted `.env` can set it and move every Gemini path (§1.2).
- **No host system settings.** `GEMINI_CLI_SYSTEM_SETTINGS_PATH` and `GEMINI_CLI_SYSTEM_DEFAULTS_PATH` point under `/dev/null`, which can never exist, so nothing has to keep the path absent. A root-owned `/etc/gemini-cli/settings.json` on the host would otherwise sit above Koda's user settings in the merge order (§1.2). Missing system files are skipped silently, so this pin prints nothing.
- **Why trust comes from the env.** Headless Gemini refuses an untrusted cwd. `--skip-trust` trusts only after the first settings load, so trust would change within a run when the 10 s settings cache expires (§1.2). `GEMINI_CLI_TRUST_WORKSPACE=true` makes every load see the same trusted cwd, which holds nothing but the stop file.
- **Fail closed on settings.** Gemini prints `Invalid configuration in …`, drops the bad key and keeps running (§1.2), so a warning seen at spawn time comes too late to stop a run that has already started without that restriction. The check therefore runs before any spawn. At each worker start, per CLI fingerprint, A-K-05 loads **the exact settings file Koda writes for each level** (for each `security.auth.selectedType` value Koda writes) and requires no warning; Koda writes only a settings file whose SHA-256 A-K-05 has loaded cleanly. The stderr check at spawn time (A-G-09) stays as a backstop: a line `Invalid configuration in` or `Security Warning: Skipping` kills the process and fails the turn. Gemini 0.68.0 also accepts an unknown key silently (§1.2), so a later pin that renamed `tools.core` or `hooksConfig.enabled` would drop that restriction without any warning. A-K-05 therefore also runs a per-key probe: each key of the user settings below, written alone with the wrong type, must print `Error in: <key>`. If any key stops erroring, Gemini is unavailable. The `telemetry.*` keys are not in the per-key probe, because the `telemetry` object is strict and a wrong type and an unknown key print the same `Error in: telemetry` (§1.2); for telemetry the env pins are the control (they take precedence over the settings), and a renamed telemetry key would make Koda's own file print that error in the load probe above. A-K-01 does the same for Codex. No file needs root ownership, so the same design works on native installs.
- The non-streaming runner uses `json` and the streaming runner `text`, as today (`gemini_runner.py:351, 525`).

Koda-written user settings (`security.auth.selectedType` is added from the provider connection when file OAuth is used):

```json
{
  "tools": { "core": ["<per level>"], "discoveryCommand": "", "callCommand": "" },
  "mcpServers": {},
  "hooksConfig": { "enabled": false },
  "skills": { "enabled": false },
  "experimental": { "enableAgents": false, "autoMemory": false },
  "security": { "disableYoloMode": true },
  "telemetry": { "enabled": false, "logPrompts": false },
  "privacy": { "usageStatisticsEnabled": false },
  "context": { "loadMemoryFromIncludeDirectories": false, "memoryBoundaryMarkers": [], "includeDirectoryTree": false },
  "advanced": { "ignoreLocalEnv": true },
  "general": { "enableAutoUpdate": false }
}
```

`security.disableYoloMode` is `false` only at `workspace_exec`. No `admin.*` key is written, because Gemini ignores it from files. The two new `context` keys are the same at every level: `memoryBoundaryMarkers: []` stops the GEMINI.md search at each workspace directory itself, so it never climbs to an ancestor git root (the cwd's ancestors include the agent's provider homes and Koda's runtime root), and it confines `@` imports to the importing file's own directory; `includeDirectoryTree: false` keeps the directory listing out of the first request (§1.2).

| Level | `--approval-mode=` | `tools.core` | Selected by | Class |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `none` | `default` | `[]` (no built-in tool registers, §1.2); no `--include-directories` | S1 = `none`; S7 | S |
| `read_only` | `default` | `list_directory, read_file, grep_search, glob` | S1 default; S2 to S6 | S once AG-25 proves path confinement, else M |
| `workspace_write` | `auto_edit` | the above plus `replace, write_file` | S1 = `workspace_write`; S9 for `workspace_exec` without L2 | S once proven, else M |
| `workspace_exec` | `yolo` | the above plus `run_shell_command` | S1 = `workspace_exec` | M (needs AG-14) |

**One row per Gemini approval mode:**

| Mode | Koda state that selects it | Argv | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| `default` | `none` and `read_only` levels: S1, S2 to S7 | `--approval-mode=default` | In headless mode any tool that would ask is denied, so only the allowlisted read tools run. |
| `auto_edit` | `workspace_write` | `--approval-mode=auto_edit` | Edit tools are auto-approved; no shell tool is registered. |
| `yolo` | `workspace_exec` only | `--approval-mode=yolo` | Every registered tool is approved; refused by `security.disableYoloMode` at every other level (verified offline). |
| `plan` | None. Today: S2, S3, S4 (C3) | not emitted | Read-only by mode rather than by registry, adds plan-mode tools and reroutes the `auto` model to Pro. |
| `-y/--yolo` (alias) | None | not emitted | Same as `yolo`; one spelling only. |
| `-s/--sandbox` (Gemini's own container sandbox) | None | not emitted | Needs docker or podman inside the runtime image. |

Never emitted: `--prompt`/`-p` (the prompt goes on stdin), `--skip-trust`, `--allowed-tools`, `--allowed-mcp-server-names`, `--policy`, `--admin-policy`, `--resume`, `--session-file`, `--session-id`, `--raw-output`, `--accept-raw-output-risk`, `--worktree`, `--debug`, `--acp`. Gemini 0.68.0 has native resume, but Koda keeps Gemini stateless with transcript replay (M3-E5-T1). Floor: `none`.

**GEMINI.md and the startup context (revisions 5 and 6; revision 3's statement was wrong).** No 0.68.0 setting turns GEMINI.md off. At startup Gemini loads the GEMINI.md of every workspace directory, and the include directories are workspace directories whatever `loadMemoryFromIncludeDirectories` says (§1.2). When the cwd is trusted, the cwd is a workspace directory too, so without a boundary setting the climb starts at the cwd and reaches the spawn home and every ancestor even at `none` (`[V]`, positive control). What each level really gives the model on 0.68.0, verified on the first request at the stub and, for JIT, on the follow-up request after a forced `read_file` (revision 6, §1.2):

| Level | Capability | Workspace in the request | GEMINI.md in the request | Tools |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `none` | `[]` | nothing: no `--include-directories`, no tree | none: the cwd holds only the stop file, its climb is off, and the spawn home is new | none |
| `read_only` | `[read]` | the workspace through the read tools; no tree | the workspace-root GEMINI.md at startup, with `@` imports only from inside the workspace root; by JIT, the GEMINI.md files between the directory of a file a tool reads and the workspace root; never one above the workspace, and never the spawn home's credential through an import (R-7). A workspace GEMINI.md that is a symlink is followed, to any file the process can read (R-1) | `list_directory`, `read_file`, `grep_search`, `glob` |
| `workspace_write` | `[read, write]` | as above | as above | the above plus `replace`, `write_file` |
| `workspace_exec` | `[read, write, exec, egress]` | as above, and anything L2 grants through the shell | as above | the above plus `run_shell_command` |

**How the climb is stopped, and how it fails closed.** `memoryBoundaryMarkers: []` makes each workspace directory its own ceiling, at startup, in JIT discovery and for `@` imports, at every level; `none` also passes no workspace directory. The settings key is guarded twice: A-K-05 (3) fails when the key stops erroring on a wrong type (a rename), and A-K-05 (6) fails when its meaning changes. Gemini is unavailable on a pin where (6) shows, at any level, an ancestor or spawn-home canary or the credential through an import, or shows any workspace content at `none`. A layout stop also works (`[V]`: an empty `.git` directory in `cwd/` and in the workspaces root keeps the ancestors out with the default markers), but §3 rejects it.

Two residuals remain at `read_only` and above, and they are named: R-7, because OQ-3(b) cannot be honoured for Gemini there (the workspace-root GEMINI.md loads at startup and JIT adds more); and R-1, because a workspace GEMINI.md symlinked to the provider's credential file or to `/proc/self/environ` puts that content into the request, which AG-25's path-confinement proof of the file tools does not cover. A-K-05 (6) records both per pin, so a pin that changes either is noticed.

**Prompt channel.** System instructions stay on stdin, because `GEMINI_SYSTEM_MD` replaces the CLI's whole built-in prompt (§3); it is pinned to `false`, the built-in prompt. The query is escaped so that `<` and `>` inside a tag-like sequence that matches either delimiter become `&lt;` and `&gt;`, so user text cannot close `<user_request>` or open `<system_instructions>`.

#### 2.3.3 Codex 0.169.0

**Every level, new turn:**

```
codex exec --json --cd <dir> --model M [--skip-git-repo-check]
  --ephemeral --ignore-rules
  --disable <F>   for every F in the 0.169.0 disable set below
  -c agents.enabled=false -c skills.include_instructions=false -c skills.bundled.enabled=false
  -c approval_policy="never"
  -c shell_environment_policy.inherit="core"
  -c shell_environment_policy.exclude=["*_API_KEY","*TOKEN*","*SECRET*","POSTGRES*","KNOWLEDGE_V2_S3*"]
  -c mcp_servers={} -c web_search="disabled" -c project_doc_max_bytes=0
  <level flags, below>
  [-c model_reasoning_effort=E] [--image P …] -
<dir> = the workspace; for S7 internal calls, a fresh empty per-task dir
env: HOME = CODEX_HOME = the spawn's Codex home (XDG_* inside it); TMPDIR = the task tmp;
     developer instructions (the task's system prompt, memory context included) in its
     Koda-written config.toml (0600), private to this spawn (§2.3.5)
```

`web_search="disabled"` replaces `tools.web_search=false`; both keys exist, and the enum key is unambiguous. Developer instructions move off argv (today `-c developer_instructions=…`, `codex_runner.py:171-174`) into the Koda-written `config.toml`; `--ignore-user-config` is therefore not used.

**Reviewed feature set (0.169.0).** Every default-on feature that adds a tool, a browser, a sub-agent, a process, a plugin or skill install, or an approval authority other than Koda is disabled at every level (32): `apps`, `auth_elicitation`, `browser_annotation_api`, `browser_use`, `browser_use_external`, `browser_use_full_cdp_access`, `computer_use`, `daemon_auto_start`, `goals`, `guardian_approval`, `guardian_include_additional_context`, `guardian_reuse_parent_compaction`, `hooks`, `image_generation`, `in_app_browser`, `in_app_chat`, `in_app_dictation`, `in_app_local_automation`, `in_app_updates`, `in_app_voice`, `mcp_oauth_refresh_coordination`, `multi_agent`, `plugin_sharing`, `plugins`, `remote_plugin`, `shell_snapshot`, `skill_mcp_dependency_install`, `skill_search`, `tool_call_mcp_elicitation`, `tool_suggest`, `view_image` (it reads any image path outside the shell's sandbox) and `workspace_dependencies`. Below the shell levels `shell_tool` is disabled too. Of the 72 default-on features, the ones left enabled form the reviewed keep list: 39 at `none`, plus `shell_tool` (40) at shell levels. It contains model, protocol, budget and display switches (`api_key_cyber_access_programs`, `api_key_model_discovery`, `bedrock_setup_wizard`, `cli_daybreak`, `compaction_image_budget`, `content_item_kinds`, `deferred_tool_world_state`, `enable_request_compression`, `fast_mode`, `incremental_tools`, `incremental_tools_legacy_gcp`, `mentions_v2`, `prediction_preserve_parent_tools`, `reasoning_effort_override`, `retain_client_tool_outputs`, `system_proxy_fallback`, `ultrafast_mode`, `unbounded_connection_retries`, `unified_image_budget`, `untrusted_as_tool_output`, `write_stdin_approval`); the code-mode host, a JavaScript orchestrator with no filesystem or network of its own (`code_mode_host`, `code_mode_host_grpc`, `code_mode_host_output_truncation`, `code_mode_interrupt`); `instant_interrupt` ("under development"; it sits beside `code_mode_interrupt` and `interrupt_on_cancel` in the feature registry, adds no tool, process or authority, and disabling it leaves the model-visible input unchanged apart from ids and timestamps, §1.2); `sleep_tool`; the shell implementation, which exposes nothing without `shell_tool` (`unified_exec`, which cannot be disabled, and `unified_exec_tty`); and removed features that still report `true` (`collaboration_modes`, `item_ids`, `realtime_conversation`, `resize_all_images`, `sqlite`, `steer`, `terminal_resize_reflow`, `tool_search_always_defer_mcp_tools`, `tui_app_server`, `worktrees`). A-K-02 fails when `features list` shows any other enabled feature, so a version bump that adds a default-on feature stops the build until it is reviewed. Revision 3 missed `instant_interrupt` because its keep list was written by hand. AG-24 will therefore store the recorded CI output of `features list` for each level as fixtures (`tests/fixtures/provider_cli/codex-0.169.0-features-{default,none,shell}.txt`; today `tests/fixtures/provider_cli/` holds only the three Claude 2.1.325 files) and generate the keep list and the counts from them.

**Compile rule.** `IP(mode)` is the inner-sandbox probe `codex sandbox -c sandbox_mode="<mode>" [the row's workspace-write keys] -- <python> -c <probe>`, run **through the same launcher and confinement profile as the spawn** (`koda-sandbox-exec` with the Codex profile under L2; the same helper with no ruleset under `off`, §2.3.5, which leaves Codex's inner sandbox working, `[V]` §1.2). It passes when the probe exits 0, a write outside the writable roots fails and a socket fails. It runs at worker start, is cached per CLI fingerprint and profile, and is shown in health and in the spawn event. `L2` means confinement status `enforced` or `filesystem_only` (§2.4). An inner sandbox mode is selected only when its `IP` passed in that profile (A-X-10).

| Effective level | L2 on | L2 off (`off`) |
|---|---|---|
| `none` | `--sandbox read-only --disable shell_tool` | same |
| `read_only` | `IP(read-only)` fails inside L2 on 0.169.0 → not faithful → `none`. A later pin whose `IP` passes there gets `--sandbox read-only` with the shell. | not faithful → `none`: the inner read-only sandbox reads every file the uid can read (§1.2), so it does not confine reads to the workspace |
| `workspace_write` | `IP(workspace-write)` fails inside L2 → `none` | `IP` ok: `--sandbox workspace-write -c sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=false` `-c sandbox_workspace_write.exclude_slash_tmp=true -c sandbox_workspace_write.exclude_tmpdir_env_var=true -c sandbox_workspace_write.writable_roots=["<task tmp>"]`; `IP` failed: `none` |
| `workspace_exec` | `IP(workspace-write)` ok inside L2: the off-mode row with `network_access=true`; failed (0.169.0): `--sandbox danger-full-access`, with L2 enforcing the workspace and the TCP ports | `IP` ok: the `workspace_write` row with `network_access=true`; `IP` failed: `none` |

So on 0.169.0 under the defaults `strict` and `filesystem`, Codex has two working levels: `none` (it answers with no shell and no file reads) and `workspace_exec`. `danger-full-access` is emitted only under L2, so in `off` no Codex level is weaker than today. At `none`, `gpt-5.5` keeps `apply_patch` in its tool list, because 0.169.0 has no switch for it. `--sandbox read-only` with `approval_policy="never"` refuses the patch, inside and outside the workspace (`[V]`, forced call at the stub, §1.2), and under L2 the workspace is read-only at `none`, so a patch cannot land either way. Codex at `none` is class S; with a shell it is class M.

**`none` is proven per model, offline.** Codex at `none` is available for a model only when the contract records, for that model and pin, both halves of A-K-04, run against the local stub (§1.2):

- **offered:** the first request's `tools` hold no `exec_command`, `write_stdin`, `view_image` or `collaboration.*` tool, while `exec_command` is present at the shell levels as a positive control;
- **refused:** the stub answers with forced calls, one per run: `exec_command`, `write_stdin`, `shell`, `shell_command`, `local_shell_call`, `view_image`, and `apply_patch` (as a custom tool call and as a function call) adding a file inside and outside the workspace. At `none` each is refused or ignored, no file appears, and the outside canary never reaches a request. At the shell levels, the calls of the tools the model is offered (`exec_command`, and `apply_patch` where offered) run, as the positive control.

A real model rarely calls a tool it was not offered, so a live check proves little about refusal; the forced calls prove it for whatever a model sends. The `apply_patch` refusal is part of the proof, so `gpt-5.5`, Koda's default model, is covered. A model whose first request carries no `tools` array (the code-mode models, §1.2) cannot be checked this way and is `unproven`; none is configured today (`koda/config.py:278-287`). A configured model without both halves (for example one an operator adds to `CODEX_AVAILABLE_MODELS`) is `unproven` too. Codex is then unavailable for that model below the shell levels, internal calls included, with the reason `codex_none_unproven:<model>` in health; it is never silently trusted. The §5.3 per-model live check stays as evidence that the real provider behaves like the stub; it is not part of the availability rule.

**Resume.** `exec resume` takes no `--sandbox`, and whether it honours `-c sandbox_mode` is unproven. Native resume is off: Codex declares `supports_native_resume=False`, and follow-ups get transcript replay (M3-E5-T1, Q-13). `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox` is never emitted and leaves the contract. If AG-24's live check later proves `exec resume -c sandbox_mode="<S>"`, the contract adds `sandbox_mode` for the `exec_resume` scope and resume returns.

**One row per Codex sandbox and approval-policy pair:**

| # | `sandbox_mode` | `approval_policy` | Koda state that selects it | Argv |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `read-only` (shell disabled) | `never` | Effective `none`: S1 = `none`; S7; `read_only` (S1 default, S2 to S6) clamped by S8 on 0.169.0; `workspace_write` under L2 clamped by S8 | the block above with `--sandbox read-only --disable shell_tool` |
| 2 | `workspace-write` | `never` | Effective `workspace_write` under `off` with `IP` ok | `--sandbox workspace-write` with `network_access=false`, both tmp excludes and `writable_roots=["<task tmp>"]` |
| 3 | `workspace-write` + network | `never` | Effective `workspace_exec` under `off` with `IP` ok; under L2 only when `IP(workspace-write)` passed inside the L2 profile (never on 0.169.0) | row 2 with `network_access=true` |
| 4 | `danger-full-access` | `never` | Effective `workspace_exec` under L2 when `IP` fails inside the profile (always on 0.169.0). Today: every non-dry-run turn (C2) | `--sandbox danger-full-access` |
| 5 | `read-only` | `on-request` | None | not emitted; AG-24 narrows the contract's `approval_policy` choices to `never` |
| 6 | `workspace-write` | `on-request` | None | not emitted |
| 7 | `danger-full-access` | `on-request` | None | not emitted |
| 8 | `read-only` | `on-failure` | None | not emitted |
| 9 | `workspace-write` | `on-failure` | None | not emitted |
| 10 | `danger-full-access` | `on-failure` | None | not emitted |
| 11 | `read-only` | `granular` | None | not emitted |
| 12 | `workspace-write` | `granular` | None | not emitted |
| 13 | `danger-full-access` | `granular` | None | not emitted |
| 14 | `read-only` | `untrusted` | None (0.169.0 refuses it at config load) | not emitted |
| 15 | `workspace-write` | `untrusted` | None (refused by the CLI) | not emitted |
| 16 | `danger-full-access` | `untrusted` | None (refused by the CLI) | not emitted |
| 17 | (no sandbox) | `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox` | None. Today: resume in danger mode (C2) | not emitted; removed from the contract |
| 18 | `workspace-write` (forced by the flag) | `--approve-for-me` (automatic review) | None | not emitted |

`read-only` with the shell enabled (row 1 without `--disable shell_tool`) has no selecting state on 0.169.0; it is today's dry-run argv and the memory extractor's (C2). Rows 2 and 3 differ only in `network_access`. Today an operator could select rows 5 to 16 through `CODEX_APPROVAL_POLICY` (`codex_runner.py:175-176`); AG-24 stops reading that setting.

Why only `never`: `exec` is headless, so any policy that asks has nobody to ask; `on-request` and `granular` hand the decision to the model or to categories Koda does not govern, and `--approve-for-me` routes approvals to an automatic reviewer, which is an authority other than Koda policy (invariant 1). Never emitted either: `--trust` (it would load the workspace's config layer and persist trust) and `--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust`.

#### 2.3.4 HTTP providers

Ollama, llama.cpp, MLX and the OpenAI-compatible providers have no built-in tools; the field does not apply and the UI shows "não se aplica". Their ignored `permission_mode` and `dry_run` parameters are removed. Their `tool_calls` already go through the dispatcher and policy (`_run_agent_loop`). D-147 stays a separate item.

#### 2.3.5 Persistent instructions and extensions

A model with write access, or a shell, can plant files that a later spawn loads automatically. Koda closes the places where that happens:

- **Provider homes are per spawn** (revision 5; revision 6 adds the incarnation, the records, the sweep and the copy rules; revision 7 the spawn lifetime and the fixed Claude project directory). Each CLI process gets its own home, created empty for that spawn at `<RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT>/provider-homes/<agent>/spawns/<incarnation>/<spawn id>/` (0700). `<incarnation>` is a random id drawn once per worker process: by the worker at start until AG-15, and from AG-15 by the kernel, which passes it in the worker's env with the worker's kernel token ("Spawns the kernel starts" below). A worker started without the supervisor, which the kernel did not spawn, still draws its own and holds no kernel token (R-9, OQ-10). `<spawn id>` is random. The home is removed when the process has exited. HOME, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CODEX_HOME`, `GEMINI_CLI_HOME` and `XDG_*` point into it. Koda writes into it only what that spawn needs, before the CLI starts, and only named files cross in or out:

  | Provider | Koda writes for the spawn | Copied in before the run | Copied out after the run |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Claude | a minimal `.claude.json`; `system.md` (§2.3.1) | for `--resume ID`, `projects/koda/<ID>.jsonl` from the agent's session store (`[V]`: enough to resume, §1.2); `.credentials.json` (file OAuth only) | `projects/koda/<session>.jsonl` of the session id the run reports, into the session store (Bash at `workspace_exec` can rewrite it, R-7); a refreshed credential file, when the write-back rule allows it (§2.4.4) |
  | Codex | `config.toml` with the developer instructions and the §2.3.3 settings | `auth.json` (file OAuth only) | a refreshed `auth.json`, when the write-back rule allows it (§2.4.4) |
  | Gemini | `.gemini/settings.json`; `cwd/` (0500) holding only the empty stop file `cwd/.gemini/.env` (0400, in a 0500 `.gemini/`) | `.gemini/oauth_creds.json`, `.gemini/google_accounts.json` (file OAuth only) | refreshed credential files, when the write-back rule allows it (§2.4.4) |

  **How a file crosses.** The worker, which is unconfined, does every copy, so it must not follow anything the model planted in a home it was granted:
  - The source is opened component by component from the source root's directory fd with `O_NOFOLLOW` (`openat`), the last component with `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`. It is accepted only if `fstat` shows a regular file with one link, within a size limit (64 KiB for a credential file, 64 MiB for a transcript).
  - The content is written to a new file created with `O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW` (0600) in the destination directory, fsynced, and renamed into place there. A content copy is used even within one volume, so a planted link or hard link is never moved into the session store; the credential check-in crosses volumes anyway (`koda-runtime` to `koda-state`).
  - The destination directories are Koda-created and granted to no confined process.
  - Claude's session id, as the run reports it, names a file only if it is a bare UUID (`^[0-9a-f]{8}(-[0-9a-f]{4}){3}-[0-9a-f]{12}$`; 2.1.325 uses one, `[V]`). The project directory is always `projects/koda/`: Koda sets `CLAUDE_CODE_PROJECT_DIR_NAME=koda` on every Claude spawn, which 2.1.325 honours whenever `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is set (Koda always sets it) and validates itself against `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$` (`[V]`, §1.2: resume from a copy there works). So Koda computes no slug and never reads a directory name from the home. Without the variable, 2.1.325 names the directory after the cwd, with every character outside `[A-Za-z0-9]` replaced by `-` and, past 200 characters, a cut and a hash (`[V]`, §1.2); revision 6's rule ("each `/` becomes `-`") was wrong. The provider contract records, per pin, that the variable is honoured and the pin's own cwd rule, which is A-C-03's positive control. A-C-03 fails on a pin that stops honouring the variable, and Claude resume is then unavailable on that pin (follow-ups are replayed, M3-E5-T1).
  - Anything else copies nothing, and the audit event `provider.spawn_copy_refused` records the reason (§2.6.2; `[V]` for the mechanism, §1.2).
  - Copy-in needs the same care only for the source: its destination is the new, empty home before the CLI starts.

  Everything else a CLI writes (Codex's SQLite state and memories, Claude's `.claude.json` backups and plugin store, Gemini's `tmp/` and trust files, C18), and anything a model plants (`CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `GEMINI.md`, `settings*.json`, `skills/`, `rules/`, `hooks.json`, extensions, `.env`), goes with the home. Therefore:
  - no spawn reads what another spawn wrote. Two concurrent spawns of one agent, at different levels and with different users' instructions and memory context, each see only their own instructions, settings and cwd (A-H-01; `[V]` at the CLI level for Codex and Gemini, §1.2). Under L2 each CLI process is granted only its own spawn home (§2.4.2), so a `workspace_exec` shell cannot rewrite the home of a concurrent turn (§5.3);
  - there is no allowlist rebuild that could miss a file, and nothing deletes a file under a running spawn: a home is removed only after the helper that ran it has exited, whether the worker or the kernel started it (the sweep below);
  - the only per-agent provider state is the Claude session store, `<RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT>/provider-homes/<agent>/claude-sessions/`, which only the worker reads and writes and no CLI process is granted. Concurrent turns of one Claude session each resume from the stored copy, and the last to finish replaces it, so the file is never interleaved (today both write in the one shared home);
  - credential files are copied in under the per-provider lock, and written back only by the rule of §2.4.4.

  **Spawn lifetime** (revision 7). A killed worker is now routine: every published chat change restarts it, and the kernel SIGKILLs it after 15 s (C17). The CLIs run in their own session, and so does each command the model runs through them (C17), so neither the CLI's process group nor the worker's reaches those commands. A process that outlived its spawn would keep the spawn's L2 grants (the workspace, the task tmp, the TCP ports) after the turn, after a lowered level and after the worker restarted (invariant 1). So every model-reachable process ends with the spawn that started it:
  - **The helper is the spawn's root.** Every spawn through `koda-sandbox-exec` (worker-side: provider CLIs, `shell_bg`, git and `cli_runner` children, AG-14; kernel-side: `StartTask`, which starts provider CLIs when runtime environments are on, `ExecuteCommand`, which runs governed shell, the kernel's own git and tar on a task's workspace, AG-15, and with OQ-9 the operator commands, operator-authored scheduled shell jobs and the operator terminal; class U, AG-16) is started as `koda-sandbox-exec <profile> --lifeline <fd> -- <argv>`, in a new session. The helper does not exec the command. It sets `PR_SET_CHILD_SUBREAPER` and reaps, in every mode (revision 9), and forks a child that applies `no_new_privs`, the Landlock ruleset and the scoping, raises its own `oom_score_adj` (so the OOM killer picks model processes before the helper), and execs. Because the helper is a subreaper, a process orphaned inside a live spawn (a background server whose shell command has returned) is reparented to the helper (in `pid_namespace`, to init inside the namespace), stays in the spawn until the spawn ends, and reaches the kernel only when the helper itself has died (`[V]`, §1.2; revision 8 set the subreaper only in the third mode, so in `cgroup` mode AG-15b would have killed that server while its spawn ran). The helper stays outside the Landlock domain, so at ABI ≥ 6 no confined process can signal it. Under `off` on Linux it runs with no ruleset, so the lifetime is the same in every confinement mode.
  - **When the spawn ends.** The helper ends the spawn when the command exits, on SIGTERM (the worker's stop, a timeout, a `shell_bg` kill), or when the lifeline reports end of file. It first sends SIGTERM to every process of the spawn and waits up to 2 s, and only then applies its mode's kill below, so a git process that is still writing removes its lock files (revision 11; `[V]`, §1.2: a SIGKILLed `git add -A` leaves `.git/index.lock`, a SIGTERMed one does not; §7.9). Each mode finds those processes its own way (revision 12): in `pid_namespace` the helper signals init, which sends SIGTERM to every other process in the namespace (`kill(-1, SIGTERM)` from init); in `cgroup` the helper signals every pid in the spawn cgroup's `cgroup.procs`; in `subreaper` it signals every descendant it finds by walking `/proc/<pid>/task/*/children` down from itself, not only its own children. A git that a model's shell command line runs is a grandchild with a live parent: signalled alone, `sh` exits, git is reparented to the helper and keeps writing, and the kill 2 s later leaves `.git/index.lock` (`[V]`, §1.2, with `sh -c 'git add -A; true'`). The worker holds the only write end of each lifeline, opened `O_CLOEXEC` so that no other child inherits it, so end of file means the worker process has exited, however it exited, the kernel's SIGKILL included. `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` from the worker was rejected because it follows the thread that forked (§3); a pipe end belongs to the process.
  - **How every descendant ends.** AG-08's self-test picks the first mechanism the host supports, and the confinement status reports it as `lifetime` (§2.4.3):
    1. `pid_namespace`: the helper creates a user and a PID namespace with an identity uid and gid map, so the command sees its own uid, never root, and the helper's first child is init (pid 1) of that namespace. Init sets `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` to SIGKILL towards the helper (the death signal follows the thread that forked, so the helper is single-threaded). Init then unshares a mount namespace, makes `/` recursively private and mounts a fresh procfs on `/proc` (revision 8), forks the command, reaps orphans, and exits when the command exits. The command applies Landlock after that mount, so its `/proc` grant is the new procfs, which lists only the namespace's processes: `getpid()`, `/proc/self`, `/proc/<pid>`, `pgrep`, `pkill` and `kill` agree, as tools that find processes by pid need (Gemini's own kill of a cancelled shell command finds descendants with `pgrep -P`), and the worker and the kernel are not listed. With the host's `/proc`, as revision 7 had it, none of that holds (`[V]`, §1.2). When init exits, for any reason, the kernel kills every other process in the namespace, and no process can leave its PID namespace. The command runs as a non-root uid in the namespace, so it holds no capability after exec. The mode needs unprivileged user namespaces and a procfs mount inside them, which a container that masks paths under `/proc` is expected to refuse (`[I]`); the self-test selects it only if the whole sequence works.
    2. `cgroup`: where user namespaces are refused but a delegated cgroup v2 subtree is writable (the root `isolation.rs` uses, `KODA_CGROUP_ROOT`; kernel ≥ 5.14 for `cgroup.kill`), the helper creates `spawn_<spawn id>/` as a child of the cgroup it starts in, read from `/proc/self/cgroup`, and the child joins it before Landlock. That cgroup is the agent's workspace cgroup `<root>/ws_<workspace>` (`isolation.rs:142-146`): the kernel places every worker there (`agent_workers.rs:685-696`), so a worker-side helper starts there, and the kernel starts its own helpers there (AG-15). No controller is enabled in the workspace cgroup's `cgroup.subtree_control`, so it can hold the worker and the spawn cgroups at once, and `cgroup.kill` and `cgroup.events` are core files that need no controller. The limits the kernel writes on the workspace (`memory.max`, `cpu.max`, `pids.max`, `cpuset.cpus`, `isolation.rs:148-171`) therefore bound every spawn too, because cgroup v2 limits are hierarchical; a spawn cgroup beside the workspace's, as revision 7 had it, would have escaped them (`[V]` for the nesting and the kill, `[I]` for the limits, §1.2). A helper whose own cgroup is not a workspace cgroup under the root (the kernel's placement soft-fails, C11) cannot use this mode, and the self-test, run from where spawns start, reports the next one. Ending the spawn writes `1` to its `cgroup.kill`, waits until `cgroup.events` reads `populated 0`, and removes it. Under L2 no confined process can write cgroupfs, because `/sys` is outside every grant (§2.4.2), so none can leave the cgroup. If the helper itself dies, its parent kills the cgroup when it sees the helper exit on a signal (the worker for a helper it started, the kernel for one it started, "Spawns the kernel starts" below), the sweep below does so at the next worker start, and the kernel kills every spawn cgroup when it starts. Each acts only on a recorded or computed path equal to `<root>/ws_<workspace>/spawn_<spawn id>` for that agent and spawn id.
    3. `subreaper`: where neither is available, ending the spawn sends SIGKILL to every process whose parent is the helper (every orphaned descendant is reparented to it, because it is a subreaper), and reaps it, until none is left. This holds while the helper lives. A descendant survives only if the helper is killed first: by the model's own code where Landlock cannot scope signals (ABI < 6, before kernel 6.12, or under `off`), or by the OOM killer or an operator. That is R-10, and health shows it. Where AG-15b runs, the kernel, to which the dead helper's orphans are then reparented, ends such a survivor within a second, unless the same command has killed the kernel too (R-10 case (2)).

    **Stopping a stuck helper.** When Koda has to stop a helper that has not ended its spawn by itself (`terminate_internal`'s wait below, the sweep, a timeout), it sends SIGCONT and SIGTERM, so a stopped helper resumes and runs its own ending, and waits up to 5 s. If the helper still runs: in `subreaper` mode it sends SIGKILL to every pid in `/proc/<helper>/task/<helper>/children` (the helper is single-threaded, so that file lists all its children) and repeats until the list stays empty, each orphan of a killed child being reparented to the helper in the meantime, and only then SIGKILLs the helper; in `pid_namespace` the helper's SIGKILL ends the namespace; in `cgroup` it SIGKILLs the helper and writes the spawn's `cgroup.kill`. So Koda's own stop never orphans a descendant (`[V]`, §1.2: a direct SIGKILL of a stopped subreaper helper left both its descendants running; killing its children first left none).

    `[V]` for all three with the pinned Codex and Claude (§1.2). Docker's default seccomp profile is expected to refuse the namespaces, and its cgroup mount is read-only in an unprivileged container (C11), so compose is expected to report `subreaper` (`[I]`; AG-08's self-test and §5.4 record it). The hosts §5.4 expects run kernels below 6.12, so there Landlock cannot scope signals, and a model process can kill its helper; AG-15b, required for the release where AG-08 records this, OQ-8 is answered (a) and OQ-9 (b) and (c) are approved (§6), has the kernel end what that helper held. Where there is no helper (macOS, or `off` without the binary), `lifetime` is `none` with the reason `no_helper`: only the CLI's process group is signalled, as today, and AG-15b is off (R-10 case (4)). While OQ-8 is pending or declined the helper runs without `--lifeline` and execs the command (AG-08), and `lifetime` is `none` with the reason `lifetime_not_approved` (R-10 case (5)).
  - **Spawns the kernel starts** (AG-15; revision 8, ordered in revision 9, one spawn function in revision 10). A provider CLI started through `StartTask`, a governed shell command run through `ExecuteCommand` (C17), and, with OQ-9, an operator command or the operator terminal (`OpenTerminal`, `server.rs:1412`) are children of the kernel, not of the worker, so the worker's lifeline cannot cover them, and `StartTask` returns while the process runs. The kernel therefore binds each to the worker incarnation that asked for it:
    - When it spawns a worker (`agent_workers.rs:646`), the kernel draws the worker's incarnation and mints its kernel token, passes both in the worker's env, and keeps the incarnation's spawn set under one lock.
    - **One spawn function under the incarnation lock (revision 10).** Every kernel spawn, AG-15 and AG-15b alike, goes through one function that takes the incarnation lock, checks that the incarnation is open, forks the helper (in the token agent's workspace cgroup with the spawn id it chose, and with a lifeline whose only write end it holds, `O_CLOEXEC`), records the helper's pid and lifeline in the spawn set, and only then releases the lock. A closed incarnation refuses the request before the fork, so a revoked token starts nothing, and closure, which takes the same lock, always sees a spawn in flight and waits for it. The lock order is incarnation then scan, so AG-15b's scan (which also takes the scan's lock to record a pid) stays deadlock-free. Revision 9's separate "pending entry before the fork" scheme and its "closed in between" branch are gone, because the fork and the record now happen under one lock.
    - It closes a spawn's lifeline, so the helper ends the spawn with every descendant, when its task ends: `TerminateTask`, the end or timeout of `ExecuteCommand`, or the close of the terminal.
    - **The kernel's own git, tar and copy** (worktree creation and removal, checkpoints, the copy of a `copy` environment; revision 11) go through the same spawn function and helper, but the kernel holds their lifeline for itself, outside the incarnation's spawn set, and closes it when the command ends, at its own timeout, or when the kernel exits. They are the kernel's bookkeeping on its own records, so a worker exit or restart during `SaveCheckpoint`, `RestoreCheckpoint` or `CreateEnvironment` does not stop them: ending them with the incarnation would SIGKILL git mid-write and leave `.git/index.lock` or a ref lock in the workspace, in the operator's own repository for a `shared` environment, or in the common directory for a worktree (`[V]`, §1.2). A revoked token still starts none, because the request is refused before the fork (§7.9).
    - **The end of an incarnation**, in this order, whether `terminate_internal` stops the worker (`agent_workers.rs:455-519`, which `terminate_agent_worker` also uses) or the reaper sees it exit (`:542-559`):
      1. `terminate_internal` sends the worker SIGTERM. Its token stays valid and its kernel spawns keep running through the 15 s grace, so a turn can finish, and a `StartTask` or `ExecuteCommand` issued during the grace works (§2.2.3).
      2. Once the worker has been reaped, after it exited or after the SIGKILL at the end of the grace (`:480-499`), the kernel closes the incarnation under the lock: it marks it closed, revokes the token and takes its spawn set. Because every spawn is fully recorded under that lock before its request returns, the set holds every live spawn.
      3. It closes every lifeline in the set, waits up to 5 s for those helpers, and stops a helper that is still running as "Stopping a stuck helper" above says. An open operator terminal of this incarnation closes here (OQ-9 (c), the notice).
      4. Only then does the caller spawn the next worker (`:266-281`, the respawn of an exited worker and the restart on a new version).
    - **A helper the kernel started that exits on a signal** before its command: in `cgroup` mode the kernel writes that spawn's `cgroup.kill` (the path follows from the token agent's workspace and the spawn id the kernel chose); in every mode it reports the signal exit in the task's status or the command's response, and the worker writes `provider.spawn_helper_lost` (§2.6.2).
    - **At start**, before it spawns any worker, the kernel kills every `spawn_*` cgroup under each `<KODA_CGROUP_ROOT>/ws_*`: no helper of its own can be alive then, because the previous kernel's lifelines closed when it exited and its workers received their death signal (`agent_workers.rs:672-676`). The root is the one `isolation.rs` uses (`KODA_CGROUP_ROOT`, default `/sys/fs/cgroup/koda`, `isolation.rs:136-146`; `sandbox_doctor.py:261`), and it carries no install id. Revision 10's per-install subtree `<KODA_CGROUP_ROOT>/<bid>` is withdrawn (revision 11): `<bid>` is the agent id (`config.py:18-19`), and the kernel has no install id, so that subtree separated nothing. A second kernel on the same host that shares the root has its spawn cgroups killed too (R-13). When the kernel itself exits, every lifeline it holds closes.

    So no process the kernel started for an incarnation runs when the next incarnation starts (its own git, tar and copy excepted, which run only commands the kernel chose and hold no spawn home), and the next worker's sweep finds no live helper behind a kernel spawn's record and never removes a home a running CLI uses (A-L-02).
  - **What changes for the model and the operator.** A process the model starts, a background server for example, runs while its spawn runs and ends with it, whether the worker or the kernel started that spawn: at the end of the provider run, of the governed shell command, or of the `shell_bg` process. Every `shell_bg` process ends when its worker exits, which a worker restart makes certain, rather than running on unmanaged as today (C17). An open operator terminal of a runtime environment closes when the agent's worker restarts, because the kernel binds it to that incarnation (OQ-9 (c)). Operator question OQ-8; for operator commands and operator-authored scheduled shell jobs, OQ-9 (c).
  - **Records.** Before it starts the helper, or asks the kernel to start a provider CLI (`StartTask`), the worker writes `provider-homes/<agent>/spawn-records/<incarnation>/<spawn id>.json` with the launcher (`worker`, or `kernel` with the task id), the lifetime mode, the cgroup path in the `cgroup` mode (chosen before the start) and, for a provider CLI, the spawn home; after the start it adds the helper's pid and start time (`/proc/<pid>/stat` field 22; for a kernel spawn, the pid `StartTask` returns, `kernel_subprocess.py:164`; the worker and the kernel share one PID namespace). A governed shell command, an operator command and an operator terminal have no home and no record; the kernel ends them. No confined process is granted `spawn-records/`. A worker that dies between the start and the second write leaves a record without a pid, and the lifeline, the worker's or the kernel's, still ends that spawn.
  - **The sweep.** At worker start, before its first spawn, the worker handles every record and home of another incarnation. The kernel runs one worker per agent and reaps the old one before it spawns the new one (C17), so none of them belongs to a live worker (unless another install shares this runtime root, R-13). Their helpers have seen their lifelines close: the old worker's when it exited, and the kernel's, which it closed for that incarnation and whose helpers it waited for before it spawned this worker (above). As a backstop for a helper that still runs with the recorded start time, whichever process started it, the worker stops it as "Stopping a stuck helper" above says (SIGCONT and SIGTERM, a 5 s wait, and in `subreaper` mode its children before it); a pid reused by another process is left alone; a recorded cgroup that still exists gets `cgroup.kill` if its path is the expected one, and any other recorded path is left alone and logged. It then removes the home and the record, and writes `provider.orphan_spawn_swept`.
  - Nothing crosses out of an orphan: its transcript and credential copies are discarded (an interrupted task is re-queued and replays, C17).
  - When it starts each spawn, the worker also removes any home of its own incarnation that no live spawn owns (a removal that failed earlier). A removal failure is logged as `spawn_home_removal_failed` and retried at that point.
  - A-H-02 (homes and records), A-L-01 (the lifetime of worker-side spawns), A-L-02 (the spawns the kernel starts) and §5.1.9 (the helper) test this.

  The homes are not placed under the task tmp, as REV's round-4 review suggested: governed shell and git are granted the task tmp read-write under L2 (§2.4.2), so a background shell of the same task could plant files in the next spawn's home there. A spawn home is granted to that one CLI process only, and so is Claude's system prompt file inside it (§2.3.1).

- **Workspace customisation** is not loaded:
  - Claude: `--safe-mode` (CLAUDE.md, skills, plugins, hooks, MCP, commands, agents) and `--restricted` (project settings).
  - Codex: `project_doc_max_bytes=0` (workspace AGENTS.md), skills off (workspace `.agents/skills` and `.codex/skills` included), `--ignore-rules`, the reviewed feature set, and no `--trust`, so the workspace `.codex/config.toml` is not loaded (all `[V]` offline, §1.2).
  - Gemini: the workspace is never the cwd, so its `.gemini/settings.json` and `.env` files are never read; the stop file ends the `.env` search at the cwd, so no ancestor's `.gemini/.env` is read either; `memoryBoundaryMarkers: []` stops the GEMINI.md search at each workspace directory, so no ancestor's GEMINI.md is read; the host's system settings are pinned away; extensions off by `--extensions=none`; hooks, skills, sub-agents and MCP off in the user settings; the workspace GEMINI.md as described in §2.3.2.
- **Host-level CLI configuration** (O-10). Gemini's system settings are pinned away (§2.3.2). Claude applies admin-managed settings even under `--restricted` and `--safe-mode`, and Codex reads `/etc/codex/*.toml` (§1.2). L2 grants `/etc` read-only, so no confined process can plant these files. Under `off`, a Koda running as root could plant them, and then they would affect every agent (R-8). AG-23 and AG-24 record whether a pinned path or a flag excludes each file, and add each to the canaries.

Executable customisation (hooks, MCP servers, plugins, extensions, rules, skills, discovery commands) would run code at `read_only` and must stay off for the levels to mean anything. Instruction files (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, GEMINI.md) only steer the model; turning them off removes a feature users may rely on. Both are operator question OQ-3. If the operator keeps instruction files, Claude omits `--safe-mode` and Codex omits `project_doc_max_bytes=0`; Gemini needs no change, because it loads the workspace-root GEMINI.md at `read_only` and above in any case (§2.3.2). Residual R-7 then applies to all three. Executable customisation stays off for Claude without `--safe-mode`: `--restricted` ignores the settings files that define hooks and enable plugins, a spawn home starts with no installed plugins, `--disable-slash-commands` turns off skills and commands, `--strict-mcp-config` turns off MCP, and custom agents need the Agent tool, which is never in `--tools` (`[I]`, covered by the live canaries).

Live canaries (§5.3) cover each crossing file and each discarded item, in the workspace and in the spawn home. For Gemini they also cover every ancestor of the cwd, with a canary variable that is not pinned and a canary GEMINI.md.

### 2.4 Confinement

#### 2.4.1 Threat and classes

**Threat.** A prompt-injected model drives built-in tools, governed shell or git, MCP servers and media tools. At risk: the master key, the previous key and `bootstrap.txt`; the worker env (DSN, S3 secret, granted secrets, runtime tokens); other agents' workspaces, homes and sessions; kernel RPC, the sidecars and publish integrity.

| Class | Members | If a required layer is missing |
|---|---|---|
| **M**: runs model-directed code | governed shell, `shell_bg` and git; a shell job whose command or work dir the model wrote (`job_create`, `job_update`; with OQ-11 (a), §2.9.1); worker-side git on workspaces, and the kernel's own git, tar and copy (worktree creation and removal, checkpoints, `copy` environments), which AG-15 runs through the helper and AG-03 hardens (residual R-11a where the helper cannot be used); Codex at any level with a shell; Claude and Gemini at `workspace_exec`; any CLI level not proven self-confined; `cli_runner.py` spawns; with OQ-9, the operator commands (`/shell`, `/git`, `/pip`, `/npm`), operator-authored scheduled shell jobs and the operator terminal of a runtime environment, which a human starts but which run in a work dir the model can write, so they can run code it planted there (§2.9.1) | Provider levels clamp to the highest class S level (S9); governed processes, a model-written shell job included with OQ-11 (a), are refused (from AG-14 on), with a reason pointing to `koda doctor`; operator commands and operator-authored jobs run as today until AG-15 (R-11) and are refused from then only where the class M profile cannot be enforced |
| **S**: self-confined CLI level | Claude `none`, `read_only`, `workspace_write`; Gemini levels below `workspace_exec`, once proven; Codex `none` for each proven model (§2.3.3) | Runs with the available layers |
| **U**: untrusted input, operator-installed code | MCP stdio servers, yt-dlp, ffmpeg, ffprobe, Playwright chromium | Runs with a minimal env and a health warning |
| **T**: trusted | Login flows in `app`, with an explicit allowlist and reasons | Not confined; HOME is the app-only login home (§2.4.4) |

#### 2.4.2 Layers

- **L0 Placement** (host-independent):
  - `koda-state` is mounted only in `app` (AG-19). The master key, the previous key and `bootstrap.txt` never enter `runtime-kernel`, and the key never moves.
  - Worker data moves to a new `koda-worker-state` volume (`WORKER_STATE_ROOT`), mounted in both `app` and `runtime-kernel`; every C14 consumer resolves through it in both processes. The supervisor forwards `WORKER_STATE_ROOT` instead of `STATE_ROOT_DIR` (`supervisor.py:92`).
  - CLI login homes move off `koda-runtime` to the app-only path (§2.4.4).
  - No Koda state or temporary file lives in a directory other users or agents share: `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT` defaults to `~/.koda-runtime/<bid>` (mode 0700) on native installs instead of the system temp dir, the compose kernel root moves to `/var/lib/koda/runtime/kernel`, and Docker MCP env files are written into a 0700 per-worker directory and deleted once the container has started (AG-03, AG-12).
- **L1 Process hygiene:** AG-04 (implemented) makes the worker and the kernel non-dumpable with no cores. One allowlist builder makes every child env, with no DSN, S3 secret, granted secrets, runtime, kernel or broker token; it sets HOME and the `XDG_*` directories to the child's own home and `TMPDIR` to the task tmp; this covers MCP and media spawns (AG-03). Prompts stay off argv, because `/proc/*/cmdline` stays readable even under Landlock `[I]`.
- **L2 OS confinement.** One Rust primitive, `rust/koda-runtime-kernel/src/confinement.rs`, exposed as the `koda-sandbox-exec` binary in the existing crate (no new service, ADR-001):
  - Its child applies `no_new_privs`, then a Landlock filesystem ruleset, then at ABI ≥ 4 TCP rules (connect only to `RUNTIME_CONFINEMENT_EGRESS_PORTS`, default `443,53`, plus the port of the provider's configured base URL; no bind), then at ABI ≥ 6 signal and abstract-UNIX-socket scoping, so a confined process cannot kill the worker, the kernel or its own helper, or reach their abstract sockets. Then the child execs the command.
  - **Lifetime.** The helper itself stays as the spawn's root, outside the domain, and ends the spawn with every descendant: in a PID namespace whose init is the helper's first child and which has its own procfs, else in a per-spawn cgroup nested in the workspace cgroup, which it kills as a unit, else as a subreaper (§2.3.5, R-10).
  - Landlock's ptrace restriction stops reads of the unconfined worker's and kernel's `/proc/<pid>/environ` and `mem`, and of sibling domains, on every ABI.
  - It exits 126 if any requested right cannot be enforced. Nesting only narrows. A process inside it cannot change mount topology, which is why Codex's bubblewrap sandboxes cannot start there (§1.2).
  - **What it does not cover:** TCP rules are per port, so the allowed ports reach every host, and an internal service listening on 443, 53 or the provider port is reachable; UDP is not restricted (DNS and other UDP egress stay open); connecting to a pathname UNIX socket is not a Landlock right. So under `strict` the kernel listens on TCP only, and a UNIX-socket kernel target is refused at start (M1-E6-T1 constraint, §9.3).
  - Both spawn paths start through it: worker-side spawns through `koda/security/process_confinement.py` (AG-14), and kernel `StartTask`, `ExecuteCommand` and, with OQ-9, `OpenTerminal`, whose profile the kernel computes itself (AG-15); the kernel holds the lifeline of the spawns it starts and ends each with its task, with the worker incarnation that asked for it, or with the kernel (§2.3.5). An operator command gets the governed-shell column below, with its own work dir as the workspace and a fresh per-command tmp in place of the task tmp.
  - A workspace grant is refused when the work dir fails AG-12's predicate, which the worker checks again for every worker-side spawn (AG-14) and the kernel for every kernel spawn (L4). The predicate takes the token's agent (on the worker side the worker's own `AGENT_ID`) and the environment the kernel recorded for the task, if any, and has three parts (revision 11; the agent, the environment and the third part are revision 12's). The work dir may not **be or contain** a shared or Koda root: HOME, `/tmp`, `/var/tmp`, `/dev/shm`, the system temp dir, `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT` or `RUNTIME_KERNEL_ROOT`. It may not **be, contain or lie under** a Koda-owned root: a state root (`STATE_ROOT_DIR`, `WORKER_STATE_ROOT`), a provider or login home, the artifact root, the `tmp_images` root, `<RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT>/provider-homes`, which holds every spawn home, spawn record and Claude session store (§2.3.5), the Koda directories that default under `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT`, wherever they are set (`CONTROL_PLANE_RUNTIME_DIR`, which holds the agents' runtime snapshot directories and the cwd of the control plane's unconfined login CLIs, `control_plane/settings.py:56-59`, `manager.py:2841-2843, 12640`; `IMAGE_TEMP_DIR` and `ARTIFACT_CACHE_DIR`, `config.py:1019-1036`; `<RUNTIME_ROOT_DIR>/tasks`, every agent's runtime task directories, `controller.py:111-113, 265-270`), and, with OQ-12 (b), the Koda install directory (`SCRIPT_DIR`, `config.py:14-15`). And **where Koda owns the parent, it allows instead of denies**: under `RUNTIME_KERNEL_ROOT` only the task's recorded environment passes (with its admin and common directory, L4; with no environment, nothing there); at or under `AGENT_WORKSPACE_ROOT` only the token agent's own `<AGENT_WORKSPACE_ROOT>/<agent>`, so another agent's workspace never passes, in either layout; and anything else under `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT` or, on native installs, `~/.koda-runtime` (which holds every agent's default `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT`, L0) is refused. Revision 11's two parts alone passed, on the worker side, where nothing else checked them, another agent's workspace and every Koda directory under the compose `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT` outside `provider-homes`, which every agent's worker shares (`docker-compose.yml:57, 250`; `supervisor.py:91, 512-515`), and on native installs another agent's `~/.koda-runtime/<agent>`. So a work dir under HOME (a native `/setdir` project, a `PROJECT_DIRS` entry, the agent's own native workspace) and the agent's own compose workspace pass it. The allowlist removes nothing users have today: those parents hold only Koda's own files, `~/.koda-runtime` is new, and in compose they lie under `/var/lib`, which `validate_work_dir` refuses today (`config.py:775`). A work dir the model chooses under HOME outside Koda's roots, `~/.ssh` for example, passes the predicate; limiting it is OQ-12 (a) (R-14).

  Allowlist (anything else is denied, including worker-state, the artifact root, `/app`, `/workspace`, shared HOME, other agents' workspaces and homes, the login homes, and the `tmp_images` root):

  | Grant | Provider CLI | Governed shell and git | MCP stdio | Media tools | Chromium |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | read-only: `/usr`, `/lib*`, `/bin`, `/sbin`, `/etc`, `/opt`, `/proc`, required `/dev` nodes; `CODEX_BIN`/`GEMINI_BIN` must resolve inside these | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes |
  | read-write: task tmp | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes |
  | workspace | read-only at `none` and `read_only`, else read-write | read-write | only if the server declares it | no | no |
  | own spawn home, read-write (Gemini's `cwd/` inside it is mode 0500); never another spawn's home, the Claude session store or `spawn-records/` | yes | no | no | no | no |
  | other | — | — | server package read-only; state dir read-write | input read-only; output dir read-write | browsers path read-only; profile read-write |

- **L3 Provider layer (inner):** Claude `--restricted --safe-mode` and its tool lists; Codex's reviewed feature set and, only where `IP` proved it inside the same profile, its inner sandbox; Gemini's `tools.core` and user settings. Spawn homes live under `<RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT>/provider-homes/<agent>/spawns/<incarnation>/<spawn id>/`, one per CLI process, created empty and removed after it, and swept at the next worker start when a killed worker left one (§2.3.5). A work dir always passes AG-12's predicate for the worker's own agent (L2 above), checked by path component; this also applies to `agent_set_workdir` (`tool_dispatcher.py:1404-1437`), to `job_create` and `job_update` (`:1116`; `scheduled_jobs.py:1473-1477`) and to internal calls (S7), and with OQ-12 (a) a work dir the model sets must also lie in the roots that question names.
- **L4 RPC:** kernel RPC authentication (M1-E6-T1) is a prerequisite for AG-15, which adds per-worker kernel tokens, minted by the kernel when it spawns the worker, bound to that worker's agent and incarnation, and revoked when the incarnation closes (§2.3.5). From AG-15 a worker holds **only** this per-worker kernel token, not the M1-E6-T1 shared token (ADR-035, MASTER_PLAN M1-E6-T1); a worker the kernel did not spawn has none (R-9, OQ-10). **Every kernel RPC has a binding (revision 10, completed in revision 11).** Revision 9 bound only the spawn RPCs, so the kernel's other RPCs took ids or paths from the body: `RestoreCheckpoint` used `payload.workspace_path` when set (`server.rs:3059-3061`), `WriteTerminal` and the other terminal RPCs looked up by `session_id` only (`server.rs:1568-1576`), and `CreateEnvironment` derived the environment id from a body task id that is global across agents (`environment_id = env-<body task id>`, inserted over any existing record, `server.rs:945, 1002-1007`; one `tasks` table with a `BIGSERIAL` id, `postgres_backend.py:933-940`). So §5.4's "cannot use agent A's kernel token for agent B's paths" held only for the spawn RPCs. L4 now gives every RPC of `RuntimeKernelService` (`runtime.proto:551-583`) the binding in the table below (revision 10's list left out `StreamTerminalSession`, `CollectSnapshot`, `GetCheckpoint`, the three browser-session RPCs, `GetAgentWorker` and `Reconcile`): each id in the body is resolved from kernel state, the agent it leads to is compared with the token's, and no `workspace_path` or `runtime_dir` is taken from the body. This is defense in depth: in-process worker code already shares the uid (C11), so it is not a model-reachable path. The kernel then binds each `StartTask`, `ExecuteCommand` and `OpenTerminal` to the token's agent and incarnation and computes the floor itself: one workspace, one spawn home under that agent's `provider-homes/<agent>/spawns/<incarnation>/` (provider CLIs only), and the task tmp. A caller profile can only narrow it; a request without a profile is rejected. The kernel decides the workspace (revision 9, worktree directory corrected in revision 10):
  - **For a task with an environment**, it is the `workspace_path` the kernel recorded when it created that environment for that task id (`EnvironmentRecord`, `state.rs:68-83`; `server.rs:981-1004`), the environment's agent must be the token's (`get_environment_for_task`, `server.rs:3252-3268`), and the path must pass AG-12's predicate. An `ExecuteCommand` or `OpenTerminal` in a task's environment names it (`runtime_env_id`, which `ExecuteCommand` already carries, `server.rs:1266`). A `worktree` environment's path is `<RUNTIME_KERNEL_ROOT>/worktrees/task-<id>-<slug>` (`workspace.rs:109-111`). Its `<base>/.git` is not the git directory: when the base is a repository subdirectory `<base>/.git` does not exist, and when the base is a linked worktree it is a file into another repository's common directory (`[V]`, §1.2). The floor therefore grants the new worktree's own administrative directory and the repository's **common** directory, which the kernel records from `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` (resolved, and config-checked by AG-03 with OQ-9 (a); without (a) the kernel's git runs as today, unchecked, R-11a) when it runs `git worktree add` (`workspace.rs:130-138`); it grants them only when both lie under the base or under the token agent's own `<AGENT_WORKSPACE_ROOT>/<agent>` and pass AG-12's predicate. Where the common directory lies outside both (a repository the operator uses outside Koda), the kernel creates the environment as `copy` instead, or refuses it. A `copy` or `copy_fallback` environment's path is its own copy under the same root (`workspace.rs:143-153`), and a `shared` one's is the base work dir itself. The kernel's own git and tar on that workspace (worktree creation and removal, checkpoint `diff`/`reset`/`apply`/`tar`) and the copy of a `copy` or `copy_fallback` environment run through `koda-sandbox-exec` under the same task class M profile, plus the repository's common directory for a worktree and, for a restore, read-only access to that one checkpoint's patch and bundle files (the kernel writes the checkpoint files itself from the commands' output, `server.rs:2931-2950`; `checkpoints.rs:97-98`). The copy reads the base and writes only the new path, follows no link and skips FIFOs and devices: `copy_workspace_recursive` takes each entry's type from readdir and then copies by path, so it would follow a link swapped in between, and a FIFO would block a kernel thread (`workspace.rs:68-97`; revision 11, §7.9). Where the helper cannot confine them they run in-process, hardened with OQ-9 (a), residual R-11a.
  - At `CreateEnvironment` the agent comes from the token, never from the body (`server.rs:934-937` reads it from the body today), and `base_work_dir` and a body `workspace_path` must pass the predicate of the next item.
  - **Otherwise** it is the request's work dir, accepted only if it passes AG-12's predicate for the token's agent with no recorded environment, so nothing under `RUNTIME_KERNEL_ROOT` (every task's worktree and copy, and the kernel's own `tasks/` and environment directories, `workspace.rs:112-115`, `server.rs:216-222`), no other agent's `<AGENT_WORKSPACE_ROOT>/<agent>` and no Koda directory under `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT` passes. From revision 12 these are the predicate's own rules (L2), so the worker side (AG-14) refuses exactly what this branch refuses; revision 11 stated the cross-agent rule here only. The predicate itself refuses a work dir under a state root, a provider or login home, the artifact or `tmp_images` root or `provider-homes` (revision 10: revision 9 refused only one that is or contains them, although the model picks the path itself, `agent_set_workdir`, `tool_dispatcher.py:1413`, `job_create`'s `work_dir`, `:1116`, and worker-state holds cross-agent data, C14). Revision 10 also refused here, and in the predicate, anything under `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT`, and in the predicate anything under HOME, which refused the compose workspaces (`/var/lib/koda/runtime/workspaces/<agent>`, under `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT=/var/lib/koda/runtime`, `docker-compose.yml:57`), every environment and every native work dir under HOME; revision 11 narrows that to `provider-homes` and to "is or contains", and revision 12 allows under those Koda-owned parents only the agent's own workspace and the task's environment (L2).
  - The grant is read-only or read-write by level, as in the table above. So agent A's token can get neither agent B's environment or workspace, nor another task's worktree, nor any directory of the kernel's own, nor a directory under the state roots, a login home, `provider-homes` or any other Koda directory under `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT`; nor, from revision 12, can agent A's worker-side spawns (AG-14).

  **Binding of every kernel RPC** (revision 11; AG-15's auth matrix reads this list from the proto):

  | RPC (`runtime.proto` line) | Binding from AG-15 |
  |---|---|
  | `CreateEnvironment` (552) | The agent is the token's; the record is keyed by the token's agent and the task id, a body `agent_id` is ignored, and lookups resolve within the token's agent (§7.9; revision 12: no first-come refusal on the global task id); `base_work_dir` and a body `workspace_path` must pass the Otherwise item's rule |
  | `StartTask` (553), `ExecuteCommand` (554), `OpenTerminal` (557) | The token's agent and incarnation; the floor above |
  | `TerminateTask` (555), `StreamTerminal` (561), `PauseTask` (563), `ResumeTask` (564), `FinalizeTask` (565), `CollectSnapshot` (566), `CleanupEnvironment` (568), `SaveCheckpoint` (579) | The task's agent, from kernel state, is the token's; `SaveCheckpoint`'s `environment_id` must be the task's own |
  | `AttachTerminal` (556), `WriteTerminal` (558), `ResizeTerminal` (559), `CloseTerminal` (560), `StreamTerminalSession` (562) | The session's own task is the body's task (all that `server.rs:1857-1875` checks today), and that task's agent is the token's |
  | `GetCheckpoint` (580), `RestoreCheckpoint` (581) | The checkpoint's own task is the body's task, whose agent is the token's (both look the id up in one global map today, `server.rs:3012-3069`); a restore writes only that task's recorded workspace |
  | `StartBrowserSession` (576), `StopBrowserSession` (577), `GetBrowserSession` (578) | The task's agent is the token's; `runtime_dir` is always `<RUNTIME_KERNEL_ROOT>/tasks/<task>/browser`, never the body's (`server.rs:2402-2411` uses the body's when set) |
  | `Reconcile` (567), `EnsureAgentWorkers` (573), `GetAgentWorker` (574), `TerminateAgentWorker` (575) | The supervisor's token only (`server.rs:3158-3209`; `Reconcile` rewrites every agent's environment state, `server.rs:2205-2239`, and the worker client's `reconcile`, `koda/internal_rpc/runtime_kernel.py:875`, has no caller) |
  | `Health` (582) | None |

  The control-plane worker broker (AG-17) has its own per-worker token.

#### 2.4.3 Modes

One setting, `RUNTIME_CONFINEMENT`, in `config.py` and `.env.example`.

| Mode | Required for class M | Use |
|---|---|---|
| `strict` (default) | filesystem + ptrace + TCP (ABI ≥ 4, kernel ≥ 6.7) | Normal |
| `filesystem` | filesystem + ptrace (ABI ≥ 1, kernel ≥ 5.13) | Explicit operator choice; audit event and a persistent health banner. Internal TCP ports stay reachable until M5-E5. |
| `off` | nothing; each provider's own restriction is the only layer (§2.3) | Development, macOS or native installs. Explicit config, a red banner that states R-8, a startup log line and `confinement=off` in every spawn event. The installer never writes it. Every class M process reads every file the uid can read (R-8). |

The status (`enforced | filesystem_only | unavailable`, the ABI, the scoping flags, and `lifetime`: `pid_namespace | cgroup | subreaper | none`, §2.3.5) is computed at worker start and shown in health, `koda doctor` and the spawn event. With `lifetime` `subreaper` or `none`, health shows R-10 with its case (for `subreaper`, whether the ABI scopes signals and whether AG-15b's reaper runs; for `none`, its reason, `no_helper` or `lifetime_not_approved`). On a native host whose ABI does not scope signals and whose mode is `cgroup` or `subreaper`, health also shows R-10 case (2): a command can stop the runtime kernel itself. On any host below Landlock ABI 6 whose lifetime mode is not `pid_namespace`, where signals are not scoped, health shows R-12: one agent's shell-level command can stop every other agent's worker and turn. `koda-sandbox-exec --self-test` settles what a host supports, including Docker's seccomp `[I]`.

**Compose** (`docker-compose.yml`, `packages/cli/release/bundle/docker-compose.release.yml`, `packages/cli/release/CHECKSUMS.txt`): AG-19 removes `koda-state` from `runtime-kernel` and adds `koda-worker-state` to both services. AG-19b adds `security_opt: [no-new-privileges:true]`, `cap_drop: [ALL]` and, after the smoke test, `read_only: true` with a tmpfs `/tmp`. No user-namespace or seccomp relaxation, no bwrap, no uid split, no new container.

#### 2.4.4 Provider credentials and login homes

- **Env credentials** where the provider supports a non-rotating token (API keys, Claude's long-lived `setup-token`): delivered in the CLI env by the allowlisted builder; nothing is written to the home.
- **File OAuth** (Claude `.credentials.json`, Codex `auth.json`, Gemini `oauth_creds.json` and `google_accounts.json`; the same names D-230 deletes, `manager.py:1575-1580`):
  - The canonical login homes move from `koda-runtime` (`docker-compose.yml:29-31`, mounted read-write by `runtime-kernel` at `:250`) to `STATE_ROOT_DIR/provider-logins/<provider>` on `koda-state`, which becomes app-only with AG-19. Class T login flows run with that HOME. The move copies and verifies; the old copies go one release later (AG-32). Operator question OQ-4.
  - Every spawn works on its own copy (AG-13a). Before the spawn the named credential files are copied into the spawn home (0600, by the copy rules of §2.3.5). Until AG-13b the worker copies from and to the canonical login home, which it can already read and write today. From AG-13b it checks the files out through the AG-17 broker (`POST /internal/provider-credentials/{provider}/checkout`, per-worker token) and checks them in afterwards. Config, hooks and instruction files never cross, because nothing else is transferred.
  - **Write-back only from a spawn whose model could not write the copy.** The spawn home is granted read-write to the CLI process (§2.4.2), so at a shell level the model can rewrite the credential copy, with a later expiry or another account's token, and a write-back would put it into every later spawn of every agent on that provider. A refreshed copy is therefore written back only from a spawn at `none` or `read_only` on any provider, or at `workspace_write` once AG-23 (Claude) or AG-25 (Gemini) proves that its write tools write only the workspace. It is never written back from a shell level: `workspace_exec`, or Codex `workspace_write` under `off`. Until a provider's compiler lands (AG-23 to AG-25), every spawn of that provider still runs today's bypass mode (C1 to C3), in which the model can already rewrite the shared login home (C13). In that window AG-13a writes back as the shared home behaves today, with the checks below, which is no less restrictive than today (§6). The rule above applies from that provider's compiler on.
  - **Checks on every write-back.** The named files only, each parsed against a per-provider schema: known keys, the size limit, the same account id as the canonical copy, and an expiry later than the canonical copy's. The write happens under the per-provider lock. AG-13a's worker-side write-back runs these checks itself, and from AG-13b the control plane runs the same checks at check-in. Concurrent eligible spawns each hold a copy, so two refreshes at once leave the newer one in place. The account and expiry checks guard against a CLI bug, not the model, which cannot write the file at an eligible level. A copy from a shell-level spawn, or one that fails a check, is discarded, and the audit event `provider.credential_writeback_refused` records the reason (§2.6.2).
  - **What a shell level gives up.** A refresh made during a shell-level spawn is lost. On a provider that rotates refresh tokens and revokes the old one, the canonical copy then stops working, and the provider has to log in again. Whether each pinned CLI rotates, and whether rotation survives per-spawn copies with concurrent spawns of one agent and of several agents, needs live evidence (O-4). Env credentials (API keys, Claude's `setup-token`) do not refresh and are recommended for agents at shell levels. If rotation does not survive, file OAuth modes require env credentials under confinement. The per-spawn copy and the shell-level rule change how production credentials are handled, so they wait for OQ-4 (§9.1).

### 2.5 Secrets and the master-key boundary

#### 2.5.1 Key handling (AG-05, implemented)

- Only `app` holds the key: the control plane and the supervisor.
- No process creates a key implicitly. `ensure_master_key()` runs at supervisor start (`supervisor.py:223`) and ends in `fresh_install`, `upgrade`, `lost_key_reset`, `verified` or `rotation`; anything else refuses to start with operator guidance (C10). On upgrade, the control plane refuses to start when the key decrypts none of the newest stored values, unless the operator writes the new key's fingerprint prefix to `.master.key.accept-undecryptable` next to the key; that file is removed once used.
- The acknowledgement file and the previous-key file sit next to the key on `koda-state`; with AG-19 they become app-only with it. `CONTROL_PLANE_MASTER_KEY_PREVIOUS` is an `app` env value and never reaches a worker (AG-03).
- The fingerprint outcome audit event is `security.master_key_fingerprint_recorded`.
- ADR-026: the relocation step is superseded; the volume stops being mounted in `runtime-kernel`, the key stays put. Rotation (M5-E6-T11) and backups (M2-E6-T1) are unchanged.

#### 2.5.2 Workers never decrypt (AG-17, AG-17b, AG-22a, AG-10a; checked at AG-19)

- Static granted secrets are still decrypted by the control plane into the spawn env.
- Dynamic needs go through internal broker routes on the existing control-plane API:
  - MCP connection env, and MCP OAuth access tokens (refresh and write-back in the control plane only);
  - the squad peer bot token, for an agent in the token agent's own squad only, which the squad admin check, posting as a peer's bot and a peer bot's username use (C10);
  - the ElevenLabs key and voice catalog, and the image-generation key;
  - CLI credential checkout and check-in (§2.4.4) and approvals (§2.9.2);
  - from AG-17b, chat settings changes and the runtime settings (§2.2.3, §2.2.1);
  - the runtime access a squad turn needs to reach a peer's runtime: `POST /internal/agents/{agent}/runtime-access` (AG-17), for the token's own agent or a member of its squad, which returns that agent's runtime base URL and a short-lived `mutate` request token issued in `app`, so the worker never resolves the runtime-access secret (C10);
  - from AG-22a, the published-spec read for squad peers (§2.2.1).
- The agent-directory reads (`list_agents`, `get_agent`, and `dashboard_service._agent_catalog`, C10) become `agent_directory()` (AG-17). It is a manager read that returns each agent's id, display name, squad and workspace names from `cp_agent_definitions` and the workspace and squad maps. It builds no default-model summary and no draft snapshot and does no seeding, so it touches no secret. It needs no broker route, because the worker already reads those rows and none of them is secret.
- **The property and its owners.** "Workers never decrypt" means that no call a supervised worker can make reaches `decrypt_secret`, `encrypt_secret` or `get_signing_key` (§5.1.4). Each path in C10 names the item that removes it. AG-19 removes the key only after all of them (its Deps), and its acceptance runs the whole-worker check (§5.1.4 (c)), so removing the key breaks no feature.
- **What is the boundary, and what is not.** The reachability check guards that functional property against regressions; it is not a security boundary. Code running in the worker process, such as in-process plugin code (R-3), can import any module in the image, whatever the entrypoint imports, and until AG-19 it can read the key file directly (C10). The boundary is the key's absence from `runtime-kernel` (AG-19). From then on, `decrypt_secret` raises `MasterKeyUnavailable` in every worker (`crypto.py:159-168`), and the ciphertext the DSN can reach is useless without the key. M1-E2-T5 narrows what the DSN can reach.
- **That boundary exists only in compose.** On a native install the control plane, the kernel and the workers run as the operator's uid on one filesystem, and the worker process itself is never confined (L2 confines its children, and no grant lets them near the key, §2.4.2). So in every confinement mode, in-process worker code can read the key file (`STATE_ROOT_DIR/control_plane/.master.key`, `koda/control_plane/settings.py:52-55`), decrypt every secret and forge snapshot and grant MACs. R-3 to R-5 never close on native installs, and the notice says so (§2.8.4).
- Route auth: a per-worker token, minted by the supervisor at spawn, stored as a hash in Postgres (ADR-011), bound to `agent_id`, rotated on respawn, rate-limited; every resolution is audited by id, never value. The token never enters a child env (AG-03), the worker is non-dumpable (AG-04), and L2 denies ptrace and `/proc/<worker>/environ` and `mem` to every confined process (D12-14).
- ADR-025 clarification: "workers resolve secret references at start" means resolution by the control plane, into the spawn env or through the broker.

#### 2.5.3 Snapshot integrity (AG-11)

- Published snapshots are HMAC-signed with the ADR-025 HKDF signing key (M1-E6-T3). The MAC covers `agent_id`, `version`, `published_at` and the SHA-256 of the canonical snapshot JSON (governance block included).
