Granting the worktree's own admin directory and the recorded `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`, only when both lie under the base or the agent's workspace root, keeps the grant inside Koda's boundary; otherwise the environment is `copy` or refused. |
| **Refuse operator commands between AG-14 and AG-15** (S9 read literally for every kernel spawn). | It would remove `/shell`, `/git`, `/pip`, `/npm` and scheduled shell jobs on every host for that window, which waits on M1-E6-T1, and add nothing, because their path does not change until AG-15. They run as today instead (R-11). |
| **Register a kernel spawn after the fork** (REV's step 2 in R8-1: refuse it, or close its lifeline at once, when the incarnation closed meanwhile). | With registration only after the fork, the closer could take the set and spawn the next worker while a forked helper had not yet registered. Revision 10 replaces both this and revision 9's pending entry before the fork with one spawn function, which AG-15 owns: it holds the incarnation lock across the check, the fork and the record of the helper's pid and lifeline (§2.3.5), so no spawn is ever unregistered, no "closed in between" branch exists, and the closer always waits for a spawn in flight. AG-15b only adds its scan's lock and pid record to that function. |
| **Give the unsupervised start a registration path** (REV's alternative in R8-7): it would ask a running `app` for a broker token and a kernel incarnation, bound to a stream it holds open. | Every broker route needs `app`, and where `app` runs, its supervisor already starts the worker with both tokens, so the path would duplicate the supervised start for a development mode outside the boundary. It is OQ-10's declined branch. |
| **SIGKILL a stuck helper directly** (revision 8's `terminate_internal` and sweep). | In `subreaper` mode that orphans every process the helper held (`[V]`, §1.2). SIGCONT and SIGTERM first let a stopped helper end its own spawn, and killing its children before it leaves none behind (§2.3.5). |
| **Refuse consume when the newest candidate's MAC fails** (revision 8). | A row inserted with the DSN and a bad MAC would shadow a valid grant, so every covered call would create a request and a card, with nothing recorded. Skipping it, with an alerting event, keeps the valid grant usable and shows the insert (§2.9.2). |
| **Refuse runtime environments at class M levels** (SEC's fallback in S7-1). | It would take the runtime-environment feature away from the levels that run commands, a §13 change. Binding the kernel's spawns to the worker incarnation is one map in a process that already tracks both workers and tasks. Until AG-15, S9 already treats the kernel path as unconfined, so class M clamps there and nothing new is exposed. |
| **Only a spawn record for each kernel spawn, ended by the next worker's sweep** (REV's alternative in R7-1). | The sweep runs only when the next worker starts, so the old CLI and its commands would run on with the old grants while the new worker replays the task, and governed shell has no home or record. The kernel ends its own spawns before it spawns the next worker; the worker still writes a record for a kernel-started CLI, so the sweep stays the backstop. |
| **Move the unsupervised branch into a module outside worker code** (REV's second option in R7-2). | The closure would then have to skip that module's import in `__main__`, which is the same exemption in another place, and it would move code that (a) already tests. Naming the one edge keeps the rule exact. |
| **Put bootstrap's change in AG-11 or AG-17b** (REV's recommendation in R7-7). | AG-17b changes no behaviour and has no gate, and this changes what an unsupervised start does. AG-11 would work, but AG-21 lands before AG-11 and AG-21b in every order, is gated by OQ-2, which already names the change, and already lists `bootstrap.py:53-56`. |
| **Keep `shell_bg` processes across a worker restart, with their handles in the spawn records** (SEC's alternative in S6-1). | A restarted worker has no task, chat or output buffer for them, and their grants belong to the turn that started them. Ending them with the worker is smaller and keeps invariant 1. |
| **Compute Claude's project directory as the pin does** (SEC's and REV's recommendation in S6-5, R6-5). | Past 200 characters the pin appends a hash of the path that Koda would have to reproduce, and the rule is internal behaviour either way. `CLAUDE_CODE_PROJECT_DIR_NAME`, which the pin honours whenever `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is set, makes the directory one constant, whatever the cwd. The rule is recorded (§1.2) and is A-C-03's positive control. |
| **Consume as one guarded `UPDATE … RETURNING`, with the event in a CTE and a pre-allocated grant id** (REV's second option in R6-2). | It needs the MAC check before the update, without the row held, and a CTE per event. The interactive helper locks, checks and writes in the order §2.9.2 states, and the gate and resolution need that helper anyway. |
| **Add OQ-2 to AG-19's gate** (REV's first option in R6-3). | AG-19 needs only the move of the chat setters and the settings read into `app` (AG-17b), not the publish semantics OQ-2 decides; gating it would hold the key removal, and through OQ-1 M3-E3-T1, on a chat-settings decision. |
| **Write credentials back from shell levels with stronger checks** (an expiry cap, or the account id). | The model writes the whole file, account id included, so a check on its content proves nothing about who wrote it. An expiry cap needs each provider's token lifetime, and validating the token needs a provider call per write-back. Not writing back from shell levels is smaller and complete; the cost is O-4's rotation question. |
| **A layout stop for Gemini's GEMINI.md climb** (an empty `.git` directory in `cwd/` and in the workspaces root). | It also works (`[V]`, §1.2), but it is redundant: `memoryBoundaryMarkers: []` already stops the climb at every level, A-K-05 (3) fails closed on a rename of the key and (6) on a change of its meaning. Creating directories named `.git` where git and other tools look for repositories is a side effect the setting does not have. |
| **Make an unsupervised start fail** (REV's first option in R4-6). | Q-19's adopted default keeps the direct start working; removing it is a §13 decision. It is recorded as R-9, outside the boundary like `off`. |
| **Keep the prompt-input branch of A-K-04 for code-mode models, and a live check in Codex's availability rule.** | No code-mode model is configured, and a live check that the model makes no command proves little, because a model rarely calls a tool it was not offered. The forced calls at the stub prove refusal offline for every configured model. |
| **Escalation as a level raise only.** | A provider switch at the same level adds a shell and egress unconfirmed (SEC-3). |
| **Gate in the API route**, revision 1. | Six internal callers publish the draft without the route (SEC-4). |
| **Keep the contextvar and set it around `execute_tool`** (ADR-009's rejected alternative). | It leaks into tasks created during the call, binds nothing to the command, and keeps five gates. |
| **Worker-verified grants** (an HMAC key in the worker). | The worker could then sign its own grants. Asymmetric keys add a key pair for the same result as one consume route. |
| **Wait for M1-E2-T5 and rely on DB grants only.** | M1-E2-T5 is §13-gated behind M2-E2-T2 and M2-E6-T1. The MAC closes direct DSN inserts once AG-17 and AG-19 ship; the role closes the rest later. |
| **A persisted snapshot high-water mark.** | A restore of an older database would refuse every agent. |
| **`GEMINI_SYSTEM_MD` for Gemini's system instructions.** | It replaces the CLI's whole built-in prompt, including its tool-use instructions. Escaping the delimiters is smaller. |
| **Gemini `plan` mode for `read_only`; Gemini `tools.exclude`.** | `plan` is read-only by mode and reroutes models; a denylist misses tools added in a version bump (SEC-10). |
| **Claude `acceptEdits`/`auto`/`plan` per level.** | Several modes with version-dependent semantics, and `auto` delegates approval to a classifier. `dontAsk` plus explicit lists is one rule. |
| **Gemini native resume.** | Adds session state in the provider home; replay (M3-E5-T1) is already planned. Revisit on cost evidence. |
| **A Koda-hosted MCP bridge** exposing governed tools natively. | Deferred: an inbound capability socket inside the sandbox and a second tool protocol. Re-evaluate if the M0-E3-T2 eval at `read_only` exceeds the AG-27 budget. |
| **bwrap or a container per execution; uid split.** | User-namespace and seccomp relaxations; uid split stays a fallback only if AG-08 shows Docker's seccomp blocks Landlock. |
| **Provider sandboxes as the boundary; native approval bridging; status quo.** | Per-provider semantics; a hidden flag and a Koda-hosted server; rejected by ADR-002. |

## 4. Consequences

**Positive**
- One authority, one field, one resolver, one delivery channel (execution and model policy), one publish gate, one approval store.
- Child runs, artifact-gap turns, dry runs and internal calls become restricted on every provider; internal calls run with no tools at all, and Codex never runs `danger-full-access` outside L2. No level is weaker than today in any confinement mode.
- Provider switches cannot add a shell or egress without confirmation, and chat toggles cannot publish operator drafts. Workers never publish or sign, so the snapshot MAC holds against them once the key leaves their reach.
- Workspace content cannot configure, extend or redirect a provider CLI.
- Policy-approved governed shell and git can run, bound per call; dashboard approvals work across processes.
- The key and the bootstrap code leave every model-reachable container, and workers stop decrypting.
- Operators can trace who granted what.
- Concurrent turns of one agent cannot read or change each other's instructions, memory context or settings, nothing a CLI writes in its home outlives the run, and no process the model starts outlives its spawn, whether the worker or the kernel started that spawn, except on the hosts R-10 names.

**Negative and costs**
- Coding agents slow down until an operator opts them in; each governed round costs a resume, and Codex and Gemini follow-ups are replayed rather than resumed.
- Under the default confinement Codex has two levels, `none` and `workspace_exec`; a Codex agent at the default level answers without reading the workspace (OQ-6).
- More approvals. Workspace customisation stops loading (OQ-3), except Gemini's JIT GEMINI.md (R-7).
- Every published chat settings change restarts the agent's worker, as the voice toggle already does: a reply in progress is cut after 15 s and re-queued (C17), its spawns end with the worker, the CLI and every process the model started through it, the ones the kernel started for that worker included (§2.3.5), and from M3-E3-T2 on pending approvals expire (§2.9.2).
- A process the model starts, a background server for example, stops when its spawn ends, and every `shell_bg` process stops when the worker restarts (OQ-8).
- Operator commands (`/shell`, `/git`, `/pip`, `/npm`), operator-authored scheduled shell jobs and the runtime environment's terminal run under the class M profile from AG-15, and `/git` and the kernel's own git and tar run hardened from AG-03: global packages, git over SSH, writes outside the work dir, the operator's global git configuration and repository hooks stop working there, and an open terminal closes when the agent restarts (OQ-9). With OQ-11 (a), a scheduled shell job whose command or work dir the model wrote is a governed agent call, confined by the agent's policy and S9 (§2.9.1); such a job stops running where the policy does not allow shell without approval.
- Below Landlock ABI 6 (kernel 6.12), a shell-level turn of one agent that runs `kill -9 -1` interrupts every other agent's turn, and repeated can move their queued work to the DLQ (R-12); this is unchanged from today, where agent commands run unconfined.
- A worker started without the supervisor loses governed and operator shell and runtime environments from AG-15, and the broker-backed features from AG-17 (OQ-10).
- Codex, governed shell and git need Landlock (kernel ≥ 5.13; ≥ 6.7 for `strict`) for anything beyond `none`.
- One new volume, which backups must include. CLIs must be upgraded deliberately; each bump re-runs the contract test, the feature review, the offline probes and the live checks.
- Every spawn starts from an empty home, so Codex and Gemini lose their local caches between runs, and Claude's transcript is copied in and out on every resumed turn.
- A file-OAuth credential refreshed during a shell-level spawn is not written back (§2.4.4); on a provider that rotates refresh tokens this can log the provider out (O-4, OQ-4 (c)).
- Gemini at `read_only` and above still loads the workspace GEMINI.md (R-7) and follows a symlinked one (R-1).

**Deleted**
- `permission_mode` and `dry_run` from `QueryContext` (`queue_manager.py:630` and its uses), `llm_runner` and every runner.
- `claude_runner._effective_permission_mode` (`:92-96`) and the duplicate argv builder; `gemini_runner._effective_approval_mode` (`:86-96`); the Codex dry-run and resume-in-danger branches (`codex_runner.py:259-260, 283-286, 312-330`).
- The reads of `CODEX_SANDBOX`, `CODEX_APPROVAL_POLICY` and their validator (the stored values in AG-32); `AGENT_EXECUTION_POLICY_JSON` and its readers.
- The `_execution_approved` contextvar, the in-memory approval stores and the approval JSON fallbacks.
- The worker-side decrypt paths (C10); the draft reads (`agent_settings.py:232-236`, `skills/_runtime.py`); the `/var/lib` prefix rule and the HOME fallback for work dirs; the `/tmp` work dir of the memory extractor.
- The unused `sandbox_policy` gates (TD-037) and the bwrap backend; the `KODA_AGENT_DEFAULT_*` claims in `docs/operations/hardening.md`; the "plan" mode concept.

---

## 5. Verification and test plan

**G** is the full suite (ruff, ruff format, mypy, pytest with coverage, the pnpm web checks); **G-RS** adds cargo fmt, clippy and test; **G-PG** runs on Postgres; **G-CI** is the docker smoke.

### 5.1 Cross-cutting automated checks

1. Resolver (AG-20): a table test over level × ceiling × provider × confinement × Codex `IP` result per profile, and a monotonicity property test (adding a ceiling never raises the level or the capability).
2. An AST spawn guard over all of `koda/`: `subprocess.*`, `asyncio.create_subprocess_*`, `os.system`, `os.spawn*` and `os.posix_spawn` go through `process_confinement` or appear on the class T allowlist with a reason (AG-14).
3. "No secret reaches any child": CLIs, governed shell, git, MCP stdio, media tools, chromium; HOME, `XDG_*` and `TMPDIR` point at the child's own directories (AG-03).
4. Workers never decrypt (`tests/test_control_plane/test_worker_never_decrypts.py`; §2.5.2):
   - (a) Supervised env step (AG-17): with `AGENT_ID` and `_KODA_RUNTIME_BOOTSTRAPPED=1` set, after `koda/__main__.py`'s env step `sys.modules` holds none of `koda.control_plane.bootstrap`, `koda.control_plane.manager` and `koda.control_plane.crypto`. Without `_KODA_RUNTIME_BOOTSTRAPPED`, the step imports `bootstrap` and logs `unsupervised_start_outside_boundary` (R-9).
   - (b) Per path: each item's test shows that the modules it changes no longer make the C10 calls it owns. AG-17 owns MCP env and OAuth, the squad peer bot tokens, squad runtime-HTTP dispatch, ElevenLabs, image generation and the agent-directory reads; AG-17b the runtime settings and chat setters; AG-22a the squad spec reads; AG-10a the skills fallback. AG-17's dispatch test runs squad runtime-HTTP dispatch with no key file and `CONTROL_PLANE_MASTER_KEY` unset: the turn reaches the peer's runtime through the runtime-access route, and the message-bus fallback (`dispatch.py:592-604`) is not taken.
   - (c) Whole worker (AG-19's acceptance, M1-E2-T3's done-when): a static reachability check over one call graph of every module in `koda/` (revision 7; revision 6's version followed only the manager's `self.<method>` calls, and missed calls through objects built from the manager).
     - **Nodes and edges.** A node is a function or a method. An edge is a call to, or a reference of, a koda function, method or class; a reference counts because a bound method passed as an argument runs too (`asyncio.to_thread(manager.send_dashboard_squad_message, …)`, `dispatch.py:453-455`). Names resolve through the module's imports, imports inside function bodies included; through `self` and `cls`, with koda base classes; and through a receiver of known koda type: a name bound to a constructor call, to a call of a function whose return annotation names a koda class (`get_control_plane_manager() -> ControlPlaneManager`, `manager.py:15064`), or to such a name; a parameter annotated with a koda class; a chained constructor call (`ControlPlaneRuntimeAccessBroker(self).get_runtime_access`, `manager.py:12966`); a `self.<attribute>` assigned from a typed value. Parameter types also flow: an argument of known type, `self` included, types the callee's parameter, iterated to a fixpoint, so a module function that receives the manager and calls back into it is followed. A class reference adds its `__init__`.
     - **Targets.** `decrypt_secret`, `encrypt_secret` and `get_signing_key` in `koda/control_plane/crypto.py`. At dfa596c 20 functions in 5 modules call them directly, `runtime_access.py:66-79` and `mcp_connection_broker.py` among them, and 435 functions reach them, among them 133 of the manager's 457 methods, with `get_runtime_access`, `send_dashboard_squad_message`, `discover_mcp_capabilities` and `get_mcp_capability_snapshot`, which revision 6's probe missed.
     - **Worker code.** The modules in the static import closure of `koda/__main__.py`: every import statement is followed, lazy ones included, and the package `__init__` is read as AG-17 leaves it. The unsupervised branch's `bootstrap` import is not followed ((a) proves the supervised branch never takes it), nor is the `migrate` subcommand. The manager and `crypto` are cut points: the worker imports the manager for reads, so its functions are in the graph but are not worker code, and the closure does not follow the manager's imports. 283 modules at dfa596c.
     - **It fails** on any edge from a function defined in worker code to a function outside worker code that reaches a target, or to a target itself, with **one exemption** (revision 8): the call of `apply_runtime_env_from_control_plane` in `__main__.main` (`koda/__main__.py:53-57`), which is the unsupervised branch of R-9 and, from AG-17, runs only when `_KODA_RUNTIME_BOOTSTRAPPED` is unset, as (a) tests. The exemption names that one edge (`koda.__main__:main` → `koda.control_plane.bootstrap:apply_runtime_env_from_control_plane`), and any other edge into `bootstrap` fails. At dfa596c the check reports 39 edges in 17 modules, and 38 in 16 apart from the exempt one: exactly the calls C10 lists. Nothing else is exempt: a module the worker imports for one harmless function still fails if another function in it decrypts, and the remedy is to change that call, as AG-17 does for `dashboard_service.py`.
     - **Receivers it cannot type.** Every module a worker imports is worker code, so an object of a class outside worker code can reach a worker only from a cut module or a module a cut module imports. An attribute reference whose receiver has no known type therefore fails when its name is that of a reaching method of a class defined in one of those modules; the remedy is a type annotation. At dfa596c there is none. (Matching untyped names against every reaching method instead would flag 102 references to generic names such as `run`, `resolve` and `start`.)
     - A prototype of this check, run at dfa596c, produced the numbers above.
   - It does not assert that the whole worker's import closure excludes `crypto` (§3).
5. Evaluator parity vectors through the API and the runtime (AG-06).
6. Snapshot tamper, cross-agent replay and rollback are refused; unsigned versions are re-signed once (AG-11).
7. The key-fingerprint matrix (AG-05, implemented: `tests/integration/test_master_key_fingerprint_postgres.py`, `tests/test_control_plane_master_key.py`).
8. Migrations run twice on G-PG (AG-18, AG-21, AG-11).
9. Linux integration for `koda-sandbox-exec`: reads outside the allowlist denied; TCP connects to 5432, 8090 and 50061 denied (ABI ≥ 4); a UDP send and a connect to an internal listener on 443 recorded as allowed (R-6); `environ` of a sibling or parent denied; a signal to the worker denied (ABI ≥ 6); nesting cannot widen; exit 126 when enforcement is incomplete; a workspace grant of `/tmp`, HOME or a state root refused (AG-08, AG-12); a process in spawn A's provider profile cannot write spawn B's home of the same agent, the Claude session store or `spawn-records/` (AG-14). Lifetime (AG-08), with a test command that starts a child in its own session and a `setsid` grandchild that would write a file after 5 s, in each mode the runner supports (`pid_namespace`, `cgroup`, `subreaper`, forced by a test flag): no descendant remains and no file is written after the command exits, after SIGTERM to the helper, and after the lifeline's write end closes; after SIGKILL of the helper, none remains in `pid_namespace`, nor in `cgroup` once the parent writes `cgroup.kill`, and in `subreaper` the survivors are recorded as R-10's positive control; the confined command cannot write the cgroup filesystem and, at ABI ≥ 6, cannot signal its helper; the command's uid inside the namespace is its own; the helper has one thread when it forks init; an orphan of the live command (a `setsid` process whose shell has returned) has the helper as its parent in `cgroup` and `subreaper`, and init in `pid_namespace` (revision 9). In `pid_namespace` (revision 8): `/proc/self` and `/proc/<getpid()>` are the command itself, `pgrep -P` finds the command's own child and `pkill` ends it, and neither the worker's nor the kernel's pid is listed. In `cgroup` (revision 8): the spawn cgroup is `ws_<workspace>/spawn_<spawn id>` inside the helper's own workspace cgroup; with `pids.max` set on the workspace, a command that forks past it gets `EAGAIN`; and a record whose cgroup path is anything else (another workspace, the root, a path with `..`) is neither killed nor removed. The self-test reports the mode the host supports, and skips `pid_namespace` where the procfs mount is refused.
10. Publish gate, `tests/test_control_plane/test_publish_gate.py`: one named test per caller of §2.2.3 (AG-21; the chat rows from AG-21b). In each test where an unpublished draft holds a rule that allows `shell_execute`, the rule must stay unpublished unless the actor is an operator.
    - Publish route: `test_operator_publish_needs_confirmation_on_escalation` (refused without `confirm_escalation`, published with it).
    - Onboarding: `test_onboarding_publish_is_an_operator_publish`.
    - Supervisor reconcile: `test_system_first_publish_uses_secure_policies` (a first publish of a draft with a shell rule publishes the secure-default execution and tool policies); `test_system_publish_refused_when_versions_exist` (with `desired_version` reset to 0 on a published agent: refused with `system_publish_of_published_agent`, no new version, the agent not spawned).
    - Runtime-snapshot fallback: `test_missing_version_row_publishes_nothing` (with the row of `desired_version` deleted, `build_runtime_snapshot` raises and `cp_agent_config_versions` gains no row; S3-4).
    - Bootstrap: `test_bootstrap_never_publishes_from_a_worker` (from AG-21: an unsupervised start of a never-published agent fails with `agent_not_published` and `publish_agent` is never called; a published agent starts).
    - Legacy import: `test_legacy_import_publishes_at_read_only`.
    - Voice toggle: `test_toggle_with_pending_draft_publishes_only_its_change`.
    - Voice default: `test_voice_default_publishes_only_voice_keys` (`voice_policy.mode`, the four `providers` voice keys and `functional_defaults.audio` are published; nothing else is).
    - ElevenLabs model: `test_audio_model_publishes_only_its_functional_default`.
    - `/provider`, `/model`, functional defaults: A-P-04; `test_adding_codex_to_workspace_write_agent_needs_confirmation`; `test_adding_codex_to_read_only_agent_is_not_escalation`.
    - Natural-language settings: `test_natural_language_settings_use_the_route` (a plain message "mude o provedor para codex" reaches the route through the setter, never `publish_agent` in the worker, with the same outcomes as `/provider`).
    - Settings-menu buttons: `test_settings_menu_buttons_use_the_route` (`callback_model`, `callback_provider` and `callback_feature_model_model` reach the route through their setters, never `publish_agent` in the worker; on `needs_confirmation` the session's provider, model and `auto_model` are unchanged).
    - Settings route (AG-17b): `test_settings_route_checks_path_user_and_agent` (a path outside the closed list gives 400, a Telegram user outside the agent's allowed users gives 403, and agent B's token can change only agent B).
    - AG-21 migration: `test_block_less_version_reads_as_read_only` (a version without a governance block gives level `read_only` and ceiling `capability(its providers, read_only)` to the gate, S10 and the resolver; an operator publish of `workspace_exec` over it needs confirmation); `test_migration_failure_blocks_no_publish` (with the migration failing for agent A, A's chat and operator publishes still work, and health shows `governance_migration_failed:A`); the migration run twice on G-PG.
    - AG-11 re-signing: `test_resign_publishes_last_published_unchanged` (a pending draft is not published); `test_resign_failure_reports_and_retries` (a failing re-sign leaves `snapshot_unsigned:<agent>`, the agent unspawned, and succeeds on the next reconcile pass).
    - Invalid draft: `test_invalid_draft_blocks_only_operator_publishes` (with a draft that fails `publish_checks`, for example an enabled general provider whose CLI is missing: the AG-21 migration, the AG-11 re-sign and a voice toggle publish, and an operator publish is refused naming the error).
    - Concurrent publishes (G-PG): `test_operator_lowering_and_voice_toggle_keep_the_lowered_level` (an operator publish that lowers the level and a voice toggle run concurrently, in both orders: the second's insert loses on `UNIQUE (agent_id, version)`, re-reads, and applies its change to the first's version, and the final version has the lowered level and the toggled voice); `test_publish_conflict_after_one_retry` (a publish that loses twice fails with `publish_conflict` and writes no row, no apply operation and no event; an operator escalation whose added capabilities changed between the attempts fails the same way); `test_gate_does_not_wait_on_the_draft_build` (a draft whose build rewrites `runtime_endpoint_json` publishes without waiting).
    - Clone: `test_cloned_exec_policy_needs_confirmation`.

### 5.2 Named tests

**Argv tests that replace M1-E2-T2's "per-runner argv unit tests"** (AG-22 to AG-25). Each runs the builder and `validate_args`; none calls a model. The table also holds three publish tests, all under M1-E2-T2: A-P-04 and A-P-06 belong to AG-21b, and A-P-07 to AG-11; the provider-home tests A-H-01 (the race) and A-H-02 (homes and records after a killed worker), which belong to AG-13a; the lifetime tests A-L-01 and A-L-02, which belong to the lifetime parts of AG-14 and AG-15; and the operator-command test A-O-01, split into (a) to (d) by owning item: (a) `/git` hardening belongs to AG-03 (OQ-9 (a)), (b) the class M profile to AG-15 (OQ-9 (b)), (c) the terminal and `nohup` end to OQ-8 with OQ-9 (c), and (d) a model-written shell job to AG-14 and AG-15 with OQ-11.

|---|---|---|

Each internal call site (memory extractor, squad planner) runs at `none` with a fresh empty work dir and `TMPDIR` under the task tmp, never `/tmp`, HOME or `DEFAULT_WORK_DIR`; the extractor on its `MEMORY_EXTRACTION_PROVIDER` chain and the planner on the coordinator's **published** model policy; both pass S10. |

At `none` there must be no `exec_command`, `write_stdin`, `view_image` or `collaboration.*` tool, no skills block, and none of the planted workspace `AGENTS.md`, `.agents/skills` or `.codex/skills` canaries; at shell levels `exec_command` must appear. On 0.169.0 at `none`, `gpt-5.5` shows `apply_patch` and `request_user_input`, and the other six configured models show `request_user_input` only. (b) **Refused:** one run per forced call, with the stub answering the first request with that call: `exec_command`, `write_stdin`, `shell`, `shell_command`, `local_shell_call`, `view_image`, and `apply_patch` as a custom tool call and as a function call, adding a file inside and outside the workspace. At `none` the follow-up request must carry a refusal (`unsupported call`, `unsupported custom tool call`, `patch rejected: …`, `aborted`) or there is no follow-up (`local_shell_call`), no file may appear, the outside canary may reach no request, and the tool list may not change. At the shell level, the forced `exec_command`, and `apply_patch` where the model offers it, must run, as the positive control. The result is the fixture `tests/fixtures/provider_cli/codex-0.169.0-tools.json`. A model whose request carries no `tools` array (the code-mode models), or a configured model without a recorded (a) and (b), is `codex_none_unproven`, and Codex is unavailable for it below the shell levels. |
| A-K-05 | `test_gemini_user_settings_load_probe` | Offline, with no credentials, at each worker start per CLI fingerprint. (1) Each exact settings file Koda writes (the four levels × each `security.auth.selectedType` value Koda writes) loads with no warning; their SHA-256 values are the only ones Koda may write. With the `read_only` file, `--approval-mode=yolo` exits with the `disableYolo` error before auth, so the file is read. (2) With the `workspace_exec` settings and a workspace `.gemini/settings.json` setting `disableYoloMode=true`, passed through `--include-directories`, `yolo` is not refused, so the workspace file is not read. (3) Per-key probe: each key of the §2.3.2 user settings except `telemetry.*`, `context.memoryBoundaryMarkers` and `context.includeDirectoryTree` included, written alone with the wrong type, must print `Error in: <key>`; a key that stops erroring fails the probe, and Gemini is unavailable. `telemetry.*` is left out, because the strict `telemetry` object prints the same error for a wrong type and an unknown key; the env pins are its control, and (1) catches a rename. (4) With a `NODE_OPTIONS` preload that records the relaunched CLI's environment, an unpinned canary (`KODA_CANARY`, `CODE_ASSIST_ENDPOINT`, `GOOGLE_GENAI_USE_VERTEXAI`) plus `GEMINI_CLI_HOME` and `GEMINI_CLI_SYSTEM_SETTINGS_PATH`, planted in a `.gemini/.env` in the workspace, in the spawn home and in each ancestor of the cwd up to the test root, must all be absent or keep their pinned values. Positive control: with the stop file removed and no canary in the spawn home, the nearest ancestor's canary must appear (with a canary in the spawn home, which is the cwd's parent, that one appears first). (5) With the system paths pinned, no `Security Warning` is printed. (6) Startup context, against a local gateway stub on `127.0.0.1` with a dummy key (§1.2), reading the first request: GEMINI.md canaries in the workspace root, a workspace subdirectory, the spawn home, every ancestor of the cwd up to the test root, and a `.git` in the test root; the workspace GEMINI.md also `@`-imports the spawn home's credential file by absolute path, relative path and symlink. At `none` (no `--include-directories`) the request has no tools, no canary and no directory tree. At every level no ancestor or spawn-home canary and no credential appears, at startup and in the follow-up request after the stub forces a `read_file` of a file two directories below the workspace root (JIT). These are fail-closed: a pin that shows any of them, at any level, makes Gemini unavailable (`gemini_startup_context_unproven` in health). At `read_only` and above the workspace-root canary appears at startup and the two subdirectory canaries in the JIT response (R-7), and with the workspace GEMINI.md a symlink to `/proc/self/environ`, `GEMINI_API_KEY` appears (R-1); these two are recorded per pin, and a pin whose result differs is reported for review. Positive control: with `memoryBoundaryMarkers` left out of the settings, the spawn home's and the ancestors' canaries appear at `none` and the ancestors' canaries and the credential at `read_only`. |
| A-H-01 | `test_concurrent_spawns_of_one_agent_are_isolated` (`tests/test_services/test_provider_homes.py`, AG-13a) | Two spawns of one agent run concurrently through the real home builder with a fake CLI that records its env and reads its configuration at start and again after a barrier both reach: Codex at `none` with developer instructions and memory context A, and Codex at `workspace_exec` with B; Gemini at `none` and at `read_only`. Each sees only its own instructions, settings, `tools.core` and cwd at both reads; the two homes differ and neither contains the other; each home is gone after its run; the session store and the login home gain only the named files (§2.3.5); a credential file refreshed by two concurrent `read_only` spawns ends as the newer copy. Write-back cases (§2.4.4): the `workspace_exec` spawn's fake CLI rewrites its credential copy with a later expiry, and in a second run with another account id, and the canonical copy is unchanged both times, with `provider.credential_writeback_refused`; a `read_only` spawn whose copy fails the schema check (an unknown key, the size limit) leaves it unchanged too. Link cases (§2.3.5): the `workspace_exec` spawn replaces its transcript path and its credential file with links to a file outside the home, and nothing is copied out. |
| A-H-02 | `test_killed_worker_leaves_no_spawn_home` (same file, AG-13a) | A worker runs a spawn through the real helper. Its fake CLI has refreshed its credential copy and written a transcript, and has started a child in its own session and a `setsid` grandchild, each of which writes a file in the workspace if it is still alive after 5 s. The record exists before the CLI starts. The worker is SIGKILLed with its process group, as the kernel does after the 15 s grace (C17): the CLI, the child and the grandchild are gone and no file appears (the lifeline closed), in each lifetime mode the runner supports. The next worker start, with a new incarnation, removes the home and the record and copies nothing out: the session store and the canonical credential are unchanged. A record whose helper still runs with the recorded start time (the test holds one stopped, and in a second case one that ignores SIGTERM) is stopped as §2.3.5 says (SIGCONT and SIGTERM, a 5 s wait, and in `subreaper` its children before it), and its descendants end with it in every mode the runner supports, `subreaper` included; a record whose pid now belongs to another process (a different start time) kills nothing; a record without a pid (the worker died between the start and the second write) leaves no process behind. A home of the current incarnation that no live spawn owns is removed when the next spawn starts. |
| A-L-01 | `test_model_started_processes_end_with_the_spawn` (`tests/security/test_spawn_lifetime.py`, AG-14) | Through `process_confinement`, for each worker-side spawn kind (a fake provider CLI, `shell_bg`, git through `cli_runner`; governed shell is a kernel spawn, A-L-02) and each lifetime mode the runner supports, the command starts a process in its own session and a `setsid` grandchild, each of which would write a canary file after 5 s. After the command exits normally, after a timeout (SIGTERM to the helper), after a `shell_bg` kill, and after the worker exits (the lifeline), no descendant remains and no canary is written. A `shell_bg` process still running when the worker exits ends with it. The spawn event records the mode. With `subreaper` and the helper SIGKILLed, the survivors are recorded and `provider.spawn_helper_lost` is written (R-10's positive control); with `pid_namespace` or `cgroup`, nothing survives that either. In each mode, with AG-15b running where it applies, a `setsid` process left by a shell command that has returned stays alive while the spawn runs and is gone once it ends, and AG-15b ends nothing of a live spawn (revision 9). In `pid_namespace`, the command's `pgrep -P` finds its own child and `pkill` ends it, and `/proc/<getpid()>` is the command itself; and a pinned Gemini `workspace_exec` run at the gateway stub, whose forced `run_shell_command` starts a `setsid` process and is cancelled by a short `tools.shell.inactivityTimeout`, leaves no descendant once Gemini has cancelled it, while the spawn still runs (§1.2). |
| A-L-02 | `test_kernel_started_processes_end_with_their_worker` (`tests/security/test_spawn_lifetime.py`, with the kernel's Rust integration tests; AG-15) | With `RUNTIME_ENVIRONMENTS_ENABLED=true`, a fake provider CLI started through `StartTask`, and a governed shell command run through `ExecuteCommand`, each start a process in its own session and a `setsid` grandchild that would write a canary after 5 s. After the kernel terminates the worker on a version change (`ensure` with a new version), after the worker is SIGKILLed mid-turn (the reaper path), after `TerminateTask`, and after the `ExecuteCommand` ends or times out, no descendant remains and no canary is written; on a version change the kernel spawns the next worker only after they are gone. A request with the old incarnation's revoked token starts nothing. The next worker's sweep finds the CLI's record with no live helper, and only then removes its home. Revision 9 adds, with revision 10's one-spawn-function model: a `StartTask` and an `ExecuteCommand` issued during the 15 s grace succeed and end with the worker; with a fault-injection hook, a `StartTask` paused inside the one spawn function (holding the incarnation lock, between the fork and the pid record) while the worker is SIGKILLed makes closure block on that lock until the pid and lifeline are recorded, so it leaves no process and no removed-but-busy home when the next worker starts, which starts only after every recorded spawn has resolved; in `cgroup` with signal scoping off, an `ExecuteCommand` whose command SIGKILLs its helper leaves no descendant, and `provider.spawn_helper_lost` is written; in `subreaper`, a helper stuck at `terminate_internal` is stopped with its children first and leaves no descendant; a kernel restarted while a spawn cgroup is populated kills it before its first worker. Revision 11 adds: two installs that run an agent with the same id, each with its own `KODA_CGROUP_ROOT`, `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT` and `RUNTIME_KERNEL_ROOT` (R-13's configuration), neither kill each other's spawn cgroups at start nor sweep each other's records; a worker SIGKILLed during a `RestoreCheckpoint`, and in a second run during a worktree creation, leaves the restore and the worktree complete, with no `.git/index.lock` and no ref or worktree lock in the workspace or the common directory (§7.9). Each lifetime mode the runner supports is covered. |
| A-O-01 | `test_operator_commands_run_under_the_class_m_profile` (`tests/security/test_operator_commands.py`, with the kernel's Rust integration tests; AG-15, OQ-9), split into (a) to (d) by owning item | **(a)** (OQ-9 (a), AG-03): `/git status` and `/git diff` in a repository with a planted `core.fsmonitor`, `post-checkout` hook and filter driver run none of them, a config key outside the allowlist refuses `/git` naming the key, and a commit that triggers auto-gc leaves no `gc.log.lock` and no detached process; the kernel's worktree creation and checkpoint run the same way. **(b)** (OQ-9 (b), AG-15): under L2, `/shell`, `/pip`, `/npm`, an operator-authored scheduled shell job and an operator terminal, each run through the real handler and the kernel, write a file in the work dir and fail to write outside it, to read HOME and to connect to a port outside `RUNTIME_CONFINEMENT_EGRESS_PORTS`; a work dir under `RUNTIME_KERNEL_ROOT` that is not the task's recorded environment, or under another agent's workspace root, is refused; before AG-15 (a kernel without the helper) the same commands run as today. **(c)** (OQ-9 (b) and (c), with OQ-8; it leaves with a declined (b), revision 12): a `nohup … &` child of each ends when its command ends, a terminal's when the terminal closes, and an open terminal closes when the agent's worker restarts. **(d)** (OQ-11, AG-14 and AG-15 via S9): a shell job the model created through `job_create`, and an operator's `/cron` job whose command or work dir the model then changed through `job_update`, each with a read-only-classified command that would run a planted workspace helper, are `model`-authored, refused between AG-14 and AG-15 where the class M layers are missing, and from AG-15 run confined under the agent's class M profile, never on the unconfined `scheduled_job` path; under a `require_approval` rule, and under the secure default, the run is refused at fire time and the chat is told; a model `job_update` that changes only the schedule, and a model `job_run_now` of an operator job, leave it `operator`-authored; an existing job at upgrade gets the class OQ-11 (b) gives it. |

| D11-06 | `test_authorization_expires` | Use after `expires_at` is refused. |

A `git_commit` that triggers auto-gc (many loose objects, `gc.auto` low) leaves no `.git/gc.log.lock` and no detached gc process, because the spawn carries `-c gc.autoDetach=false` (revision 10). In a repository whose unreachable loose objects are too young to prune, two `git_commit` calls in a row are each recorded and each return within the tool's timeout, although each reruns the foreground gc (revision 11, §2.9.1). |
| D11-14 | `test_governed_writes_refuse_dot_git` | `file_write`, `file_edit` and the other governed write tools refuse any path with a `.git` component, through symlinks too. |

|---|---|---|

| D12-05 | `test_cross_agent_grant_is_refused` | Agent B's token cannot consume agent A's grant. |
| D12-06 | `test_pending_requests_expire_on_restart` | A new worker incarnation expires the old pending rows, including a restart caused by a published chat settings change (moved here from A-P-06); the restart does not crash (D-163). |
| D12-07 | `test_grants_survive_restart_until_expiry` | A scope grant still works after a restart, and not after `expires_at`. |

| D12-13 | `test_existing_scope_grant_is_consumed_without_a_request` | A call covered by a scope grant (same scope fingerprint, session and chat) consumes it with no request created and no card shown, `uses` increments, and once `max_uses` or `expires_at` is reached a request is created. With an exhausted grant (`uses = max_uses`) and an expired one beside a fresh grant of the same scope fingerprint, session and chat, in either creation order, the fresh one is consumed and no request is created. With a newer row of the same scope fingerprint, session and chat whose MAC is wrong, inserted with the DSN beside a valid grant, the valid grant is consumed, no request is created, the bad row's `uses` is unchanged, and `security.approval_grant_mac_invalid` is written. |
| D12-14 | `test_broker_token_unreachable_from_class_m` (Linux integration, AG-14) | A class M process spawned the way the worker spawns cannot read the broker token from its env, `/proc/<worker>/environ` or `/proc/<worker>/mem`. |

### 5.3 Live provider checks

Credentials required; gated by `tests/_provider_plugin.py`; mocked tests are never proof (MISSION §7.6). Run as the image's uid (`botuser`). Per provider × level:

- a write at `read_only` leaves no file; `.git/` writes are refused below `workspace_exec`; a symlink out of the workspace is refused;
- the file tools refuse `/proc/self/environ`, `/proc/<ppid>/environ` and the provider-home credential files at every level below `workspace_exec` (R-1);
- canaries are ignored:
  - Claude: `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/settings.json` with a hook, `.claude/skills`, `.claude/agents`, `.claude/commands`, `.mcp.json`, in the workspace and the home; auto-memory does not persist across turns;
  - Codex: the workspace `AGENTS.md`, `$CODEX_HOME/AGENTS.md` (planted in one spawn home, absent from the next), `.codex/config.toml`, `rules/`, `.agents/skills` in the workspace and in HOME, `.codex/skills`, `$CODEX_HOME/skills`;
  - Gemini: a workspace `.gemini/settings.json` with a hook, an MCP server, a discovery command, `context.includeDirectories` and telemetry. A `.gemini/.env` and a `.env` in the workspace, in every ancestor of the cwd (on a native install `~` too), and in the Gemini home, each setting the pinned `GEMINI_SYSTEM_MD`, `GOOGLE_GEMINI_BASE_URL` and `GEMINI_TELEMETRY_*` and the unpinned `CODE_ASSIST_ENDPOINT`, which points at a local listener: the listener receives no request, and the run uses the configured endpoint. A system settings file at the default path `/etc/gemini-cli/settings.json`, root-owned and setting `tools.core` wider: ignored. `.gemini/extensions`. GEMINI.md canaries in the workspace root, a subdirectory, the spawn home and every ancestor of the cwd, with a `.git` above them: at `none` none of them and no workspace content reach the model; at `read_only` and above no ancestor or spawn-home canary does, while the workspace-root GEMINI.md and one beside a file that `read_file` reads are recorded as R-7 behaviour, and a workspace GEMINI.md symlinked to the credential file as R-1 behaviour;
  - Claude transcripts (R-7): a `workspace_exec` turn whose Bash appends a forged turn to its own session transcript; the next `--resume` turn, at `read_only`, shows the forged text in its context (recorded as R-7) and still has only the `read_only` tools;
  - host-level configuration (O-10): a canary `/etc/claude-code/managed-settings.json` with a hook, and `/etc/codex/config.toml` and `managed_config.toml` enabling a disabled feature; AG-23 and AG-24 record whether the pinned path or flag excludes each;
- a hook or config planted in a spawn home never reaches `app` through check-in, and never reaches a later spawn;
- a `workspace_exec` turn whose shell replaces `projects/koda/<session>.jsonl` and the credential file in its own spawn home with links to a canary outside the home: the copy-out reads and writes nothing outside the spawn home and the store, the canary never reaches the store or the login home, and `provider.spawn_copy_refused` is recorded;
- a `workspace_exec` turn whose shell rewrites its credential copy with a later expiry: the canonical copy is unchanged, the next spawn gets the canonical copy, and `provider.credential_writeback_refused` is recorded;
- a worker restarted by a published chat change while a Codex `workspace_exec` turn, and in a second run a Claude one, runs a long command that has also started a `setsid` process, each writing a canary file when it finishes: after the restart the CLI, the long command and the `setsid` process are gone, no canary file appears, and the spawn home is gone; the same turn left to end normally leaves no `setsid` process behind; the run records the lifetime mode; the Codex run is repeated once with `RUNTIME_ENVIRONMENTS_ENABLED=true`, so that the CLI is a kernel `StartTask` (AG-15), with the same result;
- concurrent spawns of one agent under L2 (A-H-01, live): a Codex `workspace_exec` turn whose shell tries to write a `config.toml`, a `settings.json` and a hook into the home of a concurrent turn of the same agent, and into the Claude session store, while a Codex `none` turn and a Gemini `none` turn start: every write fails, and each `none` turn runs with only its own instructions and settings;
- Codex, **per configured model** (`CODEX_AVAILABLE_MODELS`): at `none`, a request to run `touch <canary>` and report the result produces no `command_execution` (or `exec_command`) item in the `--json` event stream and no file, while the same request at `workspace_exec` produces one, as a positive control. The result is recorded per pin and model as evidence that the real provider behaves like the stub; availability rests on A-K-04's offline proof. At `none`, an `apply_patch` attempt leaves no file, under `off` and under L2. Under `off`, `workspace-write` writes only the workspace and the task tmp and blocks network unless enabled. Under L2, `workspace_exec` runs and stays inside the L2 profile;
- Gemini: only the `tools.core` tools are offered; `yolo` fails below `workspace_exec`; the prompt is read from stdin; the run produces no settings warning; it runs from its 0500 cwd as `botuser`, and `write_file` into that cwd fails;
- `--append-system-prompt-file` and `--safe-mode` work with subscription auth;
- OAuth refresh holds over repeated cycles with two spawns of one agent, and two agents, running at once, each on its own credential copy (O-4).

### 5.4 Docker checks (G-CI docker-smoke; evidence in VALIDATION_REPORT.md)

In the runtime-kernel image, a supervised worker that calls `decrypt_secret` gets `MasterKeyUnavailable` (the boundary of §2.5.2), and a process spawned the way the worker spawns: does not see `/var/lib/koda/state`; cannot read worker-state, another agent's workspace or home, another spawn's home, the Claude session store, the login homes or `/app` (with OQ-12 (a) or (b) approved; otherwise R-14), with the default work dir and with a work dir the model set itself (`agent_set_workdir` to another agent's workspace, to `/var/lib/koda/runtime/snapshots` or to a runtime task directory gets no grant there, revision 12); cannot read `/proc/<worker|kernel>/environ`; cannot TCP-connect to postgres, app:8090, the kernel or the sidecars in `strict`; cannot call kernel RPC without a token; cannot use agent A's kernel token for agent B's paths, which the two-agent kernel auth matrix covers for every row of L4's RPC table (§2.4.2), whose RPC list it reads from the proto. Also: the upgrade copies worker-state and decrypts every secret, and rollback to the previous images works; a `read_only` root on `runtime-kernel` passes the CLI smoke; the installer preflight records the mode; the Codex `IP` results are recorded **through `koda-sandbox-exec` with the Codex profile** and with no ruleset; the self-test's `lifetime` is recorded, and a `setsid` process that a Codex `workspace_exec` command starts is gone after the turn ends and after a worker restart, with runtime environments off and on; where the self-test reports `subreaper` and AG-15b runs (§6), a command that SIGKILLs its helper leaves a `setsid` process that is gone within 2 s, and so does a command that runs `kill -9 -1`, after which the kernel is still running and the agent's next worker starts. The `kill -9 -1` check runs with **two** agents, each mid-turn: it records that below ABI 6 the second agent's worker is also killed and its running task restarted (R-12), and that at ABI ≥ 6 (signal scoping) only the caller's own turn is affected. The Gemini load and per-key probes pass as `botuser`.

**Host matrix:** self-test results, `lifetime` and signal scoping included, on kernel 6.1 (expected `filesystem`, ABI 2), on a kernel from 6.8 to 6.11 (expected `strict`, ABI 4 or 5), on a kernel ≥ 6.12 (expected `strict` with signal scoping, ABI ≥ 6) and on Docker Desktop `[I]`. Compose is expected at `subreaper` on each; on the first two that makes AG-15b required where §6 says (OQ-8 (a) with OQ-9 (b) and (c) approved). A native host of the second kind that refuses unprivileged user namespaces (for example Ubuntu 24.04) is expected to report R-10 case (2) in health.

## 6. Release gate

The default ships only when all of these are done:
- every AG item in §7 that is not marked done, except AG-32, which by its own Deps ships one release later (revision 11: listing it made the gate impossible to meet), and M1-E4-T9 (AG-06 and AG-09). Each §9.1 question gates only the items or parts its row names: an approval keeps them in this gate, and a decline takes them out and keeps today's behaviour for them, as its row says. No item left in the gate depends on one that a decline takes out. Where an answer reaches beyond its own question's items, the table says what it does (revision 11), so the gate can be met under every combination of answers:

  | Answer | Effect on this gate |
  |---|---|
  | OQ-8 (a), with OQ-9 (b) and (c) approved | The lifetime parts of AG-14 to AG-16 stay. AG-15b stays where AG-08 records `subreaper` below ABI 6 on a deployment of the §5.4 matrix (compose is expected to), and is dropped elsewhere. |
  | OQ-8 (a), with OQ-9 (b) or (c) declined | The lifetime parts stay. AG-15b is dropped, because the scan would kill the legitimate background processes of an operator path that has no `--lifeline` helper (AG-15b); R-10 case (1) stays open in compose, as under OQ-8 (b). |
  | OQ-8 (b) | The lifetime parts stay; AG-15b is dropped; R-10 case (1) stays open in compose. |
  | OQ-8 (c) | The lifetime parts of AG-14 to AG-16 and AG-15b leave; R-10 case (5) applies on every host, and OQ-9 (c) has no effect. |
  | OQ-9 (a), (b) or (c) declined | (a): AG-03's hardening of `/git` and the kernel's git leaves; the kernel's git then runs as today, without the §2.9.1 flags or the config check (inside the helper from AG-15, R-11a), and L4's worktree floor records the common directory without that check; nothing left in the gate needs the check. (b): AG-15's confinement of operator commands leaves, and R-11 stays open. (c): their `--lifeline` form leaves, so the helper confines and execs them (AG-08). A decline of (b) or (c) also drops AG-15b (above). |
  | OQ-9 (b) declined, (c) approved | (c) has no effect (revision 12): it acts through the `--lifeline` helper that (b) adds, so with (b) declined the operator commands' background processes keep running after the command and an open terminal survives a worker restart, as today; A-O-01 (c) leaves with (b). |
  | OQ-10 declined | The registration path, an AG item owned with M5-E4-T3, joins this gate. |
  | OQ-11 (a) declined | AG-14's and AG-15's governed handling of model-authored shell jobs leaves; those jobs run on OQ-9's operator path (R-11). The `command_author` column stays, because it changes no behaviour. |
  | OQ-11 (b) declined | Nothing leaves this gate; existing shell jobs without `migration_source` are classified `model` at upgrade instead (OQ-11), and an operator makes one operator-authored again only by changing its command or work dir in the dashboard or re-creating it with `/cron` (§7.9). |
  | OQ-12 (a) or (b) declined | (a): AG-12's check of the work dirs the model chooses leaves, with its test (§7.10); a model-chosen work dir needs only to pass the predicate, as today (R-14). (b): the install-directory rule and its predicate rows leave; the install directory stays a valid work dir, as today (R-14). The rest of AG-12 stays. |

  While OQ-9 (b) or (c) is unanswered, AG-15b's scan stays off; the gate is not met until every question has an answer (the last item below);
- M1-E6-T1 (kernel RPC auth), M1-E6-T3 (signing key), M3-E3-T1 (D-011), M3-E3-T2 (D-012), M1-E4-T13 (one path resolver), M3-E5-T1 (replay);
- the M0-E3-T2 eval evidence;
- an operator answer, approve or decline, to every §9.1 question.

Each task may merge earlier if it leaves the system no less restrictive than before.

### 7.3 Foundation (no dependencies)

**AG-03 · Allowlisted env for every child process; hardened git** (M1-E2-T4)
- Files: `mcp_bootstrap.py:90`, `mcp_manager.py:128`, `mcp_client.py:156-162`; `mcp_isolation.py:329-330, 392-410`; `link_analyzer.py:128, 186`; `artifact_ingestion.py:1511, 1568`; `utils/audio.py:123, 170, 291`; `utils/video.py:37`; every git spawn (the `git_*` handlers at `tool_dispatcher.py:2870-3027`, through `_run_git` and `cli_runner.py`, worker-side git in `queue_manager.py` and `runtime/controller.py`; the operator `/git` command, `koda/handlers/commands.py:989-1011`; the kernel's own git in `rust/koda-runtime-kernel/src/workspace.rs` and `checkpoints.rs`); `provider_env.py:182-236`.
- Accept: no child gets `env=None` or an inherited env; HOME, `XDG_*` and `TMPDIR` are the child's own; MCP servers get their connection env plus the safe base, and Docker MCP env files live in a 0700 per-worker directory and are deleted once the container has started; every git spawn carries the §2.9.1 hardening (including `-c gc.autoDetach=false` and `-c maintenance.autoDetach=false`, on every git version) and refuses a repository config key outside the allowlist; `/git` sends that hardened argv as `ExecuteCommand`'s `argv`, never a shell string; the kernel's git uses a Rust builder whose flag and env list a test compares with the Python one, and a repository it refuses gets no runtime environment (§2.9.1); for the model's own git inside a class M spawn the env builder sets `gc.autoDetach=false` and `maintenance.autoDetach=false`, and no other key, through `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`, `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_n` and `GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_n` (§2.9.1); the CLI env sets `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_AUTO_MEMORY=1`.

**AG-07 · Audit of configuration changes**
- Files: `manager.py:12115, 8583, 8597, 8652, 12568`; the system-settings writer; `control_plane/audit.py` (`required=True` path); `koda/state/control_plane_store.py` and `koda/state/primary.py` (the transaction helper, `run_in_transaction`).
- Accept: every policy, model-policy and system-settings write records the actor and a before/after diff; governance events are transactional (§2.6): `run_in_transaction(fn)` runs an async callable on one pooled connection inside one transaction, the callable may read, lock, compute and write, the transaction commits when it returns and rolls back everything when it raises, and the `required=True` path writes through the caller's transaction.

**AG-08 · Confinement primitive, mode setting and doctor**
- Files: `rust/koda-runtime-kernel/src/confinement.rs`; `src/bin/koda-sandbox-exec.rs` (the spawn's root: the lifeline, the PID namespace with its init, mount namespace and procfs, the per-spawn cgroup nested in the workspace cgroup the helper starts in, under the `isolation.rs` root, the subreaper; without `--lifeline`, confine and exec, as revision 6 did); `Cargo.toml` (`landlock` crate, exact version); `config.py`; `.env.example`; `sandbox_doctor.py`; `docs/operations/sandbox-doctor-runbook.md`.
- Accept: `--self-test` prints the ABI, the enforced layers (filesystem, ptrace, TCP, scoping) and the lifetime mode (`pid_namespace`, `cgroup` or `subreaper`) as JSON; the profile follows §2.4.2 and DNS works; exit 126 when enforcement is incomplete; the helper stays as the spawn's root and ends every descendant when the command exits, on SIGTERM and when the lifeline closes (§2.3.5), each time sending SIGTERM to every process of the spawn, found as §2.3.5 says for its mode (init's `kill(-1)`, `cgroup.procs`, or the `/proc` children walk; revision 12), and waiting up to 2 s before its mode's kill (revision 11, §7.9); it sets `PR_SET_CHILD_SUBREAPER` and reaps in every mode, so an orphan of a live spawn stays with it until the spawn ends (revision 9); the status reports `lifetime: none` with the reason `no_helper` where there is no helper, and `lifetime_not_approved` while the helper runs without `--lifeline`; the helper is single-threaded, because init's `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` follows the thread that forked it; in `pid_namespace` init mounts a fresh procfs in a new mount namespace with `/` recursively private before the command applies Landlock, and the self-test selects the mode only if that mount works; in `cgroup` the spawn cgroup is a child of the helper's own workspace cgroup under `KODA_CGROUP_ROOT` (no per-install subtree, R-13), with no controller enabled below the workspace, and the mode is unavailable where the helper starts in any other cgroup; without `--lifeline` the helper confines and execs the command, which is the form AG-14 and AG-15 use until OQ-8 is approved; with no ruleset requested (`off`) it applies none and keeps the lifetime; `filesystem` and `off` emit an audit event and a banner (the `off` banner states R-8); a UNIX-socket kernel target is refused under `strict`; Docker's default seccomp behaviour, for Landlock and for the namespaces, and its cgroup mount are recorded.
- Tests: cargo unit tests; Linux integration (§5.1.9, the lifetime cases included) on an ABI ≥ 6 runner, with each lifetime mode forced in turn; `helper_spawn_end_lets_git_release_its_locks` (§7.9). G-RS. · Risk: high · Deps: —

governed writes under a `.git` component are refused; `AGENT_WORKSPACE_ROOT` defaults to `/var/lib/koda/runtime/workspaces` in compose and `~/koda-workspaces` (0700) on native installs, and the default work dir is `<AGENT_WORKSPACE_ROOT>/<agent>`; the native `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT` default is `~/.koda-runtime/<bid>` (0700) and the compose kernel root `/var/lib/koda/runtime/kernel`; the HOME fallback is gone. The kernel's Rust predicate (AG-15) is tested for parity with this Python one, row for row, except the model-chosen check of OQ-12 (a), which only the worker can make (the kernel cannot tell who chose a path).

**AG-13a · Per-spawn provider homes**
- Files: `provider_env.py` (the home builder, the copy rules, the spawn records and the sweep); `manager.py` (provider home paths); the runners' spawn paths (`claude_runner.py`, `codex_runner.py`, `gemini_runner.py`); Codex instructions into the spawn's `config.toml`; the Gemini user-settings writer; the Claude session store; the credential copy and its write-back checks.
- Accept: §2.3.5. Every CLI process gets a new, empty home under `provider-homes/<agent>/spawns/<incarnation>/<spawn id>/` (0700), removed after the process exits; HOME, `GEMINI_CLI_HOME`, `CODEX_HOME`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `XDG_*` point into it. Koda writes the Codex `config.toml` (developer instructions included) and the Gemini `settings.json` and `cwd/` with its stop file (0400, in a 0500 `.gemini/`, written before the cwd is set to 0500) into that home only. Only the named files cross, by the copy rules of §2.3.5 (no link followed, a regular single-link file within the size limit, an `O_EXCL` file renamed in the destination, a bare-UUID session id, the fixed project directory `projects/koda/`): Claude's resumed transcript in and its session's transcript out (the session store `provider-homes/<agent>/claude-sessions/`), and the credential files in and, under the write-back rule and checks of §2.4.4, back under the per-provider lock. Each spawn has a record in `spawn-records/<incarnation>/`, written before its helper starts or the kernel is asked to start it (AG-15), and a starting worker ends any recorded helper of an earlier incarnation that still runs (as §2.3.5's "Stopping a stuck helper" says: SIGCONT and SIGTERM, a 5 s wait, then in `subreaper` its children before it; `cgroup.kill` for a recorded cgroup whose path is the expected one), removes its home and record, and copies nothing out of it. Two concurrent spawns of one agent never see each other's files. Agent B cannot read agent A's sessions. Env credentials work.
- Tests: A-H-01; A-H-02 (its process assertions from AG-14's lifetime part, when spawns start through the helper); unit (the home starts empty and is removed after the run; only the named files cross; a file planted in one spawn's home is absent from the next); A-C-03; A-K-05 (4) and (6) on the spawn layout; G-PG. · Risk: med · Deps: — · Gate: OQ-3, before homes stop carrying customisation files (the split itself is not gated); OQ-4 for the per-spawn copy of file OAuth credentials (env credentials are not gated)

**AG-17b · Chat and runtime settings computed in `app`, unchanged** (M1-E2-T3)

**AG-15 · Kernel path: per-worker identity and a floor the kernel computes**
- Files: `rust/koda-runtime-kernel/src/server.rs` (`CreateEnvironment`, which takes the agent from the token; `StartTask`, `ExecuteCommand`, `OpenTerminal`, `TerminateTask`; and L4's binding on every RPC, revision 11's table), `state.rs` and `workspace.rs` (the worktree's own admin directory and `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` in the environment record; the kernel's own git and tar, and the copy of a `copy` environment, through the helper), `checkpoints.rs` (checkpoint git and tar through the helper), `processes.rs`, `commands.rs`, `agent_workers.rs` (the token and incarnation at spawn; the spawn set and its lock; the one spawn function, which this item owns; the end of an incarnation in `terminate_internal` and the reaper), `isolation.rs` (helpers started in the workspace cgroup under `KODA_CGROUP_ROOT`; the spawn cgroups killed at start, R-13); `proto/runtime/v1/runtime.proto` (required `ConfinementProfile`); `koda/internal_rpc/runtime_kernel.py`; `koda/services/runtime/kernel_subprocess.py`, `koda/services/shell_runner.py` and `koda/services/runtime/controller.py` (they send the worker's kernel token and, for a task with an environment, its id; the worker writes the record of a CLI it asks the kernel to start); the operator command handlers (`koda/handlers/commands.py`, `koda/handlers/packages.py`) and `koda/services/scheduled_job_runtime.py`, which state the basis (the latter also sends a `model`-authored job through the helper as a governed call; AG-14 owns its column and refusal).
- Accept (confinement): a request without a profile is rejected; the floor is §2.4.2 L4's: for a task with an environment, the workspace the kernel recorded for that task and the token's agent (and, for a `worktree`, the worktree's own admin directory and the repository's common directory from `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`, granted only when both lie under the base or the token agent's workspace root, else the environment is `copy` or refused, revision 10), otherwise a work dir that passes AG-12's predicate for the token's agent with no recorded environment, which refuses anything under `RUNTIME_KERNEL_ROOT`, every other agent's workspace root and every Koda directory under `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT` but the agent's own workspace (revision 11: no longer outside all of `RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT`, which holds the compose workspaces; revision 12: these are the predicate's own rules, so the worker side refuses them too); a broader caller profile is narrowed; `CreateEnvironment` takes the agent from the token and keys the record by that agent and the task id, a body `agent_id` is ignored, and every lookup resolves within the token's agent (§7.9; revision 12); every RPC has the binding of L4's table, never taking a `workspace_path` or `runtime_dir` from the body (revision 11); `StartTask`, `ExecuteCommand`, `OpenTerminal`, the kernel's own git and tar and the copy of a `copy` environment run through `koda-sandbox-exec`, started in the token agent's workspace cgroup, with the kernel's git getting the task class M profile plus the repository's common directory and, for a restore, that checkpoint's patch and bundle read-only, and the copy following no link (residual R-11a where the helper cannot confine them; §7.9); the kernel mints each worker's token and draws its incarnation when it spawns the worker, and revokes the token when the incarnation closes. The operator commands, operator-authored scheduled shell jobs and the operator terminal get the class M profile with their own work dir and a per-command tmp (§2.9.1; gated by OQ-9: until it is answered they run as today, R-11); with OQ-11 (a), a model-authored shell job runs governed through the helper (S9, A-O-01 (d)). Until the lifetime part lands, the helper confines and execs (AG-08).
- Accept (lifetime; gated by OQ-8): the kernel keeps each incarnation's spawn set under one lock, and one spawn function forks each helper and records its pid and lifeline under that lock, so a closed incarnation refuses a request before the fork and closure always sees a spawn in flight (revision 10, no separate pending-entry scheme); it holds each helper's lifeline, so the process and its descendants end with their task, with the requesting worker, or with the kernel (§2.3.5, "Spawns the kernel starts"): on `TerminateTask`, at the end or timeout of `ExecuteCommand`, at the close of a terminal, and at the end of the incarnation, in the order §2.3.5 gives (SIGTERM; the grace, during which the token stays valid and spawns work; once the worker is reaped, closure under the lock, revocation and the spawn set taken; the lifelines closed and their helpers waited for, a stuck one stopped as §2.3.5 says, an open operator terminal closed here; then the next worker); when a helper it started exits on a signal, the kernel writes that spawn's `cgroup.kill` in `cgroup` mode and reports the exit for `provider.spawn_helper_lost`; at start it kills every spawn cgroup under `<KODA_CGROUP_ROOT>/ws_*` (R-13); the kernel's own git, tar and copy hold a lifeline the kernel keeps outside the incarnation's spawn set, closed at their end, at their own timeout or at the kernel's exit, never at the end of the requesting incarnation (revision 11, §7.9).
- Tests: Rust request validation; `floor_grants_only_the_recorded_environment_workspace` (Rust: agent A's token gets neither agent B's environment worktree, nor a worktree or copy of another of A's tasks, nor a work dir under `RUNTIME_KERNEL_ROOT`, `WORKER_STATE_ROOT`, `STATE_ROOT_DIR` or a login home other than its task's recorded workspace, nor the kernel root itself; for a `worktree` base that is a repository subdirectory or a linked worktree, the granted git directory is the common directory, refused when it lies outside the base and the agent's workspace root; a body `agent_id` at `CreateEnvironment` that differs from the token's is ignored; must refuse, revision 12, as AG-12's matrix does: agent B's workspace under `AGENT_WORKSPACE_ROOT` in both layouts, `~/.koda-runtime/agent_b`, `<RUNTIME_EPHEMERAL_ROOT>/snapshots`, a runtime task directory and, with OQ-12 (b), the install directory; must pass, revision 11: the compose default workspace `/var/lib/koda/runtime/workspaces/<agent>`, a native workspace and a `/setdir` project under HOME, and the task's recorded worktree under `RUNTIME_KERNEL_ROOT` with its common directory); `kernel_auth_matrix_covers_every_rpc` (Rust: it reads every RPC of `RuntimeKernelService` from the proto descriptor and fails on one with no row in L4's table; per row, agent A's token is refused on agent B's task, session, checkpoint and browser session, a checkpoint of another of A's tasks and a body `runtime_dir` are refused, and the supervisor-only RPCs need the supervisor token); `environment_records_are_keyed_by_agent_and_task`, `kernel_bookkeeping_survives_a_worker_restart`, `kernel_checkpoint_runs_inside_the_helper_domain` and `copy_environment_never_follows_a_swapped_link` (§7.9); Linux integration under L2 with `RUNTIME_ENVIRONMENTS_ENABLED=true`: a fake CLI started through `StartTask` writes its own worktree and commits in it, and cannot read another task's worktree or another agent's; Python client test (confinement); A-O-01 (OQ-9); A-L-02, of which a `setsid` descendant of an `ExecuteCommand` process gone when the command ends is one case (lifetime). G-RS. · Risk: high · Deps: M1-E6-T1, AG-08; the lifetime part also AG-13a (the records); the model-authored jobs also AG-14 (their column) · Gate: OQ-8, for the lifetime part only; OQ-9, for the operator commands' confinement only; OQ-11 (a), for model-authored shell jobs only

**AG-15b · The kernel ends what a killed helper leaves** (required for the release if AG-08's self-test records `subreaper` with Landlock ABI < 6 on a deployment of the §5.4 matrix, which compose is expected to, OQ-8 is answered (a), and OQ-9 (b) and (c) are approved; dropped otherwise, §6)
- Files: `rust/koda-runtime-kernel/src/main.rs` (`PR_SET_CHILD_SUBREAPER` at start); a new `src/orphans.rs` (the scan; it adds the scan's lock and the pid record to AG-15's one spawn function in `agent_workers.rs`, which AG-15 owns); every site where the kernel starts a process: `agent_workers.rs:646-682` (workers), `server.rs:1122` (`StartTask`), `commands.rs:146` (`ExecuteCommand`), `processes.rs:81` (`prepare_process_command`), `server.rs:700` (the terminal's `fork`), `server.rs:2445-2522` (the browser session's Xvfb, openbox, x11vnc and websockify), `checkpoints.rs` (git and tar through `.output()` and `.status()`), `workspace.rs` (git, `git worktree add` included); system health.
- Accept: the kernel sets `PR_SET_CHILD_SUBREAPER` at start. It is already PID 1 in compose (C11) and the parent of every worker (`supervisor.py:5-6`), so every process orphaned in its subtree, the survivors of a killed helper included, is reparented to it; because every helper is itself a subreaper (AG-08), an orphan reaches the kernel only when its helper has died, and an orphan in the kernel's subtree is then exactly what a killed helper left — but only where every kernel spawn path runs under a `--lifeline` helper. While OQ-9 (b) or (c) is unanswered or declined, the operator commands, operator-authored jobs and the terminal still spawn without a `--lifeline` helper (`ExecuteCommand` forks `sh -lc` directly, `commands.rs:126-146`; the terminal forks directly, `server.rs:700`), so a scan would SIGKILL their legitimate `nohup` background processes. AG-15b therefore has one behaviour (revision 11): its scan is enabled only once AG-15 runs those paths under a `--lifeline` helper, which needs OQ-9 (b) and (c) approved, and the lifetime parts of AG-14 to AG-16 and AG-15 have landed; until then the scan is off, and if OQ-9 (b) or (c) is declined AG-15b is dropped (§6). Every process the kernel starts goes through AG-15's one spawn function, to which AG-15b adds the scan's lock, so the child (a raw `fork` included) is started and its pid recorded before that lock is released; the `std` `.output()` and `.status()` sites become spawn, record, then wait. Once a second the scan, under that lock, lists the processes whose parent is the kernel, and SIGKILLs each that it did not record, with the descendants the same scan finds, until none is left. It reaps each with `waitpid` on that pid only, never on a recorded pid, which tokio or `std` waits for, and logs `runtime.orphan_ended` with the pid and the command name. It also reaps orphans that exited by themselves, which nothing reaps today: the kernel's only `waitpid` waits on one known pid (`server.rs:817`), and tokio waits only on its own children. Under `lifetime` `none` the scan is off (R-10 case (4)). Health shows that it runs (§2.4.3, R-10).
- Tests: Linux integration with the kernel as a namespace's init and as a plain subreaper: in `subreaper` mode with signal scoping off, a command that SIGKILLs its helper and leaves a `setsid` grandchild that would write a canary after 5 s; the grandchild is gone within 2 s and no canary is written, the kernel's workers and tasks keep running, and tokio still collects their exit statuses; AG-15b is not enabled while OQ-9 (b) or (c) is unanswered or declined (health shows the scan off and why), and then a `/shell 'sleep 30 &'` survives the command; a checkpoint save, an environment worktree creation, an operator terminal and an `ExecuteCommand` each run longer than two scan intervals and complete normally, with their exit statuses; A-L-01's AG-15b case (a live spawn's orphan is left alone); the §5.4 check. G-RS. · Risk: med · Deps: AG-08, AG-14 and AG-16 (every worker-side spawn has a helper), AG-15 (its lifetime part, and its operator-command confinement in the `--lifeline` form, which needs OQ-9 (b) and (c) approved) · Gate: OQ-8 (dropped on (b) or (c)); OQ-9 (b) and (c) (off while either is unanswered, dropped if either is declined)

**AG-21b · Chat settings changes publish their own change** (M1-E2-T2)
- Files: `manager.py` (the settings route's `app` side: apply a closed patch as a draft write plus `publish_agent` with actor `telegram_user`; the runtime-settings view computed from the published snapshot); `agent_settings.py:426-445, 482-695` (session changes only on `published`; turn-time reads keep the published `AGENT_SPEC_JSON`); `commands.py:447, 510, 587, 2059, 2398, 2447`; `callbacks.py:262, 266-267, 288, 1403` (the settings-menu buttons); `chat_settings.py:196, 224, 256, 273`, reached from `messages.py:29` (the replies).

A queued task survives the restart within the recovery limit.

### 7.9 Implementation obligations (revision 11)

Round-10 findings whose decision belongs to implementation. Each is an Accept criterion of the owning item, with the named test.

| Finding | Owning item | Acceptance criterion | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
