## 8. Open questions (evidence or delegated)

| # | Question | Owner | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| O-4 | File OAuth with per-spawn homes: does each pinned CLI rotate its refresh token, and does rotation survive per-spawn copies (checkout and check-in) with concurrent spawns of one agent and with concurrent agents? With the revision 6 rule, a refresh made at a shell level is never written back (§2.4.4): on a CLI that rotates and revokes the old token, the provider then has to log in again. | AG-13a and AG-13b evidence, then operator | Evidence-gated; default preference: env credentials, which do not refresh, for agents at shell levels |
| O-7 | Pinned versions and their proven flag set. | AG-02 | Done for 2.1.325 / 0.169.0 / 0.68.0; the offline probes (A-K-01 to A-K-05) and the live checks of AG-23 to AG-25 complete the proof |
| O-8 | Does Docker's default seccomp allow `landlock_*`? Does Codex's inner sandbox start in the runtime image without L2 (`off`)? | AG-08, AG-24 | Open. Settled `[V]`: Codex 0.169.0's inner sandboxes cannot start inside any Landlock domain on any host (§1.2), so under L2 Codex has `none` and `workspace_exec` only; this is not a property of Docker. Uid split only on proof. |
| O-9 | Can a later Codex pin run its shell through a Koda-supplied wrapper, so that a nested, narrower `koda-sandbox-exec` (no TCP) confines `workspace_write` and `read_only` under L2? | AG-24 on each CLI bump | Open; nothing depends on it |
| O-10 | Host-level CLI configuration: does `CLAUDE_CODE_MANAGED_SETTINGS_PATH` pinned to an absent folder stop Claude from applying `/etc/claude-code` managed settings (MDM and server-managed settings cannot be pinned), and does any Codex 0.169.0 switch exclude `/etc/codex/config.toml`, `requirements.toml` and `managed_config.toml`? Gemini's equivalent is pinned and verified (§2.3.2). | AG-23, AG-24 (canaries in §5.3) | Open. L2 grants `/etc` read-only, so only an operator, or under `off` a Koda running as root (R-8), can place these files. If a pin is proven, the contract adds it. If not, health shows each file that is present, and the spawn event records its SHA-256 |
| O-11 | Which spawn-lifetime mode does each deployment get (§2.3.5)? Does Docker's default seccomp profile allow an unprivileged user and PID namespace in `runtime-kernel`, and a procfs mount inside them, and is its cgroup mount writable there; on native hosts, do AppArmor or sysctl settings refuse user namespaces, and is a cgroup subtree delegated? Which Landlock ABI does each host give (signal scoping needs ABI 6, kernel 6.12)? | AG-08 (self-test), §5.4 | Open. `[V]` on this design's host: all three modes work, the PID namespace with its own procfs and the cgroup nested in a workspace cgroup included (§1.2). Compose is expected to report `subreaper` on kernels below 6.12 (C11, `[I]`), where R-10 case (1) applies and AG-15b becomes required where §6 says (OQ-8 (a) with OQ-9 (b) and (c) approved); a native host that refuses user namespaces below 6.12 gets R-10 case (2); the status shows the mode and the case on every host |

## 9. Operator decisions

### 9.1 Questions for MISSION §13 sign-off

| # | Question | Why §13 | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| OQ-1 | **M3-E3-T1 go-live order.** Policy-approved governed shell, `shell_bg` and git start executing with M3-E3-T1. Should that go live only after AG-03 (no secrets in their env, hardened git), AG-14 (worker-side git and `shell_bg` confined), AG-15 (kernel `ExecuteCommand`, which runs governed shell, confined) and AG-19 (the key out of `runtime-kernel`)? Only the confinement parts of AG-14 and AG-15 are needed, and no question gates them. The alternative is to go live earlier with an accepted interim residual: until then governed shell runs unconfined in a container that mounts the master key. | Permission policy affecting users | **Require the four tasks first.** Otherwise invariants 3 and 4 are broken on the newly live path. Gates M3-E3-T1 (§9.3 item 1). The lifetime parts of AG-14 and AG-15 wait for OQ-8 and do not hold M3-E3-T1; but if OQ-8 is still open when M3-E3-T1 goes live, governed shell's and `shell_bg`'s descendants outlive the command and the worker restart, with the turn's grants (R-10 case (5)), so deciding OQ-8 with this question avoids that window. OQ-9, which decides how operator commands run on the same kernel path, is best decided with it too. |
| OQ-2 | **Chat settings changes publish, and provider switches need confirmation** (AG-21, AG-21b; §2.2.3). From AG-21, the chat toggles that publish today (voice, voice default, audio model) publish only their own change. Until AG-21b, a provider that `/provider` adds beyond the published ceiling is refused for the turn (S10). From AG-21b, the provider, model, feature-model and voice commands, and plain messages that ask for such a change (`messages.py:29` → `chat_settings.py`), publish their own change through the control plane. A change that would add capabilities (for example Codex on an agent at Workspace write) is refused in the chat and waits in the dashboard for an operator's confirmed publish. The settings-menu buttons (`callbacks.py`) are covered the same way, and a refused change leaves the conversation's provider and model as they were. Each published change restarts the agent's worker: a turn in progress is stopped after 15 s and re-queued, within the queue's recovery limit (3 restarts, then the DLQ); from M3-E3-T2 on, pending approvals expire. Model, provider and voice edits saved in the dashboard take effect on publish, no longer at the next chat settings refresh. An unsupervised `python -m koda --agent-id` start of a never-published agent fails instead of publishing it. | Externally exposed permission policy; changes the effect of Telegram commands and of plain messages | **Approve.** Without it, a chat command gives the model a shell, or a chat toggle publishes the operator's unconfirmed draft. Gates AG-21 and AG-21b, and through AG-21 also AG-11 (signed snapshots), AG-29 and AG-30 (the dashboard control and the upgrade banner), and so the release (§6). It does not gate AG-19 (M1-E2-T3), nor, through OQ-1, M3-E3-T1: the key removal needs only AG-17b, which moves the chat setters and the settings read into `app` without changing what they do (revision 7, R6-3), and AG-22a, which moves the squad reads to the published snapshot (revision 8, R7-9). |
| OQ-3 | **Provider CLIs ignore workspace, home and host customisation** (§2.3.5). (a) Executable customisation: hooks, MCP servers, plugins, extensions, skills, custom commands and agents, rules, discovery commands. This includes Gemini's host system settings (`/etc/gemini-cli`) and every `.gemini/.env` above its cwd, which on native installs means `~/.gemini/.env`; keys go in the dashboard's provider connection instead. (b) Instruction files: CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, GEMINI.md. Koda's own MCP servers and skills are unaffected. Gemini 0.68.0 cannot honour (b) at `read_only` and above: it loads the workspace-root GEMINI.md at start and adds the GEMINI.md files between a file it reads and the workspace root (R-7); at `none` it loads none, and at no level does it load one from above the workspace (§2.3.2). | Removes user-facing functionality | **(a) Approve**: needed for any level below `workspace_exec` to mean what it says, because a planted hook runs code at `read_only`, and a planted `.env` redirects Gemini's endpoints. **(b) Approve** for Claude and Codex, and for Gemini at `none`, with the operator-authored agent instructions as the replacement; accept R-7 for Gemini at `read_only` and above until a Gemini pin has a switch. Gates AG-13a (homes that carry no customisation from run to run) and AG-23 to AG-25. |
| OQ-4 | **CLI login credentials.** (a) The login homes move to an app-only path (`STATE_ROOT_DIR/provider-logins`), copied and verified, the old copies deleted one release later (AG-13b, AG-32). (b) Every provider run works on its own copy of the named credential files, and a refreshed copy is written back only when it is newer, under a per-provider lock (AG-13a, §2.4.4). (b) is what keeps concurrent runs of one agent from sharing a home (§2.3.5); a rotated refresh token that a run fails to write back would log the provider out. (c) A copy refreshed during a run at a shell level (`workspace_exec`, or Codex `workspace_write` under `off`) is never written back, because the model could have rewritten it with a later expiry or another account's token. On a CLI that rotates refresh tokens, that can log the provider out (O-4). | Touches production credentials; can log a provider out | **Approve (a)** together with AG-19, and **(b)** subject to O-4's evidence; if rotation does not survive per-spawn copies, file OAuth modes require env credentials under confinement. **Approve (c)**: the alternative lets one shell-level turn change the login of every later run of every agent on that provider; recommend env credentials (API keys, Claude's `setup-token`) for agents at shell levels. Gates AG-13a's file-OAuth copy, AG-13b and AG-32. |
| OQ-5 | **Retarget M1-E4-T7** (approval batch 1, fail closed on unsupported MCP egress) from the bubblewrap backend, which AG-16 deletes, to the helper's Landlock TCP rules. | Security policy; some MCP servers stop starting | **Approve** the retarget, so the approval is not spent on deleted code. Gates AG-16's deletion of the bubblewrap backend, and M1-E4-T7 (§9.3 item 5). |
| OQ-6 | **Codex under confinement.** On Codex 0.169.0 the inner sandboxes cannot start inside Landlock, and unconfined they read every file the uid can read. Choose: (i) Codex has no tools at Nenhuma and Somente leitura in every mode, and under `strict`/`filesystem` gets tools only at Execução no workspace (`danger-full-access` confined by L2); (ii) enforce Codex `read_only` with L2 alone, which gives its shell HTTPS egress to every host at the default level; (iii) keep Codex below `workspace_exec` unavailable until a pin proves a nesting sandbox. Today Codex runs `danger-full-access` everywhere. | Removes user-facing functionality (Codex reads at the default level); permission policy | **Approve (i)**: it is never weaker than today, keeps internal calls working on Codex-only installs (`none`, proven per configured model, §2.3.3), and becomes richer automatically if a pin's `IP` passes inside L2. Gates AG-24. |
| OQ-7 | **Delete the stored `CODEX_SANDBOX` and `CODEX_APPROVAL_POLICY` values** one release after AG-24 stops reading them (AG-32). | Destructive settings operation | **Approve**: they are listed in the release note, and keeping them for one release lets a rollback use the operator's choice. Gates AG-32. |
| OQ-8 | **Model-started processes end with their spawn** (§2.3.5, R-10). Every process that a provider CLI's shell, governed shell, `shell_bg`, git or a class U spawn starts ends when that spawn ends, whether the worker or the runtime kernel started the spawn, and so, with OQ-9, does every process an operator command starts. A background server an agent starts during a turn stops when the turn ends, and every `shell_bg` process stops when the agent's worker restarts, which every published chat change causes (OQ-2). Today such processes keep running with the grants of the turn that started them, after the turn, after a lowered level and after a restart, and nothing in Koda can find or stop them (C17). **The Docker deployment** is expected to offer only the `subreaper` mode (C11), on kernels below 6.12, where Landlock cannot stop a model process from killing its launcher; the processes that launcher held would then keep running. AG-15b closes that case: the runtime kernel ends such processes within a second. It is required for the release only if AG-08's self-test records that case, as expected. Three answers: **(a)** approve, with AG-15b (which also needs OQ-9 (b) and (c), below); **(b)** approve, and accept the compose residual instead of AG-15b: AG-15b is dropped, R-10 case (1) stays open in compose, and the notice's sentence on Docker says that such a process keeps running until it exits or the container restarts; **(c)** decline: the helper confines and execs the command as revision 6 did, R-10 case (5) applies on every host, and the lifetime parts of AG-14 to AG-16 and AG-15b leave the release gate (§6). **Decide OQ-8 and OQ-9 together:** AG-15b's scan can tell a killed helper's orphans from the legitimate `nohup` background process of an operator command, scheduled job or terminal only when every kernel spawn path runs under a `--lifeline` helper, which needs OQ-9 (b) and (c) approved. So AG-15b stays off while either is unanswered, and is dropped if either is declined; (a) then acts in compose as (b) does, and R-10 case (1) stays open there (§6, AG-15b, revision 11). | Permission policy affecting users; removes a behaviour users may rely on (background processes that outlive a turn); AG-15b ends processes in the default deployment | **Approve (a)**: otherwise invariant 1 does not hold for any process the model starts, a published lowering never reaches it, and every confinement grant outlives its turn; with (b) the default deployment would leave that open to any shell-level turn willing to kill one process. Gates only the lifetime parts of AG-14, AG-15 and AG-16, and AG-15b. Their confinement parts are not gated, so an undecided OQ-8 holds neither M3-E3-T1 (OQ-1 needs only those parts) nor AG-19b. Decide it together with OQ-9, so that AG-15b and the operator-command helper land together. |
| OQ-9 | **Operator commands and operator-authored scheduled shell jobs run as model-directed code** (§2.9.1, §2.4.1, R-11). `/shell`, `/git`, `/pip` and `/npm`, which an allowed Telegram user types, operator-authored scheduled shell jobs (via `/cron` or a migration), and the operator terminal of a runtime environment (`runtime/api.py:1749-1762`) run through the runtime kernel (`shell_runner.py:38-47`; `server.rs:1412`) in a work dir the model can write. (A shell job whose command or work dir the **model** wrote, through `job_create` or `job_update`, is OQ-11's; it is covered here only if OQ-11 (a) is declined, §2.9.1.) **(a)** From AG-03, `/git` and the kernel's own git and tar (worktree creation and removal, checkpoints) run hardened like every git process Koda spawns: the operator's global and system git configuration, repository hooks, an fsmonitor and external diff, textconv and filter drivers no longer run, auto-gc finishes inside the command instead of daemonizing, and a repository whose configuration has a key outside the allowlist (for example `lfs.*`, `submodule.*`, `pull.rebase` or `credential.helper`) is refused, naming the key; a runtime environment is then not created for it, and its task runs in its work dir. **(b)** From AG-15 they run under the class M profile the kernel computes: they write only their work dir and a per-command tmp, read no home directory, and connect only to `RUNTIME_CONFINEMENT_EGRESS_PORTS` (default 443 and 53). `pip install --user`, `npm install -g`, git over SSH (port 22 and `~/.ssh`), and anything that writes outside the work dir stop working there; a work dir under the kernel's root that is not the task's environment, or in another agent's workspace, is refused. Where the class M profile cannot be enforced (a host below kernel 5.13 outside `off`), they are refused, as governed shell is. **(c)** With OQ-8, a process they start in the background (`nohup … &`) ends when the command ends, and a terminal's processes when the terminal closes; and an open runtime-environment terminal closes when the agent's worker restarts, which every published chat change causes (§2.3.5). Until AG-15 they run as today (R-11). | Permission policy affecting users; changes what operators' commands can do today, and (c) closes a terminal on each agent restart | **Approve (a), (b) and (c).** Each of them can run code the model planted in its work dir (a Makefile target, an npm lifecycle script, a git fsmonitor or hook, `[V]` §1.2), so left unconfined they let a confined model escape through the operator's next command; and the kernel could exempt them only on a label the worker sets, which any code holding the worker's kernel token could set (§3). An operator who needs SSH, global packages or a shell outside the work dir uses a shell on the host; an operator who needs a terminal to survive an agent restart runs it on the host. Decide it with OQ-1 and OQ-8. Gates AG-03's hardening of `/git` and the kernel's git, and AG-15's confinement of these commands; an undecided OQ-9 holds neither M3-E3-T1 nor the rest of AG-03 or AG-15, and until it is answered these commands run as today (R-11). Each part can be answered on its own: declining (a) leaves `/git` a shell string and the kernel's git unhardened; declining (b) leaves the operator path unconfined, so R-11 stays open and the kernel keeps an unconfined path for requests the worker marks `operator_command` or an operator-authored `scheduled_job` (a model-written job stays governed either way, with OQ-11 (a)); declining (c) leaves their background processes running after the command and the terminal open across a restart; and (c) has no effect if (b) is declined, because it acts through the `--lifeline` helper that (b) adds (§6, revision 12). Declining (b) or (c) also drops AG-15b, so with OQ-8 (a) R-10 case (1) stays open in compose (OQ-8, §6). |
| OQ-10 | **The unsupervised start has no kernel or broker token** (R-9). A worker started with `python -m koda --agent-id` is spawned by neither the kernel nor the supervisor, so from AG-15 it holds no per-worker kernel token, and from AG-17 no broker token; it draws its own incarnation, as every worker does before AG-15. In that mode every call that needs a token is then refused with `unsupervised_start_no_token:<route>`: from AG-15 the kernel's `StartTask`, `ExecuteCommand` and `OpenTerminal` (governed shell, the operator commands, scheduled shell jobs, runtime environments); from AG-17 the broker routes (MCP connection env and OAuth, the squad peer tokens and runtime access, ElevenLabs, image generation), and from AG-13b, AG-17b and M3-E3-T2 the credential checkout, the chat settings and runtime settings, and approvals. Chat with a provider that needs none of these keeps working. | Removes functionality from the direct start, which Q-19 keeps working | **Approve.** Every broker route needs `app`, and where `app` runs, its supervisor starts the worker with both tokens, so the supported start keeps every feature; the alternative, a registration path for the direct start (it would ask a running `app` for a broker token and a kernel incarnation, bound to a stream it holds open), would duplicate the supervised start for a development mode that is outside the boundary (R-9, §3). Q-19's adopted default holds: the direct start still works, as R-9 describes. Gates only the release (§6): the supervised start is unchanged, so AG-15 and AG-17 may merge before it is answered. If declined, the registration path becomes an AG item that the release waits for, owned with M5-E4-T3. |
| OQ-11 | **Scheduled shell jobs the model wrote run as the agent's governed commands** (§2.9.1, S9, R-11; revision 11). Today an agent whose tool policy enables the scheduler tools can create a `shell_command` job (`job_create`, `tool_dispatcher.py:1199-1222`) and rewrite the command and work dir of any of its user's jobs (`job_update`, `:1073-1101`; `scheduled_jobs.py:1473, 1506-1507`). Either activates after validation with no operator step (`tool_dispatcher.py:1124-1127`; an active job reactivates, `scheduled_jobs.py:1583-1586`) and then runs on the `scheduled_job` basis, unconfined until AG-15, behind a read-only check that a command running a workspace helper passes (`[V]`, §1.2). **(a)** From AG-14, a shell job whose command or work dir the model last wrote is **model-authored** and runs as a governed class M call: S9 refuses it where the class M layers are missing (on every host until AG-15, because a kernel spawn counts as missing them), and from AG-15 it runs confined under the agent's class M profile. At fire time no human is present, so it runs only on `policy_allow`: the secure default (which denies `shell_execute`, C5), a `require_approval` rule and an `allow_with_preview` rule each refuse the run, which is recorded as blocked and reported in the chat. An operator's edit that changes the command or work dir in the dashboard (`runtime/api.py:382-391`) makes the job operator-authored again, and so does re-creating it with `/cron`; saving it unchanged writes nothing (`scheduled_jobs.py:1559-1566`) and keeps its class (revision 12); a schedule change and the model's `job_run_now` (`tool_dispatcher.py:1311-1323`) keep its class, because neither chooses what runs. **(b)** No creator is recorded today (`job.created` is always `actor_type="system"`, `scheduled_jobs.py:917-921`; no such column, `postgres_backend.py:1626-1652`), and `/cron`'s `safety_mode="restricted_wrapper"` is a value the model can pass too (`tool_dispatcher.py:1122`; `cron_store.py:70`). At upgrade, an existing shell job is model-authored if the latest `job.updated` event that changed its command or work dir came from `source="agent_tool"`, which the tool handler sets itself (`tool_dispatcher.py:1092`), and operator-authored otherwise; `koda policy audit` lists every existing shell job with its class. | Permission policy affecting users; changes what an agent's scheduled commands can do today | **Approve (a) and (b).** Without (a), a confined model can choose code that then runs on the unconfined operator path: before AG-15 on every host, and for good if OQ-9 (b) is declined. User impact of (a): a scheduled shell command the agent wrote, which runs on its own today, stops between AG-14 and AG-15, and from AG-15 runs only where the agent's published policy allows shell without approval, which the secure default does not; the same holds for an operator's job once the agent rewrites its command or work dir, and the chat says so when a run is refused. (b) keeps every existing `/cron` job running as today; a job the model created before the upgrade and never edited counts as the operator's, which leaves it R-11's exposure until AG-15 with OQ-9 (b) approved, and for good if (b) is declined, no wider than an operator's own job. The declined branch of (b) classifies every existing shell job without `migration_source` as model-authored, which stops existing `/cron` jobs in the same way until the operator changes each one's command or work dir in the dashboard or re-creates it with `/cron`; re-saving a job unchanged does not do it. If (a) is declined, model-written jobs stay on OQ-9's operator path (R-11). Gates AG-14's and AG-15's governed handling of model-authored jobs (A-O-01 (d)); the `command_author` column is recorded either way (§7.9). Decide it with OQ-9. |
| OQ-12 | **Work dirs the model chooses, and the Koda install directory** (§2.4.2 L2, AG-12, R-14; revision 12). AG-12's predicate decides which directory a spawn may be granted, whoever chose it. Where Koda owns the parent it allows only the agent's own directories, which removes nothing users have today (§2.4.2 L2). Elsewhere it refuses only shared and Koda roots, and today the model chooses its work dir itself: `agent_set_workdir` limits it only when the context has a runtime workspace or a control-plane workspace root (`tool_dispatcher.py:1428-1437`), `job_create` and `job_update` take any `work_dir` (`:1116`; `scheduled_jobs.py:1473-1477`), and `validate_work_dir` refuses only system directories (`utils/workdir.py`; `config.py:766-780`). So on a native install the model can make `~/.ssh` or `~/.aws` its read grant, or `~/.config/systemd/user` or `~/.local/bin` its write grant, where planted code runs unconfined later, and the Koda checkout, whose `.env` Koda loads into every worker (`config.py:14-15`), is a valid work dir for the model and the operator alike (`[V]`, §1.2). **(a)** A work dir the model sets (`agent_set_workdir`, `job_create`, `job_update`) must lie inside the agent's own `<AGENT_WORKSPACE_ROOT>/<agent>`, a `PROJECT_DIRS` entry, the agent's control-plane workspace root (`manager.py:2130-2149`), the task's recorded runtime environment, in a scheduled run the job's saved work dir, or the directory the operator last set with `/setdir`, each at or under it; anything else is refused, naming those roots. A work dir the operator chooses (`/setdir`, `/cron`, the dashboard) keeps the predicate alone, and a job keeps the work dir it was saved with. **(b)** The install directory (`SCRIPT_DIR`: on native installs the checkout, `docs/install/local.md`; in Docker `/app` in `runtime-kernel`, which `botuser` owns and every worker imports) is refused as any work dir, as the state roots are, and so is any directory that contains it. | Permission policy affecting users; removes what agents, and with (b) operators, can do today | **Approve (a) and (b).** Without (a), a prompt-injected model reads the operator's keys or plants code outside every grant Koda gives it, by moving its own work dir; without (b), a work dir in the checkout gives it the worker's secrets and Koda's own code (invariants 3 and 5). In Docker, declining both (a) and (b) lets a confined model set its work dir to `/app`, rewrite Koda's code and have the next worker start run it unconfined, in the default deployment. User impact of (a): an agent can no longer move its own work dir, or point a scheduled job it creates or edits, to a directory under the home directory outside those roots; the operator sets such a directory with `/setdir` or adds it to `PROJECT_DIRS`, and existing jobs keep their work dir. User impact of (b): no agent can work in the Koda checkout itself, nor in a directory that contains it (a `/setdir`, `PROJECT_DIRS` entry or job work dir that holds the checkout is refused too); developing Koda with a Koda agent needs a second clone. In Docker, (b) changes nothing users do. A declined part keeps today's behaviour for it and leaves R-14 open, and the notice then drops its sentence. Gates only AG-12's check of model-chosen work dirs (a) and its install-directory rule (b) (§6); the rest of AG-12 is not gated. |
| — | AG-10b activates delivered rules | Permission policy | Needs sign-off only if AG-09's diff section is non-empty (ADR-039). |
| — | M1-E2-T5's extended scope: the worker role also gets no write on the approval tables, `cp_agent_config_versions` or `cp_agent_definitions` (§7.1) | Production credentials (already M1-E2-T5's gate) | No new question. Include the extension in M1-E2-T5's existing approval package. |
| — | The unsupervised `python -m koda --agent-id` start stays, as a development mode outside the boundary (R-9) | Would remove functionality if changed | No new question for the start itself: this keeps Q-19's adopted default ("keep it working"). Making the direct start fail, or require `app`, would remove that functionality and needs §13 sign-off; this revision does not propose it. After AG-19 it works only where the key file is readable (native installs, development containers), as a consequence of the approved O-1 layout. What it loses from AG-15 and AG-17, for lack of tokens, is OQ-10. |

### 9.2 Recorded resolutions (revision 1, 2026-11-14)

The operator delegated these to the recommended long-term option ("siga com aquilo que você achar ser o mais recomendado … solução long-term ideal"). Revisions 2 to 9 do not change them.

| # | Resolution |
|---|---|
| O-1 | Approved: `koda-state` becomes app-only; worker data is copied (not moved) to `koda-worker-state` with verification; the key is not touched. |
| O-2 | Approved: `filesystem` is a documented installer choice on hosts with Landlock ABI < 4; minimum host kernel 5.13. |
| O-3 | Approved: the runtime evaluator becomes authoritative after the M5-E3-T2 diff report (Q-5). |
| O-4 | Evidence-gated (§8). |
| O-5 | Approved: MCP tool-policy edits take effect on publish. |
| O-6 | Approved as part of the Q-4 impact; documented in the release notes. |
| O-7 | Delegated to AG-02. |
| O-8 | Delegated to AG-08 (and AG-24 for Codex's inner sandbox outside L2). |

### 9.3 Plan follow-ups outside M1-E2 (for the orchestrator)

This revision edits only the M1-E2 block of MASTER_PLAN and the ADR-002 entry of DECISIONS. These items elsewhere need the orchestrator:

1. **M3-E3-T1:** deps per OQ-1 (AG-03, the confinement parts of AG-14 and AG-15, AG-19); the operator commands' class and windows are OQ-9's (§2.9.1), which M3-E3-T1's approval package should carry beside OQ-1; with OQ-11 (a), a scheduled shell job whose command or work dir the model wrote is a governed agent call, not an operator command (§2.9.1), so M3-E3-T1 routes it through the governed class M path on `policy_allow` only, not the `scheduled_job` basis; approval package = `koda policy audit` section 3 (AG-09); Files add `koda/handlers/commands.py`, `koda/handlers/packages.py`, `koda/services/cli_runner.py`, `koda/services/shell_tools.py`, `koda/services/scheduled_job_runtime.py`, `koda/services/scheduled_jobs.py`; Do follows §2.9.1 (the "set the approval context" option is rejected); Tests add D11-13 and D11-14.
2. **M3-E3-T2:** Files add `koda/control_plane/approvals.py` and the internal routes; deps add AG-17 (its routes and token), M1-E6-T3 (the MAC key) and AG-07 (the interactive transaction helper, `run_in_transaction`, that consume and resolution need, §2.6); its done-when states that the MAC resists a worker only from AG-19 on (R-4); Tests add D12-13 and D12-14.
3. **M2-E1-T2:** do not import `approval_grants.json` as live grants (§2.9.2); pending operations are not imported.
4. **M0-E2-T4:** the supervised-write xfail models a human grant, not a policy allow (§2.9.1).
5. **M1-E4-T7:** per OQ-5.
6. **M3-E5-T1:** Files add `codex_runner.py` (Codex has `supports_native_resume=False`).
7. **MASTER_PLAN §5.2** row for M1-E2-T3 ("copy, verify, then move" the key) and the **§7.2** governance chain (R-01): reword to the AG mapping of §7.1; the key is not moved.
8. **M1-E6-T1 / ADR-035:** under `RUNTIME_CONFINEMENT=strict` the kernel listens on TCP, not a UNIX socket (§2.4.2). It still depends on M1-E2-T4, which no longer waits for M1-E4-T13.
9. **M1-E9-T1:** its Deps (M1-E2-T2, M1-E2-T4) now cover more AG items; it may track AG-31 instead.
10. **ADR-009 consequence** "the JSON fallbacks are retired after a one-time import": the import must skip grants (item 3); this is an implementation note, not a change to ADR-009's decision.
11. **M1-E4-T9** (MASTER_PLAN "Deps AG-09"): turn the dependency into a close condition (it starts with AG-06 and closes when AG-06 and AG-09 are done), and **ADR-039** (DECISIONS): say AG-10b where it says AG-10.
12. **REMAINING_RISKS R-01:** "M1-E2-T4 (environment and cwd allowlist)" becomes "M1-E2-T4 (environment allowlist); the work-dir invariant is AG-12 under M1-E2-T2"; add R-8 (`off` mode reads) under R-01's residuals.
13. **Q-19 / D-19** (direct `python -m koda --agent-id` start): from AG-21, an unsupervised start of a never-published agent fails with `agent_not_published` instead of publishing from the worker (§2.2.3). A published agent still starts, as the single-process development mode R-9: it builds its snapshot in-process, so it decrypts provider keys, and no MAC is verified. Today every worker imports `koda/control_plane/bootstrap.py`; from AG-17 only this mode does, and §5.1.4 (a) checks it. After AG-19 the mode works only where the key file is readable. From AG-15 and AG-17 it has no kernel or broker token, so the calls that need one are refused in it (OQ-10). Q-19's adopted default holds; M5-E4-T3 should record that the mode is outside the boundary and, per OQ-10's answer, what it lacks.
14. **ADR-035 and M1-E6-T1, the kernel token's holders** (revision 11): from AG-15 a worker holds only the per-worker kernel token the kernel mints, and the shared M1-E6-T1 token is the supervisor's alone (§2.4.2 L4). ADR-035's "Holders" (DECISIONS: "the supervisor (`app`) and the agent worker processes") and M1-E6-T1's token-holder lines (MASTER_PLAN §5.3's M1-E6-T1 row, "held by supervisor and workers", and the task's "Token holders" bullet) should say so.
